# psp5 authentic – own launcher for PSP with ARK-4/ARK-5 – developer notes

**psp5 authentic is a fork of [psp5](https://github.com/08Weber/psp5), originally made by Mille Weber (08Weber) and forked by Xioonae, based on psp5 v1.5.3.** The
goal of the fork is to be as authentic as possible to its home console counterpart. These are the developer notes: what each
change touches in the code. The list for users is in [`CHANGELOG.md`](../CHANGELOG.md). The older sections at the bottom
(1.5.2 and earlier) are the original project's own history; they were written in Swedish and have been translated.

A dark launcher with a row of game cards, the game's picture as the background (the Library has a drawn background of its own), and tabs for games (Home) and music; the library is a card of Home.
All graphics are its own. The launcher is a normal app in `ms0:/PSP/GAME/PSP5` and never touches flash0, so the XMB is
always still there (HOME in psp5 = back to the XMB).

## psp5 as ARK's launcher
psp5 replaces ARK-5's own launcher (arkMenu): the file `ms0:/PSP/SAVEDATA/ARK_01234/VBOOT.PBP` is psp5.
ARK's "Custom Launcher" in the XMB then starts psp5, and with **Settings → psp5 → Boot into psp5** the PSP starts straight
into psp5 instead of the XMB.

- **Emergency exit:** hold **SELECT** while the PSP starts – ARK then skips SETTINGS.TXT and you land in the XMB.
  Hold **START** to skip all plugins.
- **Exit to XMB** (the power menu, SELECT) turns the launcher mode off for that session.
- **Back to arkMenu:** copy `ARK_01234/VBOOT_ARKMENU.PBP` to `VBOOT.PBP` (a copy is also in `backup/`).
- An ARK update can overwrite `VBOOT.PBP` – then copy `dist/PSP/GAME/PSP5/EBOOT.PBP` in as `VBOOT.PBP` again.

## Unreleased (changes after 1.2.0)

The changes after 1.2.0 are noted here; the list for users is under *Unreleased* in `CHANGELOG.md`. When the next version is released, this section becomes *Version x.y.z* and a new *Unreleased* one is put above it.

- **The power popup (SELECT) on every screen of the launcher** (`ui.c` `ui_draw()`, `ui_update()`, `videoplay_hold()`; on request: "the shut down menu isnt showing in the Media tab and it's cards, make sure to implement it in menus where it should appear and where it's missing"): `ui_update()` has always opened it on every tab (`open_power_menu()`), but `ui_draw()` called `draw_menu()` only for `TAB_GAMES` and `TAB_LIBRARY`, so on Media and on the Music, Videos and Images screens (`TAB_MEDIA`, `TAB_MUSIC`) it was open and not drawn. `draw_menu()` is called for every tab now, and also in the drawing of the image viewer and the video player. The hook that gives the open viewer or player the buttons moved below the SELECT handling: while the popup is open (or opens in that frame) they get nothing, and `videoplay_hold()` pauses the engine (`apply_pause()`: the user's pause, the question, a popup, sleep) until the popup has gone. The other screens (Settings, Information, Saved Data, the File Manager, the FTP screen, USB Mode) already ask `power_step()` in `main.c`. Tests (private, `ui/menu_test.c`): the per tab loop now draws the popup and looks for its panel (a negative control with the old condition fails 6 checks: tabs 1 and 3, Videos, Images and both viewers), and the Videos and Images screens and both viewers are tested with SELECT.

- **Videos and Images: two cards, two list screens, the image viewer and the video player** (`ui.c`/`ui.h` `HOME_MEDIA_VIDEO` (-5), `HOME_MEDIA_IMAGES` (-6), `HOME_IS_MEDIA()`, `ui->vu`, `ui->iu`, `ui->mscreen`, `ui->iv`, `ui->vp`; `media.c/h`; `musicui.c` `kind`; `icons.c` `ICON_VIDEO`, `ICON_IMAGES`; `gfx.c` `gfx_video_icon()`, `gfx_images_icon()`; `image.c` `img_load_fit()`; `imageview.c/h`; `mp4.c/h`; `mpegps.c/h`; `video.c/h`; `videoplay.c/h`; `videomem.c/h`; `lang.c` ten new texts, all 11 languages; on request: two more cards in Media, Videos (`ms0:/VIDEO`, `ef0:/VIDEO`) and Images (`PICTURE` and `PSP/PHOTO`), lists that work like Music's with the whole width, a full screen video player (MP4 and PMF, a bar, L / R, where it was left) and a full screen image viewer (L / R); the overlay of both goes away after a moment).
  - *The screens:* all three Media screens are the tab `TAB_MUSIC`; `ui->mscreen` says which (0 Music, 1 Videos, 2 Images), `screen_mu()` gives its list, `tab_name()` its name in the top bar. The list is `MusicUi` with a `kind` (`MK_MUSIC`, `MK_VIDEO`, `MK_IMAGES`, `media.h`): `media_roots()` and `media_list_dir()` (a filter in front of `music_list_dir_filter()`) tell which folders and files, `list_w()` is 428 instead of 266 and there is no player for the other two, ✕ on a file returns `MUSICUI_OPEN` and `musicui_file()` hands the lines of the folder to `open_viewer()`. Each kind has a `MusicUi` of its own, so the Music screen is left as it was. The up to four roots of Images are told apart as "Memory Stick · PICTURE". `ui_update()` hands all the buttons to the open viewer or player and `ui_draw()` draws only it (and the volume bar, the sound button's popup); `note_popup()` is not given the buttons over a video (`ui_video_open()`).
  - *The icons:* `sd_video()` and `sd_images()` in `icons.c` are signed distance shapes measured from "Video Icon.png" (a wide rounded rectangle, six small rounded holes and a square one) and "Image Icon.png" (a frame, a mountain line of four points with round joints, the corner cut away along a slanted line and a turned-up piece of paper); `gfx.c` makes each once into a texture like the note (`card_icon()`).
  - *Pictures:* `img_load_fit(path, max_w, max_h)` reads a file: JPEG with `jpeg_stdio_src`, the scale (1/2, 1/4, 1/8) as large as still gives the size that is shown, the EXIF orientation (`jpeg_save_markers` on APP1, `exif_orientation()`, `orient_image()`), PNG through `png_image_begin_read_from_stdio` (at most 2.2 million pixels), BMP by hand a row at a time (blocks of step x step pixels averaged as they are read, bottom-up and top-down, palettes of 1, 4 and 8 bits, 16 (5-5-5), 24 and 32 bits). `img_load_fit_cancel()` is the same with a `cancel` callback that is asked every 16 rows of a JPEG and every 8 of a BMP (it `longjmp`s out through the error handler; a PNG cannot be stopped); `setvbuf(fp, NULL, _IOFBF, 65536)` for JPEG and PNG (the file is opened twice: once to see what it is, since `setvbuf` must come before the first read; a BMP keeps the default buffer: its rows are read bottom-up by `fseek`, and each jump would refill the whole 64 KB), `JDCT_IFAST`, `do_fancy_upsampling = FALSE`, `do_block_smoothing = FALSE`. `imageview.c` has a thread (priority 0x28, below the main thread's 0x20) that reads `want` (the index to make ready: the shown picture first, then the one after it, then the one before it, whichever of them is not in the cache; PNGs are only wanted when shown) and gives the result through one slot that it does not touch again before the main thread has emptied it; the main thread makes the texture and keeps it in a cache of four (`Slot cache[CACHE]`: the shown one, `old` while it fades, the neighbours; the farthest one, by distance round the folder, is dropped; a file that could not be read is kept as an entry without a texture so that it is not tried again). `plan()` is called before the slot is emptied, so the thread never starts the one it has just finished. `sys_fast(1/0)` (`system.c`, counted) raises the clock to 333/166 MHz while a viewer or the player is open and gives back the old one.
  - *The video engine* (`video.c`): both kinds go through `sceMpeg` for the picture. A **PMF** is read as it is (`sceMpegQueryStreamOffset` and the header's entry points for a jump: the last entry at or before the time, else from the start; what comes before the time is decoded and not shown, and the ATRAC units before it are taken out and not played). An **MP4** (`mp4.c`: the movie box is read whole and its tables are used where they lie, `sample_offset()` walks the runs of `stsc`, `sample_dts()` and `sample_cts_offset()` keep their place for sequential reading, `mp4_video_at()` is the sync sample at or before a time) is turned by `mpegps_annexb()` (an access unit delimiter, the parameter sets on a key frame, start codes for lengths) and cut by `PsWriter` into 2048 byte sectors (a pack header, one PES packet with PTS and DTS in the first sector of a unit and none in the others, a padding packet or stuffing to fill the last one); the ring buffer's callback asks for the next unit when it is empty. A PSMF header is made for it (copied from a real PMF, with the length, the last time stamp and the size put in; the first time stamp is 90000, as the library expects). `RING(v)` is a 96 byte area for the ring buffer's structure: the SDK declares 44 bytes and the library writes up to 52 (it wrote into the fields after it and made the heap fail in the second session until that was found).
    The sound: AAC through `sceAudiocodec` (codec 0x1003; ADTS header on each frame; the rate in word 10, the input in 6 and 7, the output in 8 and 9, as PPSSPP's own implementation reads them), a PMF's ATRAC through `sceMpegAtracDecode`; both go into a queue of 32768 stereo frames that the thread `psp5_vsound` plays by `sceAudioSRCOutputBlocking` (blocks of 1024 frames; the music's sound channel is waited for). The frames played are the clock (`a_base` + played / rate); with no sound, or when it has all been played, `dt` is.
    `video_update()` (once a frame): fills the ring (three times), keeps half a second of sound in the queue, and decodes at most four units (a picture is shown when `sceMpegAvcDecode` says it has one and its time is not before the time of a jump). The ring buffer, the memory of the library, the picture buffer (always 512 x 512, 5650: opaque, half the memory, and `gfx_tex()` has a path for it; it was 512 x the height rounded up to 16, which only holds if the PSP's library never writes past the picture) and the sample buffers (the size of the biggest sample of the file) are made once per video; only the `sceMpeg` session is made again for a jump. The heap is about 8.7 MB, so all of this is kept to about 1.5 MB.
    *Real PSP results 7 (H1, H2, H3 and the own video, with the build that writes the PSP's timing in; the files `video-failed.txt`):* all four: `first-unit ... 6935 / 7611 / 7608 / 19211`, `first-decode 80628001` (not the fatal 80628002), `decode-error 80628001` for every unit, closed cleanly (31 errors -> `failed`), `timing 1 0 4004 240000` for H1 and the own video (the PSP's timing written in), `timing 0 0 0 0` for H2 and H3 (x264's own `nal-hrd` / `pic-struct`: left as they are). All four played in the XMB. So the timing is not it. Summary of everything the PSP's sceMpeg decoder has been given: T1 (PMF) and T2 (MP4 of its stream), **Main (77), CABAC, with the PSP's SEI and HRD**: decoded, played to the end and closed. T3 (that without SEI, HRD in the SPS): froze at the 4th unit. V1 (x264 Main CABAC, no timing): first picture decoded, froze at the 4th unit. V2 (x264 Baseline, no timing): fatal 80628002 at the first picture. H1 / H2 / H3 / own video (**Baseline, CAVLC**, with or without timing): 80628001 on the first picture. Pattern: Baseline fails, Main decodes. **Relabel:** `h264_sps_rewrite()` with `tick.to_main` and `h264_sps_relabel()` (only the three bytes, for a stream with HRD already) change `profile_idc` 66 to 77, the constraint byte to 0x40 and the level to at least 21; `h264_pps_info()` finds slice groups (FMO) and the entropy mode in the PPS (`unit_init()` relabels only when there are no slice groups; `UnitMaker.to_main`; `PsSynth.timing` tells the converter whether to write the timing or only to relabel). The entropy mode is untouched: **CAVLC under profile 77 is untested** (the PSP's own streams are CABAC). Test files M1 (x264 Main CABAC, no B-frames) and M2 (Main with CAVLC and B-frames, `cabac=0:bf=2`; its SPS is 77 as it needs B-frames), 480x272 with an AAC track; both pass `check_ps()` and decode like their sources. Readings: own video / H1 plays -> relabel was enough; M1 plays and H1 / M2 fail -> CAVLC is the problem (an entropy transcoder would be needed, or `sceVideocodec`); M1 fails too -> something else in a plain x264 Main stream.
    *The PSP's timing for streams that have none (`h264sei.c`, `unit.c`, `mpegps_annexb()` with a `PsSynth`):* decoded from the PMF of PPSSPP's tests (`/tmp/.../sei` scripts in the private tests): its SPS is Main level 2.1, `log2_max_frame_num` 6, POC type 2, one reference frame, 9 x 5 macroblocks, VUI: aspect idc 1, overscan flag 1/0, video format 5, full range 1, no colour description, timing (4004, 240000, fixed 1), NAL HRD **and** VCL HRD (the same: one CPB, bit_rate_scale 4, cpb_size_scale 6, bit_rate_value_minus1 624, cpb_size_value_minus1 624, not CBR, `initial_cpb_removal_delay_length` 18, `cpb_removal_delay_length` 7, `dpb_output_delay_length` 7, `time_offset_length` 24), low_delay 0, `pic_struct_present_flag` 1, no bitstream restriction. SEI: buffering period (payload type 0, 10 bytes: `sps_id` ue 0, initial_cpb_removal_delay 90000 and offset 0 for the NAL HRD and again for the VCL HRD) at each key frame (but one has other numbers: rate control); picture timing (type 1, 8 bytes) in every picture: `cpb_removal_delay` 7 bits (0 at the key frame, +2 a picture = fields), `dpb_output_delay` 7 bits (2), `pic_struct` 4 bits (0), clock timestamp flag 1, `ct_type` 0, `nuit_field_based` 1, `counting_type` 3, `full_timestamp` 0, discontinuity 1, `cnt_dropped` 0, `n_frames` 0, `seconds_flag` 0, `time_offset` 24 zero bits, then the payload's one bit; recovery point (type 6, `c4`) in the key frame's unit. A key frame's unit: AUD, SPS, PPS, SEI x3 (3-byte start codes), IDR; any other: AUD, SEI, slice. `h264_sps_rewrite()` copies the SPS up to `vui_parameters_present_flag` (`sps_vui_pos()` parses it, scaling lists of the High profiles too), keeps the old VUI's aspect ratio, format, range and colour description, writes the rest as above; `h264_sps_has_hrd()` decides (a stream with HRD is not touched). `unit_init()` finds the first SPS (the header's or the first sample's), sets `synth`, the tick (`fps = samples / duration`), and rewrites the SPS in `params`; `unit_make()` gives the unit and for `synth` the delays: `cpb = round((dts - dts of the key frame it belongs to (mp4_video_at)) / tick)`, `dpb = round((pts - (dts - 3003 ticks)) / tick)` capped at 127; the program stream's decoding time is the container's minus 3003 ticks of 90 kHz for every unit (`take_ts()`), so P-only streams get the PMF's pictures-and-timing exactly, and B-frames (pts equal to dts in many files) stay in order. Verified: `check_mp4.py` on fixtures V1..V6 (all but V3, which has HRD, are rewritten), the written delays read back (V1: (0,2),(2,2),(4,2)... as the PMF; V5 with B-frames: strictly increasing countdown), and ffmpeg's decode of the converted stream equals the source's, plane for plane (the first compare was wrong: the range flag 1 of the PMF made ffmpeg scale the gray conversion; the rewrite keeps the video's own flag). **Not known on a PSP whether this is what its decoder needs** (it is the PSP's own format and the one difference found between a stream that plays and ones that froze).
    *Real PSP results 6 (V1..V6 + the maintainer's own video; the files are `video-failed.txt` / `video-crash-last.txt`):* **V1** (x264 Main, aud=1, info SEI only): `first-unit -33 50 5539`, `first-decode 0 0`, three more `unit-ts ffffffff`, `source-end 170`, nothing after: frozen. **V2** (x264 Baseline, refs 1): `first-decode 80628002` (the decoder's fatal error, PPSSPP's name `SCE_MPEG_ERROR_AVC_DECODE_FATAL`), then `80628001` x31, closed cleanly (`td-unregist-skipped 31 1`, all other steps done). **V3-V6 and `My_video`:** `get-au -2147352167 96 0 0` = **0x80020199** (`SCE_KERNEL_ERROR_UNKNOWN_SEMID`, I believe) from the first call, 31 times, no unit, `no-picture` / `source-end`: identical for Main with HRD, High, B-frames and Baseline, so not the streams: the order of the tests (V1, V2, V3 .. then the own video, no restart of psp5) says the library was broken by V2's fatal error and stays broken (new sessions, `sceMpegFinish` / `sceMpegInit` and all, do not mend it). `s_poisoned` is set by 0x80628002 (decode) and 0x80020199 (unit), `video_poisoned()`, `video_open()` refuses first thing (it writes nothing: `video-failed.txt` of the culprit stays), `videoplay.c` shows `S_VID_POISON` when the refusal is the reason. **XMB as oracle:** the own video plays in the XMB; V1, V2, V3, V5, V6 (no audio track) "unsupported", V4 (High) "corrupted": the PSP's player needs an audio track, and the decoder cannot take High. The HandBrake PSP preset (`psp-preset.json` of the maintainer): `x264`, `medium`, `baseline`, level 1.3, RF 22 two-pass, 480x272, `pfr` 30, `av_aac` 160 kbps stereo, `av_mp4`, `Mp4iPodCompatible`, chapter markers (a text track), not web-optimised (moov at the end), keyint by x264's default (250): long groups of pictures, VFR. **Hypothesis to test (not known):** the PSP's `sceMpeg` AVC path needs what its own streams have in every picture: an SPS with VUI timing + HRD (`nal_hrd_parameters_present_flag`, `pic_struct_present_flag`) and `pic_timing` / `buffering_period` / `recovery_point` SEI messages (T3 = T2 without SEI froze; V1, plain Main, froze; V2, plain Baseline, fatal) while the XMB's MP4 route (`sceMp4` + `sceVideocodec`, timing from the container) needs none. If it is so, psp5 has to write them: rewrite the SPS (copy up to `vui_parameters_present_flag`, write Sony's VUI) and put a `pic_timing` SEI in every unit and a `buffering_period` + `recovery_point` in every key frame, in the syntax of the real stream (parse the real SPS and SEI payloads of `test.pmf` for the field lengths). Also untested: a group that does not start at a key frame (the long GOPs of x264's defaults are cut every 59 units: `plan_group()`). Test files: `$S/hb/send` H1 (the preset clone, audio, long GOP), H2 (the same with x264's `nal-hrd=vbr:pic-struct=1:aud=1`, long GOP), H3 (the same with keyint 59).
    *Real PSP results 5 (the new build: T1 and T2 played completely and closed cleanly; T3 froze; the maintainer's own video: "can't be played" with no log, since a clean close deletes it):* `unit-ts` lines (first four units) show `00000000 00016b4a 00000000 00015f8f` for a stamped unit and `ffffffff` x4 for the others: the library gives -1 in all four words for a unit with no time stamp (`ts_valid()` is right). T3 froze at the fourth unit (`source-end 128` was the last full line). **`video-failed.txt`** (`crumb_keep()`): `video_close()` keeps the crash file instead of deleting it when `v->failed || v->err_total > 0` (decode errors, get-unit errors other than "no data", put errors, a failed session `video_play()` -> `failed = 1`, the watchdog), and `video_open()` writes `open-fail <line> <code>` before each `goto fail` / early return and keeps the file; `session_create()` writes `fail-construct`, `fail-create`, `fail-query`, `fail-regist`, `fail-initau` with the answers; the last line is `end <packets> <units> <pictures> <errors>`. **Converter:** `mpegps_annexb()` takes the first AUD of the sample wherever it is (x264 with `aud=1` writes SPS, PPS, SEI, then the picture's AUD, in the first sample: the old code made its own AUD in front of those and left the second in place: two AUDs in a unit), writes it first, then the header's sets (when the sample has none), then every other NAL; any further AUD is dropped. The six test videos (`host-tests/video/fx/V1..V6`: x264 `aud=1:info=0`, main / baseline / main with `nal-hrd=vbr:pic-struct=1` (one pic_timing SEI per unit + buffering periods) / high / main with B-frames / baseline level 1.3 with the encoder's defaults) all pass `check_ps()` and decode frame for frame like their source in ffmpeg. Next: what `video-failed.txt` of the maintainer's own video says.
    *Real PSP results 4 (T1, T2, T3: the PMF of PPSSPP's tests, the same video as an MP4, and that without SEI; `video-crash.txt` of each):* **T1** `first-unit 0 50 3791`, `first-decode 0 0`, `source-end 129`, `get-au 80618001 129 190`, `alive 129 190 190 2147483647`: all 190 units given and decoded without a single `decode-error`, the clock overflowed (my resync to the first unit's time took the library's -1 for a time), then `td-unregist` and the console died: `sceMpegUnRegistStream` with the handle by value is fatal even in a clean session. **T2** the same, with `decode-error 80628001` in bursts: the converter. **T3** ends at `source-end 196` (no `alive`, so it froze within 2 seconds of the first decode). Fixes in `video.c` / `mpegps.c`: (1) the time of a unit is its decoding time (`ts_valid()`: -1 = all ones in both halves is none), else PTS - 0.034, else the one before + `frame_dur` (the MP4's duration over its samples, 1001/30000 for a PMF), else the clock; no resync of the clock. (2) `sceMpegUnRegistStream((SceMpeg)&v->mpeg, stream)`; `TD_STEP()` writes the line of each closing step first and leaves the step out when `s_skip` has it: `crumb_start()` takes the last line of a stale crash file, and when it is `td-...` writes the step into `video-skip.txt` (read by `skip_load()`). (3) `mpegps_annexb()`: the AUD is `09 10/30/50` by the slice kinds (`slice_kind()` reads first_mb and slice_type of every slice), start codes of 4 bytes for types 7, 8, 9 and 3 for all else, a sample's own AUD stays first and the sets follow it, the sets are not added when the sample has any (`mpegps_sample_has_params()`); `video.c` leaves `v->inband` streams without the header's sets and gives `plan_group()` the key flag "has an SPS" (`mpegps_unit_has_sps()`), which is what makes a group start. (4) `PsWriter` is a stream of units now (`ps_init(first, count, size, pull, arg)`, `ps_sector()`): `plan_group()` makes the table and the four sector numbers (the sector of the last byte of each of the first four units, counted from the first), `ahead()` / `advance()` take turns with two unit buffers (`v->au[2]`, `mp4_pull()`; `mp4_size()` converts into `au[0]` when nothing holds it), a sector is filled up with the next unit(s) of the group, the PES packet of the first sector has `81 C1`, 13 bytes of header (PTS, DTS, `1E 60 55`), a later one `81 C0` with 10 when a unit starts in it and `idx - last_stamp_idx >= 16`, SCR is the decoding time - 86000 and never goes back, the mux rate bytes are the PMF's. Tests (private): `video/mp4_test.c` mirrors `video.c`'s callbacks, `video/check_mp4.py` `check_ps()` checks all rules and `same_as_real()` compares an MP4 of the PMF's video (`fx/a_real_es.mp4`, `fx/real_test.pmf` is the PMF) with the PMF sector for sector (negative controls: a stamp rule of 15, 4-byte start codes), and a decode of the converted stream with ffmpeg is the PMF's frame for frame. In PPSSPP the PMF and its MP4 now show the same pictures.
    *Real PSP result 3 (fourth test, 416x240 Baseline 1.3, MP4 silent: `... first-put 32 32 256 0 / first-unit 0 50 14685 0 / first-picture 14685 / first-decode -2141028351 0 0 0 / decode-error x3 / closing / td-stop / td-flush / td-free-es / td-unregist` and the console died):* the table works (the library gave the unit and its size, 14685, as planned), `sceMpegAvcDecode` returned **0x80628001** (PPSSPP's `ErrorCodes.h` has `SCE_MPEG_ERROR_AVC_DECODE_FATAL = 0x80628002` and `AVC_INVALID_VALUE = 0x806201fe`, nothing for ...01) for every unit, and the crash came inside `sceMpegUnRegistStream` (the call took the handle by value as the SDK header says; PPSSPP reads it as the address of the handle like the other calls, which would be harmless there and fatal on a PSP if that is what the firmware does: not known). Teardown skips the call when `dec_errors > 0 || failed`. Differences between our unit and the PSP's own (`test.pmf`): SEI NALs (types 6, 6, 6 in the IDR unit: buffering period, picture timing, recovery point; type 6 picture timing in every other unit), AUD `09 10` (I) / `09 30` (P) instead of `09 F0`, 3-byte start codes in front of SEI and slices, Main profile (constraint set 1, level 2.1, VUI with HRD). **Bug fixed:** `mpegps_annexb()` put the parameter sets in front of an AUD that the sample brought; now the sample's own AUD comes first and the sets (only when the sample has none of its own) follow it (a real-stream MP4 now converts to `AUD SPS PPS SEI SEI SEI IDR` exactly like the PMF). New crash file lines: `params` (head of the SPS/PPS as the decoder gets them), `decode-mode`, `setup` (stream pointer, ES buffer pointer, InitAu result, the AU's ES handle), `au` (ES handle, size, PTS, DTS), `es-head` (the first 16 bytes at the ES handle, read only when it is an address in user RAM), `frame`, and `first-decode` / `decode-error` in hex. Test files for the maintainer (not in the repo): the PMF itself, its video as an MP4 and the same without SEI (`ffmpeg -c copy`, `-bsf:v filter_units=remove_types=6`); all three are 144 x 80 Main level 2.1 (also `host-tests/video/fx/a_real_es.mp4`, `b_no_sei.mp4`).
    *Real PSP result 2 (third test, MP4 silent: `... opened 0 0 / session / mpeg-create / first-feed 32 256 / closing 0 0`):* no unit was ever given (no `first-unit`, no `first-picture`) and the console died inside `video_close()` (the file was not deleted, so it was between `closing` and `crumb_end()`). Teardown now skips `sceMpegAvcDecodeStop` / `sceMpegFlushAllStream` when `v->decoded` is 0 and writes a `td-*` line before each call. For the missing units: the real PMF of PPSSPP's tests (`pspautotests/tests/video/pmf/test.pmf`) starts every GOP's first sector with `pack, system header (18 bytes, copied), private stream 2 (0xBF), PES 0xE0`; the 0xBF payload is `01 E0, four u16 (the sector, counted from this one, in which each of the first four pictures ends), 4 zero bytes, u16 2+4n, u16 n, n x (u16 0x0080 (0 for the last), u16 size of the picture in bytes)`, n at most 59 in that file; the PES with time stamps has flags `81 C1`, header length 13: PTS, DTS (the DTS one frame, 3003 ticks, before the PTS even without B-frames) and `1E 60 55` (P-STD buffer, 85 KB); the pictures follow each other inside the sectors (our writer starts every picture in a sector of its own and pads it with 0xBE, which the real file does only at the end of a GOP). `mpegps.c`: `ps_group()`, `ps_group_plan()` (the group is planned by converting the next up to 59 samples once, `mp4_unit()` in `video.c` is what both planning and writing call), `ps_sector()` writes them; a group with a picture over 65535 bytes gets no table (it does not fit the u16). Verified on the PC against the real layout (`check_mp4.py` `check_ps()`: sizes, flags, the four sector numbers, the stream byte for byte, a negative control that breaks one size), and in PPSSPP (which ignores the packet). **The guess that the PSP's demuxer needs the table is not confirmed.** The watchdog (`since` > 8 s without a picture → `failed`) and the `alive` / `get-au` / `first-unit` / `first-decode` lines of the crash file are there to tell the next test what the library did.
    *Real PSP result (the maintainer's second test, `video-crash-last.txt`: `... avc-profile-level 100 21 / sound-open 44100 1 / sound-out 44100 1 / opened 1 0 / session / mpeg-create 65536 550912 / first-feed 32 256 / first-sound 21 0`, then the console died):* the crash is in the first `sceAudiocodecDecode()` of the AAC decoder, with the module loaded, the cache written out and `CheckNeedMem` / `GetEDRAM` / `Init` all successful: the layout of the parameter block (words 6..10 here, which is what PPSSPP reads) is not what a PSP wants, or another word has to be set. `HW_AAC` is 0: an MP4 has no sound (`audio_ok = 0`; the clock is `dt`); `-DVIDEO_HW_AAC` brings the old path back for PPSSPP. Ways forward: a software AAC-LC decoder (testable on the PC against ffmpeg, costs CPU on the PSP), or the firmware's `sceAac*` library (`libaac.prx`; no stubs in the SDK, the NIDs and the parameter structure would have to be written from PPSSPP's `sceAac.cpp`). Not done.
    *What a real PSP needs that PPSSPP does not (found by reading, not tried):* the AAC decoder needs `PSP_MODULE_AV_AAC` (0x0306) loaded (`load_aac_module()`; the MP3 and ATRAC3+ modules are other ones); the ring buffer's sectors are written by the CPU, so `ring_cb()` ends with `sceKernelDcacheWritebackInvalidateRange()` (the decoders are other processors that read memory, not the CPU's cache); `decode_aac()` calls `sceKernelDcacheWritebackInvalidateAll()` before and after `sceAudiocodecDecode()` and the codec structure is 512 bytes, zeroed, in cache lines of its own (the decoder writes into it, and a stale line of it must not be written back over that). *The crash file:* `crumb()` appends a line to `PSP5/video-crash.txt` before every step that could hang the PSP (open, the profile and level of the H.264 stream, sound open, `sceMpegCreate` with its sizes, first feed, first picture, first sound, closing); `video_close()` deletes it; `crumb_start()` (the first thing `video_open()` does) copies a file that is still there to `video-crash-last.txt`. Nothing but numbers and step names is written.
  - *The player* (`videoplay.c`): ✕ pause, ← → a jump of 10 seconds made 0.4 s after the last press (the bar shows the target), L / R the video before or after (the place of the one left is written down), ○ leaves; at the end the overlay is shown and ✕ plays again. `videomem.c`: `videomem.txt` in `PSP5_DATA_DIR`, "length place name" lines, the newest first, 200 at most; a place in the first 5 s or last 10 s is forgotten; the place is written every 15 s while playing and when the player closes or the video is changed. `scePowerTick(PSP_POWER_TICK_ALL)` while it plays; `ui_sleep()` pauses it when the PSP sleeps.
  - *Tests* (private): `ui/menu_test.c` (three Media cards; the Videos and Images screens: the folder, the file that opens the player or viewer with its place among the files, ○, the list as it was left, no player on the right, the title); `video/mp4_test.c` + `video/check_mp4.py` (an MP4 with and without B-frames and with the movie box at the end is read, written as Annex B and as a program stream, and **ffmpeg decodes both to as many pictures as the MP4 has**; the AAC frames get ADTS headers that ffmpeg counts the same); `video/media_test.c` (the endings, the folders, where a video was left); `image/view_test.c` + `image/mkview.py` (JPEG with all eight EXIF orientations, PNG, BMP in every kind, damaged files, `img_fit_down()`); the wiring check and the stub of libjpeg's header were adjusted. In PPSSPP (screenshot kit): the cards, both lists, folders, the viewer with a camera-turned JPEG, a broken one, a 2000 x 1200 BMP and a 100 x 100 PNG, an MP4 played, paused, jumped in, played to its end and again, B-frames, a PMF, L / R, and the question after a video was left. **Not tried on a real PSP**: nothing of the video player or the viewer.

- **The player: round buttons, the △ hint, value popups, one volume bar, a player of its own for the ♪ popup, music in the File Manager and the FTP screen** (`musicui.c`, `musicui.h`, `gfx.c` `gfx_speaker_scaled()`, `osd.c` `osd_show_volume()`, `ui.c` `draw_top_bar()`, `main.c` `note_popup()`, `mode_music_*()`, `app_stop_ex()`; on request: round buttons that turn white when chosen, the △ hint at the top right of the player, a popup for Shuffle, Repeat and the equalizer, only the launcher's volume bar, no song in the top bar, no double inputs in the ♪ popup, music in every menu that can have it).
  - *Buttons:* no frame. The chosen one is a white disc (`BTN_R` 14, the symbol dark on it), the others are bare symbols, a little smaller than before (`gfx_speaker_scaled(x, y, 0.85f, color)`, the bars and triangles of previous / next, `play_glyph(.., 5.4f, ..)`, the dots r 1.6). `draw_player(m, px, py, a, focus, hint)`: `hint` draws the △ at the top right (`gfx_button(.., BTN_TRIANGLE, plate, mark)`), light plate and dark mark when the player has the focus, the two swapped when the list has it; the ♪ popup draws no hint.
  - *○ closing the ♪ popup (later, on request):* `main.c` `note_popup()` blanked the screen's input only while `mu->mini` or the sound button was down; in the frame that ○ closed the popup `mini` was already 0, so the same ○ went on to the screen below. It now also blanks the input when `musicui_mini_update()` returned "closed".
  - *Bar and popup look (later, on request):* the chosen song bar has no frame, only the dot (`gfx_circle` at the played share); the ♪ popup draws no `popup_panel` behind the player, only the player's own rounded panel, made nearly solid (`draw_player(.., panel)`: alpha 150 on the screen, 240 in the popup, so that cards and text behind do not show through).
  - *The "..." popup* lists **Shuffle, Repeat, Equalizer** (`more_rows()`, `row_values()`, `row_value()`, `row_set()`, `row_name()`; the third row only when `sys_equalizer() >= 0`, the same calls and values as Settings → Screen and Sound: 0 Off, 1-4 Heavy, Pops, Jazz, Unique). ✕ on a row opens a second level (`MU_POPUP_CHOICE`: the values listed, the current one framed, ✕ takes it, ○ cancels, START closes all), the pattern of Settings' choice popup; nothing is switched by ← → or ✕ on the row.
  - *Volume:* `MU_POPUP_VOLUME` draws nothing of its own. ← → change the system volume (`sys_set_volume`, 1 step, held buttons repeat) and `osd_show_volume()` is called every frame so the launcher's bar (`osd.c`) stays up; ✕, ○ or START end it. When the volume cannot be read nothing is set.
  - *Top bar:* the little note with the title of the playing song (`draw_top_bar()`) is gone.
  - *No double inputs:* the ♪ popup and the Music screen shared one player state (the chosen button, the held direction `rep_dir`, the open popup); the screen below gets neutral input every frame, which reset the popup's `rep_dir`, so a held ← was a new press each frame. `PlayerState mp` is the popup's own state; `swap_player()` swaps it into the player fields while a `musicui_mini_*` function runs.
  - *Music in every menu that can:* `main.c` `note_popup(mu, in, dt, allow, keep_cover)` is the one step of the ♪ popup (reads the button, opens, steers, closes, gives the screen no buttons while it is open), used by the launcher and by `files_mode()` and `ftp_mode()` (their own loops: `mode_music_begin/end/sleep`, a `MusicUi` of their own, the music paused while the PSP sleeps and going on at wake-up). `app_stop_ex(list, ui, keep_music)` leaves the player thread alive for the File Manager, the plugin picker and the FTP screen; a game that starts and USB Mode (the Memory Stick goes to the computer) still stop it.
  - *Folder in MUSIC that does not open (reported, then explained by the maintainer: `ms0:/MUSIC/OwO`, with songs in it, showed the top of `ms0:` and the path grew to `ms0:/MUSIC/OwO/MUSIC/OwO`):* `activate()` builds `dir/name` and `music_list_dir()` hands exactly that to `sceIoDopen()` (as `files.c` `read_dir()` does), so the PSP answered with the lines of `ms0:/` for that path (and with those of `ms0:/MUSIC` for `.../OwO/MUSIC`): nothing in the app makes a path resolve to its own ancestor. The cause on the card or in the firmware is not known (a folder entry that points to the top of the stick is one possibility); **not reproduced on a PSP**. `answers_with_another_folder()` (`musicui.c`) compares the lines of the folder about to be opened with those of the open folder and with those of the top of the storage (`ms0:/`); if they are the same, the folder is not entered (`warn_t`, `S_MU_BAD_FOLDER` in place of the path for 4 s, `SND_ERROR`) and the path cannot grow. `adopt_dir()` was split from `load_dir()` so that the lines can be looked at before they replace the open folder's. It does not make the songs of such a folder playable.
  - *Tests* (private): `ui/menu_test.c` (the white disc and the bare buttons, the hint's two looks, the popups, the volume, the ♪ popup's own state, a held ← in the popup with the screen behind it fed neutral input, and a negative control that fails without the swap), `music/wiring_check.py`, `ftp/main_check.py`, `power/main_check.py`, `look/source_check.py` (adjusted to the new wiring). Run lightly on request (the Music-only part of the menu test, the full menu and pixel tests, the library test and the text checks); the full suite and mutation runs were not repeated. Both ARK builds from fresh copies: 0 warnings. **None of it has been tried on a real PSP.**

- **The background goes dark behind an opened card** (`ui.c` `ui->dim`, `ui->dim_alpha`, `draw_dim()`, `DIM_SPEED`, `DIM_ALPHA_MUSIC`, `DIM_ALPHA_LIBRARY`; `musicui.c` `musicui_draw()`; on request: "make the darkening happen behind the whole open Music card instead of just from ms0:/Music down ... for Library card make it so that the background also is full brightness at the beginning and then turns darker when Library card is opened")
  - *Before:* the Music screen had a shade behind its list only (`gfx_gradient_h(0, 42, 312, 206, ...)`: from the line with the folder name down, alpha 110 fading to 0 at x 312), so the lamp's light ran bright across the top of the screen; the Library was not darkened at all.
  - *Later, on request ("change the darkness of background from 100 to 150 in both the Library card and the Cards inside Media tab"):* `DIM_ALPHA_MUSIC` and `DIM_ALPHA_LIBRARY` are 150 now (the Music, Videos and Images screens share the first, the Library has the second); the numbers below are the ones of the earlier versions. The private `ui/menu_test.c` expects 150 / 75 now.
  - *Now:* `ui->dim` goes 0 → 1 at 4 a second (`DIM_SPEED`: 15 frames) while the tab is `TAB_LIBRARY` or `TAB_MUSIC`, and back to 0 otherwise; `draw_dim()` lays one rectangle over the whole screen, the colour (8, 8, 12), alpha `dim_alpha * smooth(dim)` (100 for both: first it was 150 for Music, the value the old Music tab had, and 100 for the Library, whose lines on black would be gone under more; on request "make the strength on Music the same as on Library", so `DIM_ALPHA_MUSIC` is 100 now, and the two numbers are kept apart so that they can differ again), after the background and before the top bar, so the tabs, the clock and the battery stay bright. `dim_alpha` is set from the screen that is open and kept while it fades out. The shade in `musicui_draw()` is gone. The cards of Games and Media and the Library card of Games have the background as it was, at full brightness; the first update of a press still has the old tab, so the darkening moves from the second update on. One more full-screen rectangle in the two open screens (one draw call; the old Music tab had one too).
  - *Tests* (private): `ui/menu_test.c` new block "the darkening behind an opened card" (in the Music-only part too: 225 checks there): the Media tab and the Library card have the background exactly as `theme_backdrop_media` / `theme_backdrop_library` draw it (3 themes), the opened Music screen has it under exactly 150 / 255 of (8, 8, 12) over the whole left strip from y 0 to 271 (also half way through the fade: 75 / 255), the opened Library 100 / 255 (**these tests were written for 150 on Music and were NOT run after Music was made 100 on request; the numbers in `menu_test.c` were changed by hand to 100 / 50 and have not been run**), the speed (4 a second, 15 frames, never past 0 or 1, the strength kept while it fades out), and the Library card at full brightness again afterwards; `STRIP_DIFF` and the "Library shows its background when it has settled" check expect the darkening (`DIM_A()`). *Mutation checks* of the new lines of `ui.c` (38 changes): 35 caught; the 3 that survive change nothing (a rectangle that starts at x -1, a strength of 0 drawn). The README pictures `library.png`, `library-folders.png` and `music.png` were taken again.

- **Media tab, the Music screen, the player and the sound button** (`ui.c`, `ui.h`, `musicui.c` / `musicui.h` (new), `music.c` / `music.h` (rewritten), `main.c`, `osd.c`, `loader.c`, `theme.c`, `icons.c`, `gfx.c`, `lang.c`, `home.h`; on request, with three pictures of the maintainer's: "Media.png" (the background), "Music Icon.png" (the note) and "Music Player.png" (the player))
  - *The tabs.* `TAB_GAMES, TAB_MEDIA, TAB_LIBRARY, TAB_MUSIC`. The top bar has two tabs (Games, Media); the Library and Music are *screens* (`is_screen()`: the top bar shows their name and the cog wheel, L / R do nothing, ○ goes back). The Media tab is a row of cards like Games' (`row_tab`: the card, the *Open* button under it, the name next to it) with one card, `HOME_MEDIA_MUSIC` (-4: a negative id, which the loader of the saved Home ignores). ✕ on it opens `TAB_MUSIC`; ○ on the Music screen from the top goes back to `TAB_MEDIA`.
  - *The background.* `theme.c` `med_scene()` draws it once into the video-memory snapshot (kind 2 of `scene_ready()`, beside the surface (0) and the Library (1); `Ui.bg_lib` is 0 / 1 / 2): the curves `MED_TOP[]` and `MED_BOT[]` were measured from the reference picture, the colours come from the theme (`Theme.glow` for the lamp and the high light, `Theme.haze`, `Theme.beam` for the line), as the Library's do. The Media tab and the Music screen share it (no dip between them: `lib_calm`). The reference is only a stylization here: the mock mixes colours additively by alpha, so what the PSP's blending gives was not seen.
  - *The note.* `ICON_MUSIC` (`icons.c`, at the END of the enum: `icontest.py` hardcodes the number of `ICON_CUBE`) is a signed-distance shape (`music_shape()`, parameters measured on the reference: IoU 0.9674 against its mask). `gfx_music_icon()` renders one cached 43 x 43 texture and draws it 1:1 at full size and scaled while the card slides in.
  - *The Music screen* (`musicui.c`). Level 0 is the list of storages (`music_roots()`: `ms0:/MUSIC`, `ef0:/MUSIC`, not `ef0` twice when ARK has put the internal storage at `ms0:`), level 1 a folder (`music_list_dir()`: the folders first, then the songs it can play, each group by name without regard to capitals, no hidden names, at most 1000 lines); with one root the screen opens straight into it. The list is drawn as the File Manager draws files (a faint fill, a 1 px frame, separators, `gfx_folder_picture()`, a green icon with the ending, a marquee for a long chosen name, a scroll bar). The panel on the right is the player: cover, title, artist, the bar with the times, the buttons (`MU_VOLUME .. MU_MORE`, `MU_BAR`), the popups (`popup_dim` / `popup_panel` / `popup_choice`): *More* with Shuffle and Repeat, and *Volume* (0 to 30, `sys_volume()` / `sys_set_volume()`: the PSP's own volume, as Settings > Screen and Sound; "Unavailable" when the Impose service does not answer). ← → on the bar jump `SEEK_STEP` = 10 s (clamped to 0 .. duration - 1; a held direction repeats). The Music keys that the old tab had (L / R, □, ← → for track and seek, ✕ for pause) are gone.
  - *The player* (`music.c`, one thread, a queue of 16 commands: the screen writes `s_q_head`, the thread `s_q_tail`). The sound goes out through the SRC channel (`sceAudioSRCChReserve(samples, rate, 2)`; the channel is reserved for the size of the frames of the song: 17 to 4111 samples, and again when the rate or the size changes); mono is made stereo here. A kind of file is a `Codec { open, frame, seek, close }`; `FORMATS[]` maps the ending to one: `mp3` (sceMp3, as before, behind the interface), `wav` (`wav_codec`: PCM 8 / 16 / 24 / 32 bits, mono or stereo, in software; extensible; chunks found by walking the file; the data length may be 0xFFFFFFFF or longer than the file), `at3` / `oma` / `aa3` (`atrac_codec`: libpspatrac3, `PSP_MODULE_AV_ATRAC3PLUS` loaded after AVCODEC and MP3, the file read through a buffer of 64 KB by `sceAtracGetStreamDataInfo` / `sceAtracAddStreamData`, jumps by `sceAtracGetBufferInfoForReseting` / `sceAtracResetPlayPosition`; a file that fits the buffer is given whole; the output is taken as two channels, which is what PPSSPP's library does by default and which I could not check on a PSP). A rate that the SRC channel does not have is refused (`rate_ok()`), and so is a file that a codec cannot open (it is skipped). **Not in: AAC (`.m4a`, `.aac`) and WMA.** `sceAudiocodec` has an AAC codec (0x1003) but the layout of its parameter block is not something I could confirm without a PSP, and a wrong block can crash the PSP; the SDK has nothing for WMA. Ogg and FLAC have no decoder on the PSP.
  - *The queue.* `music_play_folder(dir, entries, n, index)` hands the thread the songs of the folder (the folders among the entries are left out; the index is the chosen line of the entries) through a staging copy under a semaphore; `build_order()` makes the order (shuffled with the chosen song first, each song once; a shuffled folder is shuffled again for each round of *All*), `step_pos()` moves in it (at the end: nothing, or round when Repeat is All; a broken file is passed on in the direction of the button: on for a song that ended and for Next, back for Previous), Repeat *Current Song* starts the same song again, Previous after 3 s begins the song again, a jump past the end (`seek` returns -1) is the next song. `music_set_repeat()` is not a command (the thread reads it when a song ends); shuffle is (the order is built again round the song that plays). When songs fail one after the other the player stops after `min(n, 8)` of them, not for ever. `music_track()` copies the tags between two reads of a sequence number; `music_generation()` changes with every start (the screen keeps its title text and cover for a generation).
  - *Two faults the new test found in the first version of the queue (both fixed):* (1) `start_at()` stopped looking after the first broken file whenever a command had started it, because it asked whether the queue was empty and the command that was being done was still in it (`s_q_tail` moves when a command is done): a broken file at the start of a folder, or next to the song after *Next*, stopped the music. It asks `command_waiting()` now (more than the one in flight). (2) the skip always went forward: *Previous* onto a broken file played the song after it. `start_at(pos, step)` takes the direction, and the end of the folder goes round for Repeat *All* while skipping.
  - *Another fault, in the song's end:* a WAV sample of 8 bits was shifted left as a negative number (`(p[0] - 128) << 8`), which is undefined in C (UBSan found it); it is a multiplication now.
  - *The sound button.* A program cannot read ♪ (`PSP_CTRL_NOTE`); `osd.c` reads it as the volume bar does its settings, in the kernel: `sctrlHENFindFunction("sceController_Service", "sceCtrl_driver", 0x3A622550)` (the NID of `sceCtrlPeekBufferPositive`) and `kuKernelCall` with a `SceCtrlData`, one event for each press (`osd_note_pressed()`). `main.c` reads it once a frame after `osd_update()` (not while a game starts or the PSP sleeps), opens / steers / closes the popup (`musicui_mini_open/update/draw`: the player alone, in the middle of the screen, over any screen; ○ or ♪ closes it) and replaces the buttons of the screen below with `UI_NO_INPUT` while it is open and in the frame of the press. It is drawn by `osd_draw()` (`osd_set_overlay()`), at the end of every frame of the screens that live in the main loop; the File Manager, USB Mode and the FTP server have their own loops and call `app_stop()` first, which ends the music (`music_term()`), as it always did. **Not tried on a PSP: the kernel call, whether the firmware's own ♪ picture comes up as well, and whether the player thread's priority is right (0x14, as before).**
  - *The cover.* `loader_request_cover(serial, path, album)` / `loader_take_cover(&serial, &tex)` (the loader no longer includes `music.h`): the request is written between two "not wanted" marks and the thread drops a copy if the wish changed meanwhile (so it never opens half of two names); the screen asks only while the Music screen or the popup shows the cover (`musicui_update_cover()`), and lets it go when both are closed.
  - *Texts* (`lang.c`, 11 languages): `S_TAB_MEDIA`, `S_ON`, `S_MU_SHUFFLE`, `S_MU_REPEAT`, `S_MU_REPEAT_ONE`; the hint of an empty Music folder is "Put your songs in ms0:/MUSIC"; Repeat *All* is the existing `S_ALL`, the storages are `S_F_MS` / `S_F_INTERNAL`.
  - *File Manager:* `.oma` and `.aa3` have the green of the other songs (`type_color()`).
  - *The pictures of the README* (PPSSPP 1.20.4, the kit, the demo stick; test build `make EXTRA=-DPSP5_TEST` of the commit): new `media.png` (the Media tab) and `music.png` (the Music screen with a song playing: the cover, the bar, the buttons), and `games.png`, `options.png`, `power-menu.png` and `theme.png` taken again because the top bar now says *Media* where it said *Music* (each differs from the old one only in that word: 575 to 724 pixels in a box of 61 x 24). The README table has *Media* next to *Music* and the two File Manager pictures together. Scripts: `session-tools/music-2026-11-07/scripts/` (`media.txt`, `stale.txt`, `theme.txt`; `options.png` is `sc6.txt` of the kit). The demo stick needs `3132575872 ULUS90003` in `PSP5/lastplayed.txt` for Neon Harbor to be the first card.
  - *Tests* (private, none of them on a PSP): new group **`music`** (`music/music_test.c`, 505 checks, ASan + UBSan: `music.c` is included in the test and runs on fakes of `sceMp3`, `sceAtrac`, `sceAudio` and the kernel's semaphores (`music/fake/`); the fake SRC channel is a gate that lets the thread take as many blocks as a test says and holds it at the next one, so the state is read at an exact place in a song) and new check **`musicwiring`** (`music/wiring_check.py`: the text of `osd.c`, `main.c`, `loader.c`, `Makefile` and `music.c` for what the PC cannot run; 12 hand-made changes, all caught). `ui/menu_test.c` has the Media tab (the card, the Open button, the background in every theme), the Music screen (storages, folders, songs, frames, the moving bars, the player's buttons, the bar, seek, both popups, the volume, the cover, the popup of ♪) and no longer the old Music tab; `ui/stubs.c` has a fake folder tree and a fake player; `icons/icontest.py` the note against the reference mask; `gfx/gfxtest.c` `test_music_icon()`; `ui/loader_test.c` the cover request (a name, an album, once, the serial, an empty path = none). *Mutation checks of `music.c`* (`musicmut.py`: every number +1 / -1 and every operator turned round in the lines from the list of files that can be played to the end; the numbers of a negative literal and of an array size left out): 1483 changes, 723 caught, 7 would not build, **753 survive**. The first runs found real holes, each closed by a test (and two real faults in `music.c`, above): the order of names (capitals), the font need of a tag in Cyrillic / Greek (the borderline lead byte), the WAV reader (the letters of RIFF / WAVE, a format chunk of 26 and of 40 bytes, a data length that is too long or 0xFFFFFFFF, a chunk after the data, no channels, a block align that does not fit, a last block of 17 and of 16 samples, one sample), every rate of the output, the ATRAC header (15 and 16 bytes, no channels, three channels, no samples), the library starving when it is not fed, the time and the length, a jump 0.4 and 0.6 s before the end, the limit of 8 failed songs, Repeat on a file that has gone, two *Next* one after the other, Previous at 2.56 s, shuffle of two songs and of the first song, the guards of `music_play_folder()` (a line past the end, 1000 and 1001 songs), and what `music_term()` clears. **What survives, by kind** (I read the lists): 383 of the 502 changes in the **MP3 header parser** (`frame_len()`, `parse_first_frame()`, `find_frame()`, `mp3_seek()`: MPEG-2, mono offsets, `Info`, VBRI and the Xing table are not exercised; this is the old code, moved behind `Codec` and not changed, and it has run on a PSP before; the test has MPEG-1 stereo with a Xing header and without any); in the rest (370 of 981): values that are written before they are read (`int end = 0`, `u32 ch = 2`, `s_decoded = 0` before the first frame), error codes that every caller compares with `< 0` (`return -2`), `fd >= 0` against `fd > 0` (a PC file number is never 0), buffer sizes and loop bounds that only matter as an overflow that no sanitizer sees in this build (`s_atrac_out`, `i <= values`, `s_out_idx ^= 0`), sleep times, the quality of the shuffle's random numbers (every variant is still a permutation with the chosen song first), arguments that the fakes ignore (`sceKernelStartThread` length, the semaphore's wait count), the size of the listing's first allocation, `n > 1` before sorting one name, and conditions that differ only for an input that cannot occur (`cmp(…) >= 3.0f` with a time that is a multiple of 0.128). *Mutation checks of `musicui.c`* (`uimut.py`, the same kinds of change; run against the Music sections of `menu_test.c` alone: `MENU_ONLY_MUSIC=1`, 197 checks, 3 seconds): **1441 changes, 459 caught, 1 would not build, 981 survive.** The logic is checked well (`MusicUi` on its own: where the screen was left and the line it was left on, ○ up through the folders and back to the right storage, a folder that is empty or cannot be read, the depth of 15, the held buttons' speed (0.32 s, then 0.085 s, then 0.045 s after 1.2 s), the stick's limits (39 / 40 and 215 / 216), the volume (0 to 30, nothing asked of the system outside it, no volume to be read), Shuffle and Repeat going round both ways, the bar's jumps (clamped at 0 and at the last second, from where the last jump went for 0.6 s, a length of 1 s or less as unknown), the popup's speed, the popup of ♪): of its 423 changes in the code that is not drawing (`musicui_open` to `musicui_update_cover`) 94 survive, and the ones I looked at are equivalent (`n < 1` before `n = 0`, a sign of the scroll that only matters for 3 or more storages, `<=` where the numbers cannot meet, the speed of the list's scroll, the fade of the cover). **The drawing is not pinned: 847 of the 926 changes in `draw_player`, `draw_list`, `draw_popup` and `musicui_draw` survive** (the places, sizes and colours of the list, the bar, the buttons and the popups are checked as pictures that I looked at, and by the frame, the fill and a few positions in `menu_test.c`, not number by number). The changed lines of `ui.c` have no mutation run of their own.
  - *Not checked, said plainly:* the sound of any decoder, the SRC channel with real rates, the ATRAC library's behaviour with the real files (the fake answers as the header says), the drawing on a PSP (the mock adds by alpha). **PPSSPP was run for the pictures only** (software renderer, the screenshot kit): the Media background and the Music screen look as they should with true additive blending, and a song played there (MP3 through its `sceMp3`); no other format was played in it.

- **PS4: no artifact at the wave's lower end, and a loop that is never empty** (`theme.c` `ps4_points()` (new), `ps4_draw()` (new), `deco_ps4()`, `PS4_LIFE` / `PS4_LOOP` / `PS4_FADE` / `PS4_N`; `themedata.h` `ps4n_x` / `ps4n_y`; on request: "check the PS4 theme, it has weird artifact at the bottom of the Wave, also make it loop better so that it doesnt disappear completely")
  - *What was seen.* Frames of the real `theme.c` on the PC mock (`render_real`, 120 frames, 15 a second), the lower end enlarged: from about 5 s a short bright streak at the bottom end of the wave, at 6.3 to 7 s two streaks that cross and make triangles; the same kind of two or three stray pixels and a short diagonal streak at the top right corner at 0.8, 2.4 and 3.2 s. Drawing the strip's outline (the five offset curves `-56, -30, -4, 3, 24` and the points) showed folds.
  - *The cause (the table, not the code).* In `ps4n_x` / `ps4n_y` the lowest point (`j` = 0) of keys 17 to 29 (4.6 to 7.8 s) had wandered: the angle of its step to the next point (from straight up) goes 35, 42, 53, 66, 75, 81, 86, 89, 91, 92, 96, 100, 105 degrees, which is flat, while the steps above it are at 20 to 30 (keys 28 and 29 also had `j` = 1 at 38 and 61). The reference's wave has faded away at the bottom by then (the measured edge brightness of that point falls from 34 of 255 at key 17 to 1 from key 22 on), so the dynamic programming that traced the wave had nothing to follow and put the point anywhere. The two points that `deco_ps4()` adds below the picture went on in the direction of the first step, which is flat, to the left, so the strip turned sideways at its end, and the offset curves across it (up to 56 px) crossed: the dot product of a step along an offset curve with the middle line's step was -446 at the bottom and -356 at the top at the worst age (it must be positive), the worst turn between two steps was 72 degrees at the bottom and 70 at the top, and the highest point of the strip's end edge was at y = 208, on the screen (it must be below 272, or the band shows a cut end). At the top the last point of every key is pinned to the screen's corner (480, 0), so the last step turned 40 to 66 degrees up from a flat step (keys 8 to 10 even dip slightly just before it), which folds the strip on the inside of that bend (the band at its left is 56 px wide and a step is 17 to 20 px: the radius of a bend is the step over the turn in radians, so a bend must turn less than about 17 to 20 degrees a step, or the inside of the band is folded).
  - *The repair (`themedata.h`, done with a private script, `session-tools/ps4-2026-11-06/repair_ps4.py`, that reads the table, applies the rule and writes it back in the same layout).* Each of the two lowest steps of a key (point `j` to `j` + 1, `j` = 1 then 0, from the top down) is clamped to what a smooth curve allows: its angle from straight up lies in [the step above's angle - 14, the step above's angle + 2] degrees and is not below 0, and it is at most 23 px long (a normal step is 17 to 22). The last step is clamped to turn at most 12 degrees from the one before it (its length is kept). A clamp is continuous in the data, so nothing jumps between two keys (and the Catmull-Rom curve in time stays smooth: no point of the strip moves more than 0.4 px in 0.01 s), and a step that was already inside the limits is not touched: **16 points in the 13 keys 17 to 29 at the bottom (keys 0 to 16 are as measured) and 29 last points** (key 20 keeps its last point); `ae` and `bb` are as measured. The new lowest point of keys 20 to 29 lies 12 to 40 px from the old one (keys 17 to 19: 0.5 to 7 px), at y 266 to 286, below the edge of the screen from key 19 on; the new last points lie at x 480 to 491, y 0 to 15, a little below the corner (the wave leaves the screen at the right edge, and its last 20 px there carry an alpha of 0 to 25 of 255 for the band and 6 to 30 for the rim).
  - *A third fault, found through a surviving mutation (`theme.c` `ps4_points()`, the normals).* The change `if (NX[j] < 0)` to `if (NX[j] < -1)` (no turning of the normal at all) survived every test. The turn was used at 14 of the 801 ages x 26 points (ages 2.41 to 2.6 s): the raw normal `(-dy, dx) / l` of a nearly flat piece that dips by a hair has `NX` a little below 0 (down to -0.0026) and `NX < 0` negated both parts, so `NY` went from +1 to -1: the band at that point lay on the other side of the wave. That was a star-shaped streak at the top right for four frames at 30 a second (x 447 to 479, y 0 to 69, up to 48 of 255 in the picture; the six pinned moments do not fall in it, so no fingerprint saw it, and the strip test's dot product along a flat piece is blind to a vertical jump of a normal). The rule is `NY < 0` now: the strip runs from the left to the right, so its right-hand normal has `NY` = `dx / l` above 0 everywhere, and the turn is only a guard for a curve that went back. Checked: the old rule and the new one, 180 frames (0 to 6 s at 30 a second): 4 frames differ, 5 451 pixels, and the new ones are clean. The strip test checks the normals now (none points up, neighbours turn at most 32 degrees; the largest turn is 14), and with the old rule put back it fails both checks (lowest `NY` -1.000, a turn of 179 degrees).
  - *The code.* `deco_ps4()` was split in three: `ps4_points(age, ...)` (the points, the brightness at them and the normals, as before, from the key that the age gives: 15 pictures a second, every fourth of them a key, Catmull-Rom in time), `ps4_draw(age, strength)` (the strips, 4 quads across the wave for each of the 25 steps: 100 quads; the frame's display list is 6 784 bytes with the snapshot) and `deco_ps4()` (the loop). The strip goes on below the picture in a straight line, by 1.5 and by 3.0 of the table's lowest step (it was 1 and 2), so that its end, with the band at its left (the end edge of the band is 56 px from the middle line, so on a strip that leans 35 degrees it ends 32 px higher than the middle line does), is below the edge of the screen at every age: the highest point of the end edge, over every age and every offset from -56 to 24, is at y = 278 (it was 208); the upper end is past the right edge or above the top (the test measures that the most it is on the screen is -0.2 px).
  - *The loop.* Before: one wave of 8 s with `smooth_step(0, 0.5, t) * smooth_step(0, 0.9, 8 - t)`, so it was gone for about a second at the seam (and the reference's last picture is not its first: the wave is 40 px further right and the lower half is dimmer, which is why there was a fade). Now a wave lives `PS4_LIFE` = 8 s (the table covers 0.1 to 7.8 s; after that the last key is held) and a new one is born every `PS4_LOOP` = 6 s. In the last `PS4_FADE` = 2 s of the old one the new one (age = the time in the loop) is drawn with the strength `born = smooth_step(0, 2, t)` and the old one (age `t + 6`) with `1 - born`; the weights always add up to 1, and from 2 s to 6 s only the new one is drawn at full strength. The last moment of the loop is the old wave at age 6 s at full strength, and the first is the same wave at age 6 s (as the old one with `born` = 0): the same picture, pixel for pixel (frames at 0, 6 and 12 s are equal; the old test said 8 s). The speed of the wave is as before (the age is real time; only the round is shorter, 6 s instead of 8). The measured strength (the sum of the absolute difference to the picture without the wave, on the mock, at 120 moments) is at least 0.54 of the strongest at every moment (102 301 against 186 655, at 0.15 s: the old wave is nearly out and the new one has just begun to come in) and never changes by more than 0.08 of the strongest between two moments 0.05 s apart. The edge brightness of the table falls from 48 (the mean `ae` at 0.1 s) to 20 (at 5.5 to 6 s) and rises again to 23 (7.8 s), so the weakest moment of every round is when the old wave's brightness is at its lowest and the new one has not come in yet; this is the reference's own brightness. Making the weakest moment stronger (a minimum brightness) was not done: it would change the look.
  - *What it costs.* One wave is 100 quads (100 draw calls, which is also what it was); two waves, in the last 2 s of every 6, are 200 (the display list 13 184 bytes instead of 6 784, the mock counts 201 draw calls against 101). PS3's ribbon makes 301 and PS2 25, so PS4 is under PS3 in the 2 s that it draws two and as before in the other 4. Each draw call is two kernel calls on the PSP (see the dust entry below), so **the cost is the second wave (the number of points of a strip is as before: the 1.5 and 3.0 instead of 1 and 2 only make the end longer); that this does not slow the PSP down is a guess, nothing here has been measured on a PSP.** If PS4 stutters in the 2 s of two waves, one way back would be to draw the old wave, which is fading, with half the quads (every second step of its strip, as PS3's silk does).
  - *Not done on purpose.* A "ping-pong" (the wave rolls to the right and back, which loops with no cross-fade and no empty moment) was not made: it changes how the wave moves, which the maintainer's reference does not do. Half a loop between two waves (always two on the screen) was not made either: it would show two fainter waves for all of the loop. The pictures of the `ps4` row of `want_frame` changed at all six moments (2.6 and 4.7 s have one wave, the other four two or the old one fading).
  - *Tests (private).* `gfx/theme_test.c` (512 checks): the old "the wave is not there at 0 s" and "it loops in 8 s" are replaced by: the wave changes the picture at every one of 120 moments of the loop (at least 30 000 and 0.3 of its best), it is never more than 0.08 of its best different from the moment before, it is there at seven moments from 0.3 to 5.9 s with no hook, it reaches the bottom and the top right corner (counting absolute differences: the mock mixes by alpha, so the old count of brighter pixels at the bottom, 6 pixels, was luck), frames at 0, 6 and 12 s are equal and at 3 s are not, one wave is 100 draw calls and 3000 to 12 000 bytes and two waves are more than one and under two (200 draw calls), no jump where it loops (`loop_steps` over 6 s); a time before 0 is the same place in the loop (6 s for PS3 and PS4); the six `want_frame` fingerprints of PS4 are new, the background (`want_scene`) is as it was. **New group `ps4`** (`gfx/ps4_test.c`, 19 checks; it includes `theme.c` and records the quads, `gfx_quad` is a macro for `rec_quad` while it is read): at 801 ages (0 to 8 s, every 0.01 s): no fold at any of 41 offsets from -56 to 24 (the smallest dot product is +169; the old table gave -446), no turn over 35 degrees (the worst is 27.7, in the table's own wiggle at 7.2 s; the old table gave 72), to the right the whole way and never steeply down, steps of 17 to 35 px, the lowest steps lean at most 35 degrees (the old ones 96), the lower end below the screen, the upper end off the screen, no point that moves more than 1 px in 0.01 s (the most is 0.38), the points are the curve through the keys worked out separately (uniform Catmull-Rom, the first and the last key held before and after: 0.0001 px at the most), the normals point to the right-hand side (never up) and neighbours turn at most 32 degrees; and for the cross-fade: 100 or 200 quads, two only in the last 2 s of a wave's life (200 of the 601 moments), the strength of the old wave and of the new one add up to 1 within 0.10 (the whole-number cut of each alpha takes 6% off in the middle: `(int)(alpha * strength)`), a wave on its own is at full strength (exactly), the last moment of the loop and the first are at the same place, nothing is lit at `a` = 0. `ps4` was added to the list of groups in `run_all.sh` (86 groups).
  - *Mutation checks.* **Mutation results** (`session-tools/ps4-2026-11-06/`: `genmut.py` makes the list, `fastmut6.py` runs it, resumable, the quick group `ps4` first and `theme` with `THEME_ANIM_ONLY=1` only for what gets past it). **352 changes of the new code** (every number from `PS4_LIFE` to the end of `deco_ps4()` by 1 and by 0.1, every comparison and logical operator turned round, every `+ - * /` swapped): **247 caught by `ps4`, 80 more by `theme`, 25 survive, and all 25 are changes that cannot show**: the guard that only a zero-length neighbour step can reach (`l < 0.001f` three ways and `l = 1` two ways: 5), the turn of a normal that no longer happens (`NY < 0` with `-1` and with `<=`: 2; the test asserts that every `NY` is above 0.02), an array one element larger (`OFF[6]`, `al[3][5]`, `al[2][6]`, `xy[9]`, `cl[5]`: 5), the alpha byte of `RGBA(10, 132, 255, 0)`, which `COLOR_ALPHA` replaces (2), `gfx_blend_add(2)` for 1 (the code tests for true), `t < 0` with `-1` and with `<=` (the same picture: a wave of age 6 s at full strength is the old wave at the seam), the choice of key where both branches give the same key (`k1 > PS4N_K - 3`, `>=`, `k1 > 1`, `k1 + 3 <`: 4), a floor of 1 or -1 for the rim brightness `AE` (never reached: the table's lowest value is 1 alpha, so the spline does not go below 1), and the two points below the picture at 1.6 / 3.1 steps instead of 1.5 / 3.0 (off the screen; the end is still below it). The first run of the same list (before the check against the separately worked out curve and the check of the normals) left 30: five were holes, the clamps of the place among the keys at the two ends of a wave's life (ages under 0.1 s and over 7.83 s, where a wave has less than 1% weight) and the floor of the band brightness `BB`, which the check of the curve closed; and the surviving `NX < -1` led to the third fault above. **Hand-made changes of the table** (`m_ps4_data.py`, 8): the old table put back (all of it, only the bottom points, only the top points, the bottom of key 25, the top of key 9) is caught by both groups every time; a middle point moved 15 px sideways is caught by `ps4` only (the picture test does not see it: that is what the group is for); 3 px sideways and 8 px in y survive (a turn under 35 degrees without a fold; the table's own wiggle is 28).
- **The PS1 symbols float smoothly** (`theme.c` `deco_ps1()`, `PS1_RING_N`; `gfx.c` / `gfx.h` `gfx_stroke_loop()` (new); on request: "make sure that the buttons in PS1 theme background are moving fluidly and a little more, they are moving by line one pixel now").
  - *What was wrong.* The cluster bobbed by `floor(sin(0.8 t) * 2 + 0.5)` pixels: whole pixels (the first version of the symbols was made on whole pixels for sharp lines), a step every second or so, which is a jerk and not a movement. A whole-pixel line cannot move by less than a pixel, and `gfx_ring()` and `gfx_frame()` (the circle and the square) are whole-pixel shapes by design (`shape_round()` and the 0.5 rule of `gfx_frame()`: a pixel belongs to the shape when its middle does), so they could not float either; the triangle and the cross were already `gfx_stroke()`s.
  - *The motion.* `u = loop_pos(time, LOOP_S)`, offset `(2 sin(4 pi u), 4 sin(2 pi u))` from `tsin()`: a figure of eight (a Lissajous 2:1), 4 px up and down (it was 2) and 2 px to each side (it had none), round in 8 s like the other themes (the old bob had no loop; the test that leaves PS1 out of "the same picture a loop earlier" is gone). At `u` = 0 the offset is (0, 0): the symbols are where the whole-pixel version had them, and the lines are sharp there; between, a line is as soft as an anti-aliased line moving by fractions of a pixel is. The largest speed is 3.1 px a second, 0.052 px a frame.
  - *`gfx_stroke_loop(xy, n, width, colour)`.* A closed outline with the ramps of `gfx_stroke()` (a cross-section at every point with the mitre of the two sides, the first once more to close it, three strips; no ends, so nothing is drawn twice where it closes: a colour with some alpha has the same alpha all the way round; with `gfx_stroke()` and its half-pixel caps the closing corner was drawn twice). A point that comes twice in a row and a last point that is the first again are dropped before anything is made (without it the closing corner had a sliver of extra light, found by the test); at least 3 points are needed, at most 128 are used (`STROKE_MAX`). A mitre is cut off at twice the half width (`max(cos, 0.5)`), as in `gfx_stroke()`: a triangle with a corner sharper than 60 degrees has less light than its length times its width. 3 draw calls and (n + 1) x 96 bytes of the list.
  - *The symbols.* The triangle is a loop of its three corners, the square a loop of its four corners 1 px inside its outside edge (`s - w / 2`, so that at rest the outside edge is where the whole-pixel frame had it), the circle a loop of 48 points at the radius `r - 0.95` and 1.9 thick (the old `ICON_RRING` was `0.19 r` thick, 1.9 px: its outside edge is at `r`; the sagitta of 48 points is 0.03 px), the cross two `gfx_stroke()`s as before; the glow is the same `gfx_glow()` at floats. The positions are floats now, `tsin()` makes the circle (96 table lookups a frame).
  - *The picture at rest.* The square's sharp left edge (two whole columns of one colour with none beside them at 343 and 346) is still true with the loop, which is how the new strokes were matched to the old shapes; the six pinned fingerprints of PS1 are new (`want_frame[9]`), the pictures of every other theme did not change (the PPSSPP pictures of PS2, PS3, PS4, the Default theme with its dust and Home with the games' pictures, 3 moments each, are byte for byte the same with the code before and after, taken with a fixed time step: see the entry below).
  - *The PC mock and the tests.* At the places of rest the edges of the triangles of a strip lie exactly through pixel middles and the mock then draws such a pixel twice (a real GE has a fill rule: top and left): a test that measures the picture at exactly `time` = 0 (or on any exact whole position) is off by one doubled row. The tests sample at half a frame past each frame and measure the straight edges of the square (the top edge in the column x = 352 and the left edge in the row y = 150, the five samples round the strongest, less the level of the soft glow just outside them): the edge is where the formula says to 0.12 px at worst (measured 0.095; what is left is the glow, which the GE puts on whole pixels), it moves by 0.12 px at most between two frames of 1/60 s (measured 0.080 where the formula says 0.052; a whole-pixel jerk is 1), it covers more than 7.8 px up and down and 3.8 sideways (measured 8.02 and 4.00), a line on a fraction of a pixel takes one row more than at rest, and the picture after 8 s is the one at 0 to the last bit; and the old checks (the cluster at the right, 60 to 80 px, clear of the lists, the cards and the bottom row; the square's sharp left edge) hold. The loop and the picture after a loop earlier are checked for PS1 too.
  - *Tests of the stroke* (private, `gfx/gfxtest.c` `test_stroke_loop()`, `GFXTEST_LOOP_ONLY=1`): 3 draw calls and (n + 1) x 96 bytes; across every side of a square there is colour times width, all along (1.0 for half of white two wide), on all four sides; the four corners of a square that is symmetric on the pixel grid are exactly the same, the closing one among them (with half see-through white a doubled corner would be 0.75, not 0.5); the light is the area of the line (56) at 20 fractions of a pixel (no pulsing as it moves); a line on a fraction takes three rows; a circle of 48 points has the light of its ring and the pixels at the radius 9 are lit and those at 5 are not, also with 96 points less than a pixel apart; the light of a triangle is its length times its width times the colour, the same at nine places and from each of its three corners, and with a point twice or the first point again at the end; a loop closed by its first point is drawn as exactly the same picture as without it; a sharp tip reaches a pixel above its point and not three; lines 0.6 and 0.4 wide have the light of four `gfx_stroke()`s; four points half a pixel apart are an outline; 200 points are cut to 128 (3 calls, 129 x 96 bytes); no texture; two points, none, a negative number and four on one spot draw nothing and ask for nothing; 300 points do not overflow (the address sanitizer).
  - *Mutation checks* (`session-tools/perf-2026-11-06/m_loop2.py` -> `m_loop_g.py`, `GFXTEST_LOOP_ONLY=1`; `m_ps1_t.py`, `TESTS=theme`). `gfx_stroke_loop()`: 55 changes, 52 caught; the 3 that survive are the same behaviour (the normal that a 180 degree turn takes is mirrored either way; `if` for `while` and `n > 2` for `n > 1` where the trailing point of the first loop can only be one). `deco_ps1()`: 43 changes (the distances, sizes, widths, alphas, glow, every point of the triangle, circle, cross and square, the period, every constant and phase of the motion, the sign of each axis, the number of symbols), all caught by the pinned pictures and the motion checks. (The first run of the stroke list caught 46 of 56: nothing looked at a loop closed by its first point, at the tip of a sharp corner, at the points less than a pixel apart or at the light of a triangle; what found the closing sliver was a test written for it.)
  - *Pictures.* The README has no picture of PS1 (`theme.png` is PS3). `session-tools/perf-2026-11-06/ps1_shots/` has the PPSSPP pictures of PS1 at six moments.

- **The shapes (disc, soft glow, ring) live in video memory, swizzled** (`gfx.c` `shape_to_vram()`, `SHAPE_VRAM`, `SHAPE_SLOT`, `gfx_init()`, `tex_free()`; `Texture.vram` (new field); on request: "the PS5 themes still dont run at full framerate in any menus ... PS1,2,3,4 themes run fluidly").
  - *What was left after the batching.* PS1 to PS4 are fluid and the Default and the nine cover colours are not, in every menu and most in Games: the only difference is the dust, which is now 5 draw calls and 470 little sprites (940 vertices, 18 600 pixels = 0.14 of a screen: **less fill than PS2, PS3 or PS4 have**: 30 000 to 32 000 pixels). So what is left is not the fill and not the calls (PS3 makes 301 of them): it is what a tiny textured sprite costs the GE: each shrinks the whole 64 x 64 shape (16 KB, in main memory, rows after rows) to 2 to 16 pixels, so with the filter it reads four texels a pixel spread over all of the shape (a 30:1 shrink), most of them not in the texture cache, from main memory. The same shapes are used by every card's shadow (`gfx_glow()`), the rings and the Library's specks. **That this is the cost is a guess nobody could measure on a PSP; it is what is different.**
  - *The change.* `gfx_init()` makes the three shapes as before, swizzles them (`tex_swizzle()`) and copies them into video memory behind the snapshot: slot k at `3 * FB_SIZE + k * 0x5000` (a swizzled shape is 72 rows of 256 bytes = 18 432; 61 440 of the 425 984 free bytes are used). The CPU reaches video memory through `sceGeEdramGetAddr()` (0x04000000, cached) and `tex_flush()` writes it back; the GE reads the same address (as it does the snapshot). `Texture.vram` says the pixels are not the texture's to free (`tex_free()`); `gfx_init()` frees the old structures first, so a sleep (which wipes video memory and calls `gfx_init()` again) makes the shapes again in the same places and leaks nothing (the old code leaked three textures a time). `s_snap` has a 0 for the new field.
  - *The mock* has a video memory now (`inc/pspge.h`: a weak 2 MB `g_mock_vram` and `sceGeEdramGetAddr()`; `mockge.c` treats only 0x04000000 to 0x04200000 as the snapshot's address: the old test `& 0xff000000` would have taken one host pointer in 256 for it) and records the texture it was given (`g_tex_ptr`, `g_tex_swz`).
  - *The picture is the same.* The pinned fingerprints of `theme_test.c` are unchanged (every theme alone, with its moving part at six moments, the faint dust, the Library). **PPSSPP: 24 pictures byte for byte the same with the code before and after** (Default, Cobalt Blue, PS1, PS2, PS3 and PS4 on Home at three moments with the theme's surface, Home with the games' pictures, the Library card and the Library at two moments). **To compare animated pictures the two builds must have the same time:** the clock of the animations is the PSP's system time, which PPSSPP derives from its emulated work, so two builds with a different start-up cost (here three shapes more to make in `gfx_init()`) are some milliseconds apart and show the dust and the ribbon at another phase (the first comparison came out with differences in every theme, even PS3, which does not use the shapes; a test copy of `main.c` with `dt = 1/60` made all 24 equal). Build the two test copies with that one line changed.
  - *Not done.* The dust could be fewer or smaller dots (not the same picture: a choice for the maintainer), or drawn at half the rate into a layer (needs a 522 KB layer in video memory: no room), or switched off in a setting. Mipmaps for the shapes (the GE would read a small level: not the same picture).
