  - *Tests* (private). `gfx/gfxtest.c` `test_shapes()` (`GFXTEST_SHAPES_ONLY=1`): the disc and the soft glow are bound at their slots in video memory and swizzled; the three slots fit and are behind the snapshot; each slot holds 64 rows and 8 below them that are copies of the last (the formula written out again); nothing past the ring's 72 rows; what they draw is there; after a wipe of video memory the shapes draw nothing (the test can see the loss), `gfx_init()` brings them back and the pictures are the same to the last bit, in the same places, and written back from the data cache where they lie; three `gfx_term()` and `gfx_init()` leave the soft glow where it was (the address sanitizer stops at a `free` of video memory).
  - *Mutation checks* (`session-tools/perf-2026-11-06/m_shapes.py` -> `m_shapes_g.py`, `fastmut4.py`, `TESTS=gfxtest MUTENV=GFXTEST_SHAPES_ONLY=1`): 30 changes, 23 caught; the 7 that survive cannot show: no check of the texture before `tex_swizzle()` (it answers 0 for none), `return NULL` for `return t` when the swizzle fails (it does not), `>=` for `>` and no check at all of the size against the slot (the shapes are 18 432 bytes of 20 480), the `free` of the old main-memory buffer left out and of the old structures in `gfx_init()` (leaks: the harness does not look for them), and the flag on `s_snap` (it is never freed).

- **The pictures of the cards and the background picture are swizzled** (`gfx.c` / `gfx.h` `tex_swizzle()` (new), `Texture.swizzled` (new field), `bind_filter()`, `tex_flush()`; `image.c` `tex_round_corners()` and `ntx_save()` refuse a swizzled texture; `art.c` `load_icon()` and `art_load_bg()` call it last; on request: "the library goes slower when there is many games in it. i dont think its a background problem. push everything and take a look at that").
  - *What was measured (PC mock, `session-tools/perf-2026-11-06/perf_menu.c`, `NOASAN=1` build, the mock's drawing wrapped away).* Nothing that the main thread does grows with the number of games: `ui_update` + `ui_draw` of the Library is the same 5 us (host) with 6, 25, 100, 400, 1500 and 4000 games, scrolling or not; opening the Library is 6 us at 4000 games (the view is made at the start); the draw calls of a Library frame are 42 with 6 games and **70 with 25 or more** (15 cards with the pictures ready, every card its own shadow and picture: 47 changes of texture, 16 KB of the list, 2 screens of pixels written) and stay 70 for 1500. So what grows with the games is how many cards are on the screen (up to 5 columns x 3 rows, 5 rows while it scrolls), then nothing. The real `loader_focus()` is O(n) a frame (a `memset` and a loop over the games; 4000 games are some 20 000 instructions, nothing) and was not in the harness (a stub).
  - *The suspect.* The cards' pictures are 144 x 80 in 8888 (46 KB), in main memory, row after row, drawn at 80 x 44 (shrunk 1.8 times, filtered): the GE reads them through its small texture cache and fetches a new line for every row it steps down; with 25 cards that is some 1.15 MB of texture a frame. The comment of `gfx_tex()` records that the same was found for the wide pictures (they are drawn in strips of 64 texels). A swizzled texture (blocks of 16 bytes by 8 rows, the blocks in rows) is read much faster from main memory, and gives the same picture. **This is the well-known remedy and a guess about this program: nobody measured the GE on a PSP.**
  - *The layout* is the standard one (the swizzle routine of the PSP homebrew examples, written out again in the test): block (bx, by) at `by * row * 8 + bx * 128` bytes, inside it the 8 rows of 16 bytes one after the other; `row` = `stride * bytes a pixel` (a multiple of 16 for every texture made by `tex_create()`: the stride is a multiple of 8 pixels). `sceGuTexMode(psm, 0, 0, 1)` tells the GE (`bind_filter()` passes `t->swizzled`), the buffer width of `sceGuTexImage()` stays the stride. **Rows below the picture:** the picture is padded to a whole number of blocks with copies of its last row, at least one row (`swizzled_rows()` = (h + 8) / 8 * 8: 88 for the 80 of an icon, 184 for the 182 of a background), exactly what the one extra row of a linear texture is, so the filter at the bottom edge (a weight of nothing at the last half texel) sees the same and never reads memory that is not the texture's. A swizzled texture is 4 KB bigger (7 more rows of an icon).
  - *Order of things.* The cache files (`.ico`, `.bg`) hold rows and stay as they are (`ntx_save()` refuses a swizzled texture); an icon is loaded, cut (`tex_round_corners()`, which refuses a swizzled one) and **then** swizzled, in the loader thread; `tex_flush()` writes back `swizzled_rows()` rows (the GE reads main memory: forgetting the write-back is the classic way to see garbage on a real PSP, and the mock now records it: `g_wb_ptr`, `g_wb_len`, `g_wb_calls` in `inc/pspkernel.h`). Nothing else reads the pixels of a card picture after the loader (only `image.c`, `gfx.c`; checked with `grep`). The Music tab's covers and the Library's snapshot (video memory) are not swizzled.
  - *The picture is exactly the same.* **PPSSPP (true GE emulation, built with `-DPSP5_TEST`): Home with the game's picture behind the cards at three moments, the Library card, the Library at two moments, `library.png` and `library-folders.png`: all 8 are byte for byte the same with the code before and after** (`cmp`). **The negative control** (a build that swizzles but tells the GE the texture is plain) gives pictures of striped, scrambled cards (`sw_shots/neg_lib_lib.png`): that is what a wrong layout on a real PSP would look like, and it shows the comparison can fail. The mock draws a swizzled texture to the last bit like a plain one (`mockge.c` reads it through the same formula: `sceGuTexMode(.., swizzled)`).
  - *Not measured:* how much faster a PSP draws these; whether the swizzled layout is read right by a real GE (PPSSPP is written from the hardware's behaviour, and every homebrew uses the same layout). If the cards look scrambled on a PSP the remedy is to take out the two calls of `tex_swizzle()` in `art.c` (the rest does nothing without them).
  - *Not done, and why.* Pictures in video memory (a 144 x 80 picture is 46 KB and only 425 984 bytes are free: nine pictures); pictures made at the card's size (80 x 44: 3 times less to read, but a different filter, so not the same picture); the card's shadow (a soft black nine-slice under every card, 5 000 pixels, 3 500 of which the opaque picture covers again) drawn without its middle, or all the shadows in one call (a few thousand pixels and 25 calls; the shadows of neighbours do not reach each other's pictures, but the pictures are already one call each); the loading of the pictures (below).
  - *What the loader does as you scroll (read, not measured):* one picture at a time, nearest to the card first, in a thread of priority 0x30 below the main thread's: a picture that has not been seen before is decoded from the game's file (an ISO is opened and read, a PNG decoded, scaled, and a cache file written to the Memory Stick), later ones are read from the cache (two failed opens, one read). Through a Library of hundreds of games the first time is slow in the sense of cards that stay empty for a moment, and the folder games' sizes are counted by another thread of the same priority (`gamesize.c`, only for the size sorts and Console Storage). Neither takes frames from the main thread, but they share the Memory Stick and the memory bus with the drawing.
  - *Tests* (private). `gfx/gfxtest.c` `test_swizzle()` (`GFXTEST_SWIZZLE_ONLY=1` runs only it): 17 sizes and both formats (the icon 144 x 80, the background 320 x 182 and 480 x 272 in 5650, tiny and odd ones, heights of 1, 7, 9, 12, 13, 50) against the standard swizzle routine, with copies of the last row below (also when the extra row of the plain texture holds something else); swizzling again changes nothing; the write-back is the whole new buffer, from its start, and the pixels start on 16 bytes; `tex_flush()` of a swizzled and of a plain texture writes back what it should; a row that is not a multiple of 16 bytes, no height and no row are refused and not a byte is touched; a swizzled texture is drawn as exactly the same picture as the plain one (8 places, sizes and tints, two textures one after the other, to the last bit; and a swizzled, a plain and a swizzled one are three copies). `art/art_test.c`: icons and backgrounds are swizzled (their pixels are read back through its own unswizzle formula and are the ones they were), the cache file is still read from, `tex_round_corners()` and `ntx_save()` leave a swizzled texture alone, the square-cornered picture is swizzled too. `cards/mask_part.inc` (the test's copy of `tex_round_corners()`) has the new guard.
  - *Mutation checks* (`session-tools/perf-2026-11-06/m_swizzle.py` -> `m_swizzle_g.py`, `fastmut4.py`, `TESTS=gfxtest MUTENV=GFXTEST_SWIZZLE_ONLY=1`; the art code in mutated copies of the repository with `run_all.sh --only art`). `gfx.c`: 44 changes, 41 caught (the first run caught 30: nothing looked at the write-back, the extra row, a row that is not a multiple of 16, no height or no row); the 3 that survive cannot show on the PC: no check of the texture pointer before its data (the row guard says no too), `memalign(8)` for `memalign(16)` (the PC's allocator aligns to 16 anyway) and a failed allocation answered with 1 instead of 0 (no way to make `memalign` fail here). `image.c` and `art.c`: 5 changes (either guard, either call of `tex_swizzle()`, swizzling before the corners are cut), all caught.
  - *Also made:* `perf_menu.c` got the scene `many` (`PERF_SCENE=many PERF_N=<games>`, with a `loader_icon()` that gives every game a ready 144 x 80 picture, `PERF_FRAMES=<n>` for the time of n frames idle and scrolling, `PERFOPEN` for opening the Library).

- **The dust and the Library's specks are drawn in batches** (`gfx.c` / `gfx.h` `gfx_dots()` and `GfxDot` (new), `theme.c` `DotBatch`, `batch_dot()`, `batch_flush()`, `deco_dust()`, `lib_dust()`, `tsin()`, `s_speck_col`; on request: "the PS5 particles in themes make menus run at low FPS, can you please optimize them without changing the looks ... also look at whether you can optimize the Library card menu, it seems it also doesnt run at full framerate").
  - *The cause (measured on the PC mock, read from the PSP SDK's own library).* Every `gfx_dot()` is a `sceGuGetMemory()` and a `sceGuDrawArray()`, and in `GU_DIRECT` mode (the list is built with `sceGuStart(GU_DIRECT, ...)`, as here) **both end in a call of `sceGeListUpdateStallAddr`**, a call into the kernel that tells the chip how far the list goes (read from the disassembly of `libpspgu.a`: `psp-objdump -dr sceGuGetMemory.o sceGuDrawArray.o`, both `jal`/`j` to it when `gu_curr_context` is 0). The Default theme's frame was 467 draw calls (so some 930 kernel calls), 74 changes of texture (the discs and their halos alternate between the two shapes, and a change is `sceGuTexImage` and `sceGuTexFlush`) and 22.7 KB of the list. The other themes: PS1 25 calls, PS2 25, PS3 301, PS4 101. The CPU's own work was not it: 135 000 instructions a frame, a third of them in `tsin()`; the pixels the dots cover are some 1% of the screen. Nothing of this could be measured on a PSP; it is the count of calls that was measured.
  - *The change.* `gfx_dots(const GfxDot *d, int n, int soft)` draws `n` dots in one `sceGuGetMemory()` and one `sceGuDrawArray()` (256 at most in a call, then another), in the order given, with the same two vertices as `gfx_dot()` (so exactly the same pixels). `theme.c` collects them in a `DotBatch` (a stack array of 256, 4 KB) and flushes it at the end of each kind. The dust is **three batches**: the specks with their halos (soft), then the soft discs (hard), then the discs' rims and the stars (soft), which is the old order except that a disc's rim is drawn after all discs instead of after its own: the adding mode (`GU_FIX`) is commutative (integer sums, clamped at the end), and the PC mock, which mixes by alpha and so would show a difference, gives the same pixels too: the fingerprints did not change. A frame is 5 draw calls (the picture of the surface, the specks in two calls since there are more than 256 of them with their halos, the discs, the rims and stars) and 4 changes of texture. `batch_dot()` leaves out a dot whose alpha is 0 (it adds nothing, and lays nothing over). `lib_dust()` (366 specks, laid over: the order matters and is kept) is two calls (256 and 110) instead of 366.
  - *Two small things in the same place.* `tsin()` cuts the fraction of a turn with a cast (`turns - (float)(int)turns`, one more turn for a negative one) instead of `floorf()` (a function call on the PSP): the values are the same to the last bit (see the test), `NaN`, infinity and anything beyond 2e9 (a float that large has no fraction) give 0 without a conversion. The 270 colours of the specks (`mix_rgb()` of the theme's dust and white) are made when the theme's dust colour is another and kept (`s_speck_col`). Together about 135 000 instructions a frame became 88 000 (callgrind, the mock's drawing left out).
  - *Numbers (PC mock, one frame).* Default theme: 467 draw calls / 74 binds / 22 752 B of the list -> 5 / 4 / 22 608 B (the vertices are the same 48 bytes a dot; the commands and the jumps in the list between the calls are gone). The Library's picture drawn again: about 480 calls -> 89. Drawing again the surface of a theme: 564 -> 102.
  - *The picture is exactly the same.* The 13 x 7 pinned fingerprints of `theme_test.c` and the Library's did not change; 280 values of `a` from 0.0035 to 0.07 (the dust fading in, where the batches have a few dots) are pinned to the old code's picture (`want_faint`); and in the PPSSPP (true adding) the same pictures were taken with the code before and after (`-DPSP5_TEST` builds, 9 pictures: Home at three moments in the Default and in Cobalt Blue, the Library card, the Library at two moments): all 9 are byte for byte the same (`cmp`).
  - *The Library card and the Library.* Measured with a copy of `menu_test.c` that counts the draw calls of one frame (`session-tools/perf-2026-11-06/perf_menu.c`): the Library card of Games is **32 draw calls and 6 KB** (12 of them the star's rays), the Library's grid 42, a game card without a picture (the theme's surface and its dust) was 490: the Library's own screens are the cheapest of all and nothing in them is made of hundreds of calls, except the moment the picture in video memory is drawn again: the Library's background and the theme's surface share one picture (there is no room for two: video memory has 425 984 bytes free and a picture is 557 056), so moving from a card whose background is the theme's surface (a game without a picture, or the art off) to the Library card, or back, draws the one that is not there. That was about 480 calls with the dust and is 89 now (the beam of the surface is 88 quads in as many calls: not batched, since the strip's triangles would be given in another order, and nothing says the last bit would be the same). **Nothing was found that is slow in every frame of the Library card or the Library: if it still stutters on a PSP, the next things to look at are the moments of change (the picture drawn again, the pictures of games being decoded by `loader.c`, which is a thread at priority 0x30 below the main thread's) and `gfx_snapshot_draw_soft()` of the commit before this one, which fills the screen with a filtered picture every frame (the same number of pixels as before, with a filter; nobody has measured what that costs on a PSP).**
  - *Not done, on purpose.* The two shapes (64 x 64, 8888, in main memory, shrunk to a few pixels without mipmaps) could live in video memory, which the chip reads faster; that is a guess nothing here can check. A vertex of 20 bytes (16-bit texture coordinates) would be 17% less list for the dots; the mock does not know the format. The four rays of the Library's star (12 calls) and its two dots could be one call each; a frame of the Library card is 32 calls anyway.
  - *Tests* (private). `gfx/gfxtest.c` `test_dots()` (`GFXTEST_DOTS_ONLY=1` runs only it): `gfx_dots()` is exactly what `n` calls of `gfx_dot()` draw, to the last bit, for 1, 40, 255, 256, 257, 513 and 601 dots and both shapes; one call and 48 bytes a dot up to 256 dots, then one more call for each 256; no dots (0, negative) draw nothing, ask for nothing and bind nothing; the last dot is on top, also over the end of a call; one bind, none for the same shape twice; the blend mode is not touched; the vertices themselves are read from the mock's pool (the corners, the colour in both, no depth) and the sprites are counted. `gfx/theme_test.c`: the whole background is 3 to 6 calls and at most 4 changes of texture in every dust theme; a dust too faint for any alpha is only the picture of the surface (1 call) and comes back with `a`; the Library's picture is drawn again in 40 to 150 calls; the faint-dust fingerprint above. `gfx/tsin_test.c` (new, in `run_all.sh` as `tsin`; it `#include`s `theme.c` to reach the static function): the new `tsin()` equals the one with `floorf()` bit for bit for 69 000 turns from -60 to 60 and for 46 special ones (whole turns and next to them, tiny, 2500.25, 1e6, 8388608, 1e10, 3.4e38), within 0.0003 of `sinf`, 0 for `NaN` and infinity (built with `float-cast-overflow` fatal).
  - *Mutation checks* (`session-tools/perf-2026-11-06/fastmut4.py`, lists `m_dots_g.py` and `m_dots_t.py`). `gfx_dots()`: 50 changes, 49 caught; the one that survives (`n <= GFX_DOTS_MAX`) is the same function. The batch helper, the dust, the Library specks, `tsin()` and the colours: 62 changes, 51 caught after the tests above were added (the first run caught 46: the sine of negative and large turns was not looked at, the vertices not at all); the 11 that survive cannot show: the guard of `batch_flush()` that `gfx_dots()` repeats (2), flushing at 255 dots instead of 256 (the same calls and pixels), the three ways to make the speck colours again every frame (slower, not different: the colour cache cannot be seen in the picture), the limits of `tsin()` beyond which a float has no fraction (3: 1e9, and 2e9 either side; and a turn that is exactly 0, whose sine is 0), `f <= 0` for `f < 0` (a fraction of exactly 0 becomes 1, which gives 0 too), and one dot of alpha 1 in a batch of one that is left out (it adds one step of colour at most, and no fingerprint sees it).
  - *Also made:* `session-tools/perf-2026-11-06/` (the meters: `perf_theme.c` draw calls, binds and bytes of one frame of a theme; `perf_cpu.c` the time of building the list with the mock's drawing wrapped away; `perf_menu.c` + `perf_wrap.c` the draw calls of the Library card, a game card and the Library, and who makes them (`who_*.txt`, addresses for `addr2line`); `build_perf.sh`, `build_menu.sh`). The mock got `g_pixels` (pixels written) next to its other counters.

- **The Library's background is softer** (`gfx.c` / `gfx.h` `gfx_snapshot_draw_soft()` (new), `theme.c` `scene_ready()` and `lib_star()`; on request: "fix the Library background? it's too sharp on the edges, make the edges render softer so they dont look pixelated").
  - *What was wrong.* The fine lines of the picture (the 19 hairs of the petal, 24 points each, the nine rings, the fan, the sweeps) are `gfx_polyline()`: hardware lines one pixel wide (`GU_LINE_STRIP` has no anti-aliasing, so a diagonal line is a staircase and a faint ring is a dotted one), and so were the four rays of the star. The rims were `gfx_stroke()` and already soft.
  - *The options.* Soft strips for every line cost 64 bytes (one textured quad a segment) to 96 bytes (`gfx_stroke()`) a point; the picture has about 1000 points, so 70 to 100 KB of the 128 KB display list (the picture takes 42 KB now), and fewer points would show corners. The picture is made once into a snapshot, so reading the *finished* picture smoothly costs nothing.
  - *What it does.* `gfx_snapshot_draw_soft()` shows the snapshot with a linear filter, every sprite half a pixel down and to the right: each pixel is the mean of 2 x 2 texels, a line one pixel wide is two pixels of half strength and the steps go. It reads only texels of the picture: the sprites cover columns 1 to 479 and rows 1 to 271 (u, v are the pixel's own number, so the mean is of the texel before it and its own), and the first column and row are drawn exactly by two more sprites. 8 strips of 64 pixels and the two edges are 2 draw calls and 480 bytes (the exact `gfx_snapshot_draw()` is 1 call and 384). Only the Library uses it (`scene_ready(t, a, library)`): the surface and the backgrounds of the themes are exactly as before. The star is drawn every frame on top of it, so its rays are `gfx_stroke(..., 1.0f)` now (three strips each, about 2 KB a frame; the star is 14 draw calls, 2 dots and 4 x 3 strips).
  - *Pitfall (PPSSPP).* The first version moved the *texture coordinates* by half a texel: the PC mock blurs with that, and PPSSPP does not (a sprite drawn one texel to a pixel is read as it is, whatever its coordinates say; the screenshot came out byte for byte the old one). Moving the *position* of the sprite by half a pixel blurs in both. Nobody has seen either on a real PSP.
  - *Numbers.* In the rings at the lower right (nothing but the rings and a few specks of dust) a pixel that stands out from the mean of its four neighbours by more than 12 of 255: 620 before, 26 now (the worst one 88, now 17); in the petal and the fan 1185, now 286 (the bright rims stay). The picture is a little dimmer, since a line of one pixel is now two pixels of half strength.
  - *Tests* (private). `gfx/gfxtest.c` `test_snapshot_soft()` (`GFXTEST_SNAPSHOT_ONLY=1` runs only it): the exact picture is exact in every channel; a vertical line, a horizontal one and a single pixel become two pixels, two rows and four pixels of half and a quarter strength, one pixel down and to the right; no light is lost; the first column and row are as they were to the pixel (the ends are where a wrong coordinate shows), the last column is read like a line; a flat picture is flat to every edge (nothing outside the picture is read); 2 draw calls and exactly 480 bytes (384 and 1 call for the exact one). `gfx/theme_test.c` (848 checks): in the rings at most 100 pixels stand out by more than 12 (worst at most 40), in the petal at most 600, in all 13 themes; the second frame is 16 draw calls (the soft picture 2 and the star 14); the 13 Library fingerprints and the 3 exact ones are new.
  - *Mutation checks.* 109 changes of `gfx_snapshot_draw()` and `gfx_snapshot_draw_soft()` (`fastmut3.py`, `TESTS=gfxtest`): 69 caught; the 40 that survive cannot show: how the picture is cut into strips (any width or number of 64 or more, with or without an overlap, gives the same pixels: 20), the z of a vertex and the alpha of the white (ignored in through mode, with blending off: 12), the index pointer of `sceGuDrawArray()` (unused), a vertex count that is odd or has more than the strips (the mock draws pairs and an unwritten vertex is a sprite with no area), the end of the first column's u and the end of the first row's v (one texel row or column either way), and `edge = v + 3 * n` (past the memory that was asked for, which the mock's pool hides). 4 hand-made changes of the choice in `scene_ready()` (the Library soft and the surface exact): all caught. 41 changes of `lib_star()`: 33 caught; the 8 that survive are a flag 1 for 2 (`blend_add`, `soft`), arrays one larger, and a colour channel 255 for 254 in the core of the star, which the full brightness hides.
  - *The pictures of the README:* `library.png` and `library-folders.png` were taken again with a `-DPSP5_TEST` build (`scripts/fb.txt`, `fb2.txt`); the rest of the screens are unchanged.

- **The themes move like the maintainer's reference animations, and every theme has the same background** (`theme.c`, the section *The moving shapes* (new), `themedata.h` (new, header only), `theme.h`; on request, with four animated pictures of the console's own themes (PS2, PS3, PS4, PS5; the first stills were not enough, so a zip with the animations followed) and, after three rounds of look-and-correct: "the PS5 [animation] for every theme except PS1/2/3/4 (rename PS5 theme to Default)", "PS1 ... make the buttons that render on the background bigger and a bit closer to the center ... and make them full pixel", "PS2 for PS2, PS3 for PS3, PS4 for PS4", and "for the source background (the beam and stuff) please use the same source background render for every theme, just change their color configuration properly").
  - *How it was made.* Prototypes of all four were drawn on the PC first (a copy of `theme.c` and the real `gfx.c` on the mock graphics chip; frames side by side with the animations, 120 frames at 15 a second) and measured against the animations before anything went into `theme.c`: blobs and ridges were tracked frame by frame (numpy; no scipy), the curves smoothed and reduced to a few numbers. The maintainer corrected them in rounds: the dust first along a curve, then in the wide area that was drawn on a picture (`DUST_TOP` / `DUST_BOT`: its top and bottom edge every 30 px); PS4 had only the bright part of the wave (the whole wave, no hook); PS3 smoother and lighter, then "make the corners of the waves less pixelated" (the hard edges and the one-pixel lines) and "remove the particles"; PS2 first without the circle and then "the dots dont move in the circular motion at all while the original ... go in circular motion back and forth" (the tumbling ring). The numbers of the final pictures were checked against the prototypes: the real code draws the same frames (mean difference under 0.01 of 255).
  - *The code.* `tsin(turns)` is a sine from a 1024-step table (angles in turns: the dust alone is about 1000 sines a frame); `loop_pos()` is the place in a loop, 0 to 1 (everything goes a whole number of turns in the loop, so the last frame is followed by the first: `LOOP_S` = 8 s; the ribbon of PS3 has `PS3F_PERIOD` = 6 s). `theme_backdrop()` draws the surface from the picture in video memory (as before, the same for every theme) and then calls `deco_dust()` (`DECO_SPOT`: the Default and the nine colours), `deco_ps1()`, `deco_ps2()`, `deco_ps3()` or `deco_ps4()` with `a` limited to 0-1. The old `deco_ps2()` (towers and orbs), `deco_ps3()`, `deco_ps4()`, `wave()` and the dust of `deco_spot()` are gone; `deco_spot()` draws the beam and the haze and nothing else. The first theme is called `Default` (its code `ps5` is what `psp5.cfg` has, so nothing in a config file changes).
    - *Dust.* 270 specks (a bright point, the larger ones with a halo), 36 soft discs and 14 stars (`Dust`, 14 KB in all, made once from the generator `spot_rnd` with the seed `0x9d3c`; the rejection sampling `dust_place()` keeps them in the area, thicker where `DUST_THICK` says, 25% of the discs and 10% of the specks may stray 25 px out of it). Each drifts 1.4-3.6 px on a path of 1-3 turns in the loop and twinkles 1-4 times (`powf` is not used: `tw*(0.7+0.3*tw)` for the specks and `tw*(0.8+0.2*tw)` for the discs are close to `tw^1.3` and `tw^1.2`). Cost: 22752 bytes of the display list in a steady frame, the first frame of a theme (which makes the surface picture) about 8 KB more. The dust of the old picture was drawn once and cost nothing per frame.
    - *PS1.* `PS1_X` = 376, `PS1_Y` = 150 (was 418, 130); the distances 24, the radius 10, half the side 8, lines 2 px; the bob was `floor(sin(0.8 t) * 2 + 0.5)`, whole pixels (it floats smoothly now: see the first entry of this section; the triangle's base is at y+7; everything is `int` until the call). 4480 bytes.
    - *PS2.* Eight dots, each `th0` (turns) + 3 turns in the loop + a wandering of `wob` (1-3 turns in the loop), on a ring of radius `R` ≈ 55 px that tumbles about a fixed axis (158 degrees on the screen): the dot is at `(R cos th, R sin th cos(tilt))` along and across the axis, and `z = sin(th) sin(tilt)` makes the near ones a little bigger and brighter. `ps2_tilt()` is pi/2 (edge-on) at 1.33, 3.07, 4.80 and 6.55 s (measured: the ratio of the short to the long axis of the dots in the animation fell to 0.06 at those times, and the apparent speed of the dots, steady at about 2.5 rad/s, changed sign after each of them), a smooth Hermite curve with four half turns in the loop. 1536 bytes.
    - *PS3.* Three curves (the upper edge, the lower edge and the bright strand) at 51 points, each a sum of 7 cosines along the screen times a series in time (a constant and 5 sine/cosine pairs of the 6 s period): 3 x 11 x 7 numbers in `themedata.h` instead of 3 x 30 x 26 measured. The animation jumps where it loops, so its last 2 s were blended into its first 2 s (`smoothstep`) before the fit: the first version (8 s, no blend) sped the ribbon up three to four times near the seam. The silk is made of quads with their own brightness at each corner (rows: feathered edge, body up to 1.5 px from the middle of the strand, strand, body, feathered edge) at every second point, the strand at every point (its edges fade to 0 and the silk reaches under them: when the strand's edge had the silk's brightness, a row of bright dots showed along it in PPSSPP, where the edges of two strips that are not quite the same overlap or leave a pixel out; the PC mock does not show it), two threads as strips 3.6 px wide (a hardware line is stepped); the strand and the edges are kept inside each other with `soft_max()` (no corner where they meet). 19584 bytes. Cream (`RGBA(255, 236, 205)`) as the theme's other warm light; one line to make it grey-white like the animation.
    - *PS4.* 24 points along the wave from the bottom of the screen to the top right corner at 30 keys of the 8 s (every 4th frame of the animation, Catmull-Rom between them, so the motion is smooth), traced by dynamic programming along the diagonal `x - y` through the animation's frames (the wave is nearly constant in `x + y`) and the brightness of the rim (`ae`) and of the band at its left (`bb`) measured across the wave. They are packed: `short` tenths of a pixel for x and y, `unsigned char` alpha times two for `ae` and `bb` (4 KB). Drawn as 4 quads across the wave per segment (feathered band, rim, fall-off), the colour `RGBA(10, 132, 255)` (the animation's rim is 0, 31, 59 brighter than its inside); it fades in over 0.5 s and out over 0.9 s so nothing jumps at the seam. 6784 bytes.
  - *One background for every theme.* `GLOW_A` = 95, `HAZE_A` = 80 and `SHEEN_A` = 230 are the alphas of the lamp's glow, the haze and the second light in every row of `THEMES[]`; the colours are the theme's. The nine colour themes were given new colours for those strengths by a private fitter (`session-tools/themefit2/fit.c`: it includes the new `theme.c`, draws `draw_scene()` on the mock and moves the glow, haze and sheen colours by coordinate descent until the picture is as close as possible to the old picture of the same theme, rendered by `old_theme.c`): the difference to the old picture is a root mean square of 0.17 to 0.24 of 255, Chroma Teal (with its sheen) 0.64. For PS1 to PS4 the aim was the four corners of the real themes' pictures (the numbers of `theme_test.c`) and the lamp colours the tests ask for, with a little weight on being close to the old look; PS1: glow (255, 255, 220), haze (73, 74, 65), beam (255, 255, 221); PS2: sheen black, glow (45, 36, 255), haze (116, 112, 255), beam (203, 226, 255) and `surf_top` / `surf_bottom` (1, 0, 58) / (0, 0, 6); PS3: glow white, haze (163, 155, 139), beam (255, 255, 219), surface (24, 21, 17) / (0, 0, 1); PS4: glow (229, 220, 255), haze (171, 255, 255), sheen (26, 10, 59), beam (227, 255, 255), surface (20, 41, 93) / (22, 6, 56). The mean colour of the four corners is now within 1.6 (PS1), 6.7 (PS2), 5.0 (PS3) and 7.9 (PS4) of the pictures' (the test asks 12). `intro`, `accent` and `dust` were not touched, so the start-up animation and the Library's colours (which come from `beam`, `dust`/`accent` and the surface) changed only for the four themes whose beam or surface changed.
  - *Tests* (private, `gfx/theme_test.c`, now 822 checks; the group takes about a minute). New: all 13 themes have the same alphas and the background is made in the same number of draw calls (one more for a theme with a second light), and from nothing but the colours (name, code, `intro`, `accent` change nothing); pictures pinned to the last bit (`want_scene` for the 13 backgrounds and `want_frame` for every theme at 0.3, 1.0, 2.6, 4.7, 7.1 and 7.9 s: the start of the loop, the middle and the end where the PS4 wave fades; `THEME_PRINT_HASH=1` prints new values); what moves is drawn in the adding mode and PS1's symbols by alpha (the mock does not mix by mode, so this is counted: `g_draws_add`), and the mode is the usual one when the frame is done; a time before 0 gives the picture of the same place in the loop; the dust (still without it, moves, stays in its area, 15-26 KB, loops in 8 s, takes the theme's colour, fades with `a`); PS1 (centre, size, clear of the lists, a sharp square edge two whole columns wide, the top edge two rows of one colour at every moment of the bob); PS2 (edge-on at the measured times, open between them, 8 separate dots, exactly 24 additive draw calls, loops); PS3 (no isolated bright pixel, nothing outside its band of the screen, the first lit pixel of a column is faint, loops in 6 s, the seam is not a speed-up); PS4 (not there at 0 s, never goes back down towards the right (no hook), reaches both ends, loops, 3-12 KB); and the step from the last frame to the first is no bigger than a normal step for the dust, PS2, PS3 and PS4. Changed: the first theme's name (`config_test.c`, `settings_test.c`, `boot_test.c`), the `Appearance` frame of `settings_test.c` (the list says Default), the Library fingerprints of PS1-PS4. `THEME_ANIM_ONLY=1` runs just the checks above (7 s) for the mutation runs; the mock mixes additive drawing by alpha, so the colour of "what a shape adds" is judged with that in mind.
  - *Mutation checks.* 1016 changes of the shapes (from `LOOP_S` to the end of `deco_ps4()`: every number by 1 and by 0.1, every comparison and logical operator; `fastmut2.py` with `THEME_ANIM_ONLY=1`). The first run left 165. A count of the draw calls made in the adding mode (a mutant that drew the dust, the ribbon or the wave by alpha survived), the check that the mode is the usual one again when the frame is done, pictures at six moments instead of two (the fade of PS4, the tilt of PS2 between its edge-on moments) and a time before 0 killed 19 of them; of the 147 that were run again, 146 survive. They are equivalents, or changes that no pixel can show: arrays one element larger than needed (about thirty); a flag 1 for 2 (`soft`, `gfx_blend_add()`: the code only tests for true); the alpha 0 of an `RGBA()` that `COLOR_ALPHA` replaces; `*_ready` 1 for 2 (and 0, which only makes the same table again every frame); `<` for `<=` and `>` for `>=` where the two values are never equal (`t <= 0`, which is the same place in the loop; `size > 1.0f`); the disc loop that goes one more (`i <= DUST_SPECKS + DUST_DISCS`: it is the first star, whose size is 0); a guard that only a NaN or a normal of length 0 can reach (the `return 0` of `tsin()`, `l < 0.001f`); the first index of the table of sines (sin 0 = 0); the key indices of PS4 that give the same key at the ends (`k1 > 1 ? ... : 0`); the margin 3.0 of `soft_max()` (not active); `R[j + 1]` for `R[j]` in the strand (less than one step of alpha); `* 2.99f` for `* 3.0f` (only a random number of exactly 1 differs); and the steps of 1 px and 0.1 in the tables of the dust area (`DUST_X`, `DUST_THICK`: with the fixed random numbers no particle moves). 629 changes of the rows of `THEMES[]` and the three strengths: 457 caught (every colour that was changed in this work, the strengths and the names); the 172 that survive are the dark base of the start-up (`intro`) and the `accent`, which this work did not touch, a zero alpha that is replaced, and `clampf()`'s `<=`. The 4 changes of `theme_backdrop()`: all caught (the last one, `a` limited to 2 instead of 1, by the new check that `a = 1.5` draws what moves as `a = 1` does).
  - *Not done / to know.* None of this has been seen on a real PSP; the cost is measured on the mock (display-list bytes, draw calls), not in milliseconds. The theme names (also *Default*) are product names and stay in English in every language. The animations' phase is whatever `time` the screen passes (`ui->time`, `S.time`, ...), so moving between screens can shift the dust a few pixels. The preview swatches in Appearance are still still pictures (the classic themes have a small wave line).

- **The theme is the background only: one look for bars, switches, ticks, hint buttons and cards** (`look.h` (new, header only), and every screen that drew one of them; on request, with five of the maintainer's pictures of the console's own screens ("card color", "bar colors", "tick", "toggle color", "hint icon colors") and two more that came while it was made (the empty tick and the switch that is off): "stop accent colouring, keep the background colors"). *Before:* the accent of the theme (`Theme.accent`) coloured the switches (`theme_toggle_on()`: 63 % of it), the bars (File Manager, Manage Saved Data, the progress panel, the music position), the marked row (alpha 46) and the round tick, the three dots of the Loading screens and the strip of the previews of Settings > Appearance; the card colour of a theme (`Theme.card`) coloured every card that has no picture; the hints had a black capsule behind them (`RGBA(0, 0, 0, 110)`) and a disc `RGBA(236, 236, 240)` with a `RGBA(20, 20, 26)` mark. *Now:* `Theme.card` and `theme_toggle_on()` are gone, `Theme.accent` is only the warm light of the backdrop of the old consoles and the dust of the start-up animation (`theme.c`, `boot.c`), and nothing on top of the background reads it.
  - *The colours* were measured in the pictures (a pixel of the picture over its background gives the alpha of a white overlay): bar `LOOK_BAR_TRACK` white at alpha 38 (a trace of white), `LOOK_BAR_FILL` 177 179 188; switch on `LOOK_SWITCH_ON` 76 78 86 with a knob `LOOK_KNOB_ON` 203 205 209, switch off `LOOK_SWITCH_OFF` white at alpha 32 with a knob that is only a ring `LOOK_KNOB_OFF` 142 144 153 (`gfx_ring(x + 2, y + 2, 10, 10, 5, ...)`; the track shows through it); tick `LOOK_TICK_FILL` white at alpha 42, `LOOK_TICK_EDGE` white at alpha 31 (a ring on top), `LOOK_TICK_MARK` white, 14 x 14, radius 3.5, the box is the same ticked or not and only the check mark differs; hint button `LOOK_HINT_PLATE` 191 191 195 and `LOOK_HINT_MARK` 64 64 69; card `LOOK_CARD` 14 17 21 (the picture's face goes from 12 14 18 at the top to 21 25 29 at the bottom: one colour in between was chosen). The off switch is a design of this change: the on colour of the picture (76 78 86) is nearly the old off track (74 76 84), so the two states are told apart by the knob (a disc at the right, a ring at the left) and the track.
  - *The code:* `look.h` has the colours and four static inline functions that only call `gfx_*`: `look_bar()` / `look_bar_color()` (the background `gfx_round_rect` and the part that is done as `gfx_bar_fill()`: the left end round, the end straight), `look_switch()`, `look_tick()` and `look_fade()`. Header only on purpose: no new object for the Makefile and for the link line of every host test, and a test that wraps `gfx_round_rect` / `gfx_bar_fill` / `gfx_ring` sees the calls. Users: `files.c` and `saves.c` (the free-space bar, the ticks, the hints, the card of the picture place), `popup.c` (`popup_progress()` lost its `accent` parameter), `ftpscreen.c` (the bar, the hints), `main.c` (the USB hint), `settings.c` (the switches, the glyphs of the Controls rows and of the choice popup, the Console Storage bar's background; its fill keeps the colours of what is on the storage), `ui.c` (the switch of Keep in Home, the music position bar, the dots, the cards), `info.c` (the picture place), `osd.c` (the volume bar: `look_bar()` too, so it has a straight end now), `theme.c` (the strip of `theme_swatch()` is white). The black capsule behind the hints is gone from `files.c`, `saves.c`, `ftpscreen.c` and `main.c`. The Home screen's own pills (Play, the three dots, the time played) are dark translucent pills and were not touched.
  - *A marked row is not highlighted* (the accent row fill is gone from `draw_list()` of `files.c` and `saves.c`); the rows that are not marked now show the empty box (`look_tick(..., 0, ...)`) instead of a ring: the check mark was the only thing that changed.
  - *Tests* (private). New group **`look`** (`look/look_test.c`, 63 checks: the constants; `look_bar()` pixel by pixel in all 13 themes, the round left end, the straight end, nothing beyond it, a fade; `look_switch()` on and off in every theme, the ring, the size 26 x 14; `look_tick()` on whole pixels, round corners, the fill reaching into the corners (a fill of radius 7 is caught), the white check mark, the same box without it, a fade; `gfx_button()` with the plate and mark) and new source check **`looksrc`** (`look/source_check.py`: the volume bar and the USB hint cannot be drawn on the PC, so it reads `osd.c` and `main.c`; no capsule behind a hint, every `gfx_button` with `LOOK_HINT_*`, nothing but `theme.c` / `boot.c` reads `->accent`, `->card` or `theme_toggle_on`, `look_*` and `LOOK_CARD` are used where they should be, a mark does not move the highlight). Changed: `ui/popup_sig.h` (`SigBtn` has the plate and mark, `sig_buttons_off_look()`, `sig_progress_bar()`), `files/popup_test.c` (669 checks: the hints' colours, nothing behind them, the tick of a marked row against the same row unmarked with another row marked so that the rows keep their place, the free-space bar), `saves/saves_test.c` (1499), `ui/menu_test.c` (1150: the switch of Keep in Home in every theme, off and on; the music position bar in every theme and its length; the dots of both Loading screens), `ui/library_test.c` (2456), `ui/stubs.c` (the music stub has a length, a position and a scanning flag), `ftp/ftpscreen_test.c` (142; the golden file `ftp/ftpscreen_golden.txt` was written again: 6906 lines of 7029, all of them capsules, buttons and bars), `settings/settings_test.c` (the frame hashes of the pages with a switch, a glyph or a bar, written again twice: the first time for the grey switch, the second for the ring), `gfx/theme_test.c` (504: no card colour, the strip of the previews is white in every theme). *Mutation checks* (34 hand-made changes, all caught): the bar's fill colour, a round end, the track alpha; the knob of the switch off as a disc, the knob on at the left; the tick without a fill, a fill of radius 7 (it got through at first: the corner pixel was covered by the ring; the check now looks at pixels the fill alone reaches), the check mark on an unticked box; the marked row highlight back in the File Manager and in Manage Saved Data, a mark that moves the highlight (twice), a capsule back behind the hints (File Manager, Manage Saved Data, FTP, USB), hints in the old colours (two places), the old rings, the free-space bar and the progress panel in the old shapes, the switch of Keep in Home and of Settings inverted, the music bar without a fill, the dots and a card in an old colour (four), the Console Storage track, the strip of the previews, the volume bar, the card colour of Information and of Manage Saved Data.
  - *The pictures of the README* were taken again (`file-manager`, `file-manager-conflict`, `saved-data`, `games-and-apps`, `settings-screen-sound`, `settings-controls`, `settings-appearance`, `options`, `install-plugin`, `console-storage`, `music`, `library`, `library-folders`, `ftp-server`): the scripts `sc1`..`sc6` of the screenshot kit (Settings > Screen and Sound, Controls, Storage > File Manager with marks, Manage Saved Data, Games and Apps, the options popup with Keep in Home on) were written because the old kit scripts (`fa.txt`, `fc.txt`) no longer reach the same screens (the menus have moved). The Library picture's selected card changed from the third to the fourth of the first row ("press right" three times from the first card, which the old picture did not show): the script is not exact about frames.

- **The plate of the type tag and the outline of the battery are opaque** (`ui.c`, the two `gfx_round_rect` of the tag: `with_alpha(LIGHT, 0.92f * ...)` became `with_alpha(LIGHT, ...)`; `draw_battery()`: the terminal and the body `0.85f * a` became `a`; on request, after "is the icon next to the name ... darker than the clock next to time played": the plate came out 219 219 225 in the picture, the clock face 236 236 240). *Text is not affected:* the titles, `Play` and the time are `TEXT_MAIN` (242 242 246, a little brighter than `LIGHT` 238 238 242) at full alpha when the screen is at rest; the picture's brightest pixels read 255 because the kit shrinks the screenshot with LANCZOS (overshoot), so the measure is the common values of the glyphs (241 241 245 for the title). Other light plates that are a little see-through, **not changed on purpose** (the music player is to be reworked): the selected row of the Music list (0.93) and the pause disc under the song's position (0.95). *Tests:* `ui/menu_test.c` (the plate of the Library's tag is at least 236 at x 27, y 243; the outline of the battery at least 232 at the top of the body and at the terminal), `look/source_check.py` (no `with_alpha(LIGHT, 0.92f` or `0.85f` in `ui.c`); 4 mutation checks, all caught.
- **A mark does not move the highlight; the top row of the Library is one size and one colour** (`files.c` `update()`, `saves.c` `mark_highlighted()`, `ui.c` `lib_header()` / `draw_library()`, `saves.c` `draw_header()`; on request: "make marking an item ... not automatically move your selection one row lower, just mark the row", and "make the text on the left in Library ... be the same size as the text for sorting by ... the sorting by text the same color as the text on the left ... Dont make it both gray!"). The two `s_sel++` / `S.sel++` after `marked ^= 1` are gone (□ marks, ↓ moves). In the Library the view text, the colon and the number of cards (*All: 14*) are `SORT_SIZE` (0.56; the text was 0.66 and the number 0.6, which the width of the number, the gap and `text_fit()` all used) and `TEXT_MAIN` (the number was `TEXT_DIM`), and the Sort text is `TEXT_MAIN` always (it was `TEXT_DIM` unless the button was chosen: `sort_shown > 0.5f`); in Manage Saved Data and Games and Apps the Sort text is `TEXT_MAIN` too (`on > 0.5f ? TEXT_MAIN : TEXT_DIM` before), and `SORT_TEXT_SIZE` (0.56, as the Library's `SORT_SIZE`; it was 0.5; it shrinks to 0.4 when the line is long, as before). *Tests:* `files/popup_test.c` (□ marks and the highlight stays, □ again unmarks on the same row; two marks are now ↓ between), `saves/saves_test.c` (the same for a save and for games; the Sort text is white chosen or not, in 11 languages), `ui/library_test.c` (the text and the number are 0.56 or less, the number as large as the Sort text, the view text and the Sort text the same white `242 242 246`, in 11 languages and four views). 5 mutation checks, all caught.

- **The transfer bars are drawn like the storage bars** (`popup.c` `popup_progress()`, `ftpscreen.c` `draw_running()`; on request: "make both bars show colors the way that other bars show them (so inherit them from the theme, with the same background and the same sharp end of the progress, not the circular one)", and for USB mode "Don't do the USB mode then, also make sure that the transfer bars in file manager follow the same style as well"). *Before:* the FTP bar was `gfx_round_rect` in a fixed green (`RGBA(120, 220, 150, 255)`; the unknown-size bar at alpha 110) with a round end, and the panel of a copy, move or delete had a round end too (in the theme's accent). *Now:* both draw the background as before (`gfx_round_rect`, white at alpha 40) and the done part with `gfx_bar_fill()` (the left end round, the end of the progress a straight edge at x + w; the same call as the free-space bar of the File Manager, Manage Saved Data and Console Storage) in `theme_cur()->accent`; the FTP bar is 260 x 4 (radius 2, at least 4 px once something is done), the panel's bar 268 x 6 (radius 3). A file that comes in over FTP (`total` 0) is the whole bar at 45 % of the alpha of the fill. (Changed the same day: the colour is the light grey of `look.h`, not the theme's accent: see *The theme is the background only* above.) The round dot on the FTP screen stays green when a computer is connected (it is a sign, not a bar). The three panels that use `popup_progress()` (File Manager, Manage Saved Data, game delete) pass `theme_cur()->accent`. *USB Mode has no bar, on purpose:* the PSP's USB storage driver gives the card to the computer (`sys_usb_start()` loads `usbstor*.prx`; nothing in psp5 touches the card while it runs), and there is no call that says which file moves or how many bytes: only `sceUsbGetState()` (cable, activated, connection established). *Tests* (private): `ftp/ftpscreen_test.c` (142 checks; the fill is a `gfx_bar_fill` and the background the white at alpha 40, the colour of every theme, the faint full bar, the edges; the golden file `ftp/ftpscreen_golden.txt` was written again: 77 lines changed, all of them the bar), `files/popup_test.c` and `saves/saves_test.c` (`sig_progress_bar()` in `ui/popup_sig.h`: one `gfx_bar_fill` in the panel, 16 px in and 54 below its top, 6 high, radius 3, in the theme's colour, 30 % of 268 px; the free-space bar behind the File Manager's panel is not counted). The game delete panel cannot be checked that way in `ui/menu_test.c` (the demo game is "already gone": nothing is counted, so no bar is drawn); its call is the same as the other two. *Mutation checks* (12 changes of `popup.c` and `ftpscreen.c`: round fill, a fixed colour, another theme colour, the place, the radius, the background alpha, the 4 px minimum, no clamp at 100 %, the faint bar solid or round): all 12 caught. *Picture:* `docs/screenshots/ftp-server.png` was taken again (only the bar differs; a scratch build that opens the screen with a made-up transfer, not part of the repository).

- **The Information list scrolls at the edge, like Settings and the File Manager** (`info.c`; on request: "the Game Information menu still scrolls with the last thing on the screen ... can we make it scroll the same way stuff scrolls in other menus? (and also if any other menu scrolls this way ...)"). *What was wrong:* `info_update()` put the list at `S.sel * ROW_H - visible` whenever the highlight was below the sixth row, so the highlight stayed on the last row shown and the list moved with every step, up and down (what Settings did until it was changed; the CHANGELOG of 1.2.0 describes it). *Now:* `S.top` is the first row shown (`LIST_ROWS` 7, as in Settings, so `LIST_BOTTOM` is `LIST_TOP + 7 * ROW_H` = 240, it was 236); `S.top = S.sel` when the highlight goes above it (with the captions directly above the row: `while (S.top > 0 && s_rows[S.top - 1].header)`), `S.top = S.sel - (LIST_ROWS - 1)` when it goes below the last one shown; `S.scroll` follows `S.top * ROW_H` as before. *The other lists were looked at and already move at the edge:* Settings and its choice popup (`S.top`, `pop_top`), the File Manager (`s_top`), Manage Saved Data and Games and Apps (`saves.c`, `S.top`), the Music list (`music_target` in `ui.c`) and the Library grid (`grid_target`); the Home cards are a carousel with the selected card in the middle, and the options popups have no more rows than fit. *Tests* (private, `gameinfo/info_test.c`, 908 checks): the first row shown stays until the highlight is past the seventh row and then moves by as much as the highlight did, the end shows the last row last, the list comes to rest on `S.top * ROW_H`, going up keeps the list still until the highlight is above the first row shown, a caption comes up with the row under it, and a list that fits never scrolls. *Mutation checks* (7 changes): 5 caught; the two that got through cannot change anything (`sel < top + 1` and `sel > top + LIST_ROWS - 2` give the same `S.top` as the lines they replace: the formula gives `top` again).

- **File Manager: a question when a name is taken, and the weak spots of rename, names and moves** (`files.c`, `lang.c`, `lang.h`; on request, after a check of the File Manager: "we should have a popup for when folders or files are named the same (Replace, Skip, Merge (only for folders), Rename, Cancel; the choice was then called Overwrite, "a more common name") ... check the weak spots with Renames, Names and Moving across cards", and "do everything you suggested": Rename asks for a typed name starting at `name (2)`, "for all" is offered, the weak spots are fixed in the same change; the viewers and players for file types were put off).
  - *Before.* `unique_dest()` gave a copy of a taken name `name (2)` without asking; a folder onto a folder of that name became `Folder (2)`; Rename and New Folder trusted `sceIoRename()` / `sceIoMkdir()`; a move on the same card trusted the return value of `sceIoRename()`; a copy got the time of now.
  - *The worker* (`worker()` -> `transfer_item()`). One item goes to a folder: nothing of that name there -> `plain_transfer()` (a verified rename on the same card, else `copy_tree()` and, for a move, `delete_tree()` of the original). A name that is taken -> `cf_resolve()`: an answer given for all is used, else the worker writes `ask_name`, `ask_dir`, `ask_src_dir`, `ask_dst_dir`, `ask_same`, `ask_more` into `W`, sets `W.ask = 1` and waits in a loop of `sceKernelDelayThread(10000)` until the screen sets `W.ask = 2` (or `W.cancel`: ○ on the progress panel, or the screen closing, which is `CF_CANCEL`). Answers: `CF_REPLACE`, `CF_SKIP`, `CF_MERGE`, `CF_RENAME`, `CF_CANCEL`. `cf_choices()` says which are offered: a copy into its own folder (`same`: the parent of the source is the destination) only Rename and Cancel; folder on folder all five; anything else without Merge; Skip is the one chosen at first.
  - *Overwrite* (the choice is `CF_REPLACE`, the function `replace_item()`; it was first called Replace and was renamed on request: "a more common name"): the old one is renamed to `<name>.psp5old` (a free name: `side_name()`), the new one is made by `plain_transfer()`, and only when that has worked the old one is removed; when it has not (a failed read, a full card, ○) the half-made new one is removed and the old one gets its name back. A power cut leaves a `.psp5old` beside it, never a lost file. Never done when `same` or when the source lies inside the thing that would go (`is_inside(src, dst)`: `R_INTO_ITSELF`).
  - *Merge* (`merge_dir()`): every child of the source goes through `transfer_item()` into the folder that is there, so a name taken inside it is asked again; a moved folder is removed with `sceIoRmdir()` when it is empty. Cancelled halfway: the files that arrived are whole (`copy_file()` removes a half file), nothing that was there is touched (the clean-up of a failed copy is only for what the copy made whole, not for a merge).
  - *"For all"* (`W.all_dd`, `W.all_other`): two remembered answers, one for folder on folder and one for everything else (so Merge for all does not decide about the files inside it). It is offered when more names can be taken (`ask_more`: more items to come, or the item is a folder). A remembered answer is not used for an item that is `same` unless it is Rename, and an answer given for such an item is not remembered unless it is Rename. Rename for all uses the next free `name (k)` (`unique_dest()`) and does not open the keyboard; Rename with "for all" off opens it. This also holds for a copy into its own folder, where "for all" is offered when more than one item goes (`ask_more`). (A version that greyed Rename out while "for all" was on, and hid "for all" for a copy into its own folder, was made first and taken back on request: Rename is chosen one by one with the keyboard, and "for all" numbers them by itself.)
  - *The screen* (`s_cf`, `cf_build()`, `cf_update()`, `draw_conflict()`): when `W.ask == 1` the question opens (the progress panel is hidden, the list's frame and the key hints fade); ↑ ↓ choose, ✕ answers, △ turns "for all" on and off, ○ answers Cancel. Rename opens `osk_input()` with `unique_dest()`'s name; a name that cannot be (`valid_name()`) or is taken (`path_exists()`) shows a toast and leaves the question open; closing the keyboard does the same. The panel is drawn by `draw_conflict()` in `popup.h`'s panel and choice (250 px wide, in the middle of the screen, the name, one or two lines of what is there, the choices, and the "for all" row with the △ button): it does not use `popup_ask()` because five choices and the row do not fit under its fixed top (y = 70).
  - *The weak spots.* (1) **Rename and New Folder** (`rename_item()`, `make_folder()`, results `RN_OK/BAD/EXISTS/FAILED`) look with `path_exists()` first; a change of capitals only goes by `<name>.psp5tmp` (FAT sees one name). (2) **`rename_checked()` and `move_verified()`**: a rename is only believed when the thing is there under the new name; the PSP's `sceIoRename()` may take only the NAME of its second argument (PPSSPP does: `DirectoryFileSystem::RenameFile` puts the new name in the old folder, and it answers "already exists" for the same name; PPSSPP's `File::Rename` replaces an existing target on Linux and refuses on Windows; real PSPs were not tried): then the thing sits in its own folder under the new name, and is renamed back before the move is done by copying. (3) **`valid_name()`** refuses a name over 255 bytes, one that ends in a dot or a space, and control characters. (4) **`name_ext()` / `numbered_name()`**: `a.tar.gz` is `a (2).tar.gz` (`.tar` plus gz, bz2, xz or zst), and a stem that would make the name longer than 255 bytes is cut on a character boundary. (5) **`keep_times()`**: `sceIoChstat(dst, &st, FIO_CST_CT | FIO_CST_AT | FIO_CST_MT)` after a file is closed and after a folder's files are made (the macros have a fallback for a host build; the PSP SDK has them in `pspiofilemgr_stat.h`). (6) **A move to the other card whose original cannot all be removed** is `R_NOT_DELETED` (`W.partial`, said at the end: "Copied, but the original could not be removed."), the other items still go. (7) **`R_INTO_ITSELF`** has a text of its own. (8) **Room**: the start counts only the size of top-level items whose name is free (`need`); `copy_file()` asks for room for a file of 1 MB or more when its turn comes (`R_NO_SPACE`), and a write that fails on a card with less than two buffers free says `R_NO_SPACE` instead of `R_FAILED` (`write_failed()`).
  - *Texts* (9, in 11 languages; 260 strings now): `S_F_CF_FILE`, `S_F_CF_DIR` (what is there), `S_F_CF_REPLACE`, `S_F_CF_SKIP`, `S_F_CF_MERGE`, `S_F_CF_ALL` (Apply to All), `S_F_EXISTS`, `S_F_INTO_ITSELF`, `S_F_NOT_DELETED`. *Rename* and *Cancel* are the existing `S_F_RENAME` and `S_F_CANCEL`. The Russian and the Japanese `S_F_NOT_DELETED` were first longer than the toast (518 px against 448) and were made shorter.
  - *Tests* (private). `files/files_test.c` (169 checks): the worker with a scripted player (`SCRIPT(...)`, answers tied to a name because the order of a folder's files is the file system's): every answer on file/file, folder/folder, file/folder and folder/file, a copy and a move on the same card and to the other, "for all" (Skip, Replace, Rename; folder and file kept apart; not used for the item itself), the choices of every combination, safe Replace (failed read, ○, a rename that fails or only says it is done), Merge (asked inside, a move leaves only what was skipped, ○ halfway touches nothing), the item itself and a folder into itself, a move to the other card with an original that cannot be removed, a rename that takes only the name (`g_io_rename_ignores_path` in `posix/posixio.c`), the dates (`PSP5_REAL_MTIME=1` is now set for this group in `run_all.sh`; `sceIoChstat` has a stand-in), the names (`valid_name`, `numbered_name`, a 255-byte name, a Japanese name cut between characters, `.tar.gz`), `rename_item()` and `make_folder()`, and a nearly full card (`g_io_free_clusters`). `files/popup_test.c` (650 checks): the question on the screen (choices, Skip chosen, ↑ ↓ and the ends, △ with and without "for all", the keyboard (`g_osk_reply`, `g_osk_init` in `files/inc/psputility.h`: a taken name, a bad name, closed, cannot open, a free name), ○, a copy into its own folder, a folder and a file with "for all", Rename with 'for all' (drawn like the other choices, △ on it keeps it chosen, ✕ with 'for all' on does not open the keyboard and the next free names `name (2)`, `name (3)` are used; with it off the keyboard is asked for each one), a copy into the own folder with two items (Rename and Cancel, 'for all' offered and on, no keyboard; or a name typed for each), whole pixels, the panel's size and place, every choice and sentence and toast in the 11 languages, and the toast of every way an operation can end).
    *Mutation checks* (35 hand-made changes of `files.c`, each run through the two groups): the choices (Merge for a file, no `same`, the chosen row), the remembered answers (both guards, the two slots, `ask_more`), the guards against the item itself, the bar after a skip, Replace (no put back, no removal of the old one, the rename not checked, two), the partial move, the dates (file and folder), the room (the limit, the write, the start), the names (the character boundary, `.tar.gz`, `valid_name`), Rename and New Folder (the look first, capitals only), the keyboard (typed name checked, △ without "for all", "for all" with the keyboard), `R_INTO_ITSELF`, the empty folder after a move merge, the toast table, and the rename checks. 26 were caught at once; **nine got through and each got a test**: the answer for the item itself (two), `ask_more` for a folder, the write that runs out of room, the cut inside a character (the Japanese name was too short to need a cut), a rename that only takes the capitals (a count of rename calls), the toast table (a toast of every result), and the two rename checks (a rename that fails and one that lies). All 35 are caught now. The Rename-with-'for all' rule got 4 more ("for all" hidden for a copy into its own folder, the keyboard always, the keyboard whenever "for all" is offered, the keyboard unless "for all" is on): 3 caught; the last is equivalent ("for all" is only ever on when it is offered).
  - *Not done, on purpose:* an "Overwrite" that keeps the old one as a backup after a power cut is not tidied at the next start (a `.psp5old` stays); the time of a folder that is merged is not set; the question does not show the sizes and dates of the old and the new one (a later idea); the File Manager still does not open files by type.
  - *Picture:* `file-manager-conflict.png` (the demo's PSP5 folders: copy `ms0:/PSP5` into `ms0:/PSP/GAME`).

- **Date and Time** (`datetime.c`, `datetime.h`, `settings.c`, `config.c`, `lang.c`, `ui.c`, `info.c`, `saves.c`; on request: Settings -> System -> Date and Time, under Language, with *Current Date and Time* ("it will just show what we set"), *Daylight Saving* (Remove One Hour / Keep Unchanged / Add One Hour), *Date Format* (YYYY.MM.DD, MM.DD.YYYY, DD.MM.YYYY) and *Time Format* (12-Hour Clock, 24-Hour Clock), "all of these settings will affect the stuff that PSP5 shows everywhere ... the actual settings won't be changed, only what PSP5 shows, cause we don't want to brick the PSP". Then, in the middle of the work: "the date and time should only show current time, there is no need to make it changeable, it shouldn't be a button that acts on click").
  - *It is display only.* Nothing writes the PSP's clock or its settings (no `sceRtcSetCurrentTick`, no `sceUtilitySetSystemParam`): the module reads the clock (`sceRtcGetCurrentClockLocalTime()`, which is the local time the PSP has, with its own time zone and Daylight Saving) and moves it for the display only. The first version had an editor with an offset (`timeoffset=`, a popup with ← → ↑ ↓); the maintainer did not want the time to be changeable, so the offset, the editor, `dt_set_shown()` and two texts (*Set*, *PSP Time*) are gone. What is left: the row shows the time, the three choices move it by -1 h / 0 / +1 h and write it in the chosen formats.
  - *The module* (`datetime.c`). A time is the packed time of `scan_pack_time()` (year - 1980 in six bits, month, day, hour, minute, second in one number, 0 = none, the years 1980 to 2043). `dt_shift(t, minutes)` moves one by whole minutes over the calendar (days from a civil date and back, the proleptic Gregorian algorithm with 64-bit numbers; the seconds stay; before the first minute of 1980 or after the last of 2043 it stays at the end; `0` stays `0`; a shift of `0` gives the time back as it is, even one that is no date, such as a file with an empty date, so the standard settings show every time exactly as before). A time with a field outside its range (month 0 or 13 to 15, day 0, hour 24 to 31, minute 60 to 63, second 60 to 63) is made a date the same way every time before it is moved. `dt_shown(t)` is `dt_shift(t, dt_shift_minutes())` where `dt_shift_minutes()` is -60, 0 or 60 from `g_dt_dst` (a value that is none of the three is *keep*). `dt_text_stored()` writes a file's or a game's time as shown; `dt_text()` writes a time that is shown already; `dt_clock_text()` is the time of day for the top bar and uses only the hour and the minute of the PSP's clock (not the date), so a PSP whose clock says the year 1970 or 2099 still shows its clock, as it always did.
  - *The texts.* `YYYY.MM.DD`, `MM.DD.YYYY` or `DD.MM.YYYY` (always two digits for the day and the month, four for the year, points between), and `14:05` or, with the 12-hour clock, `2:05 PM` (no zero in front of the hour; `12:05 AM` after midnight, `12:00 PM` at noon). The standard is `YYYY.MM.DD` and the 24-hour clock: the order is the one psp5 always had, but the dashes (`2026-10-28`) became points (`2026.10.28`), which is a visible change for everyone. The longest text is `2026.10.28 12:05 PM`, 19 characters: the buffers that hold one were looked at (`pan_date[24]`, `when[24]` in `saves.c`, `char text[40]` in `info.c`, `clock_text[16]` in `ui.c`, which was 8 and would have cut `12:05 PM`).
  - *Where it is used.* `ui.c` `draw_top_bar()` (the clock), `info.c` `time_row()` (*Modified*, *Last played*), `saves.c` `fmt_time()` (the date of a save and of a game's last played time). Not changed, on purpose: the FTP server (`LIST` and `MDTM` tell a computer the real file dates: it is a protocol), the sort orders (they sort the packed numbers, and a shift of an hour keeps their order), the played times and `lastplayed.txt` (they are written in the PSP's time and moved only when they are shown). `psp5time.prx` is untouched.
  - *The page* (`settings.c`). `PG_DATETIME` (between `PG_CSTORE` and `PG_PLUGMGR`, so that the fixed pages are still the ones before `PG_PLUGEDIT`), the row after *Language* on the System page. Its rows: `IT_DATETIME` (new type: it only draws `dt_text(dt_now())` at the right, nothing happens on ✕, it is not saved), and three ordinary choices on `g_dt_dst`, `g_dt_date` and `g_dt_time` (unsigned chars, `own`, so they are in `psp5.cfg`). The date formats are not translated (`YYYY.MM.DD` is the same in every language), the other ten texts are (`S_DATETIME`, `S_DT_CURRENT`, `S_DT_DST`, `S_DT_DST_REMOVE`, `S_DT_DST_KEEP`, `S_DT_DST_ADD`, `S_DT_DATE_FORMAT`, `S_DT_TIME_FORMAT`, `S_DT_24H`, `S_DT_12H`; 251 strings now).
  - *`psp5.cfg`.* `dst=` (0, 1, 2), `datefmt=` (0, 1, 2) and `timefmt=` (0, 1), written after `options=` and read back only when the value is one of the choices; a value in memory that is none of them is written as the standard. An old file has none of the lines and keeps the standard. The file stays far below its 384 bytes (about 200 with the longest of everything).
  - *Tests* (private). New group `datetime` (`units/datetime_test.c`, 469 checks: packing; 612 shifts made with Python's `datetime` (`shift_table.inc`), leap days, the ends of 1980 and 2043; **every day of 1980 to 2043 stepped to the next and back against a calendar of its own** (23375 days); the odd fields; Daylight Saving; the six texts and a buffer that is too small; the clock for the top bar over midnight, with the year 1970 or 2099, with an hour of 24 and a minute of 60, and with a clock that cannot be read). `units/config_test.c` (the three lines: written, read back, in a file with the others, an old file, every value that is none of the choices, the longest file), `gameinfo/info_test.c` (the dates in the new format; *Modified* and *Last played* in the three date formats, the 12-hour clock and both hours of Daylight Saving, the ends of 1980 and 2043, no row for no time, and every row still fitting in the 11 languages in all 18 combinations), `saves/saves_test.c` (`fmt_time()` in the formats, over the new year, the small buffer), `ui/library_test.c` (the top bar says 15 different times in the right way, one clock only, at the right, and the tabs end before the longest, `11:58 PM`; `ui/stubs.c` has a clock that a test can set) and `settings/settings_test.c` (the System page has seven rows with Date and Time under Language; the page: four rows, their names and values in order; ✕ and the other buttons on the first row do nothing; each choice's popup; the clock row follows a choice at once; a clock that cannot be read; every language with every combination: a label and its value never touch). Every stored fingerprint of the Settings pages was made again, after looking at the pages in PPSSPP with the real font.
    *Mutation checks.* `datetime.c`: 296 changes of operators and numbers, 259 caught, 6 in a comment line, **31 not caught and all of them cannot change anything**: the branches and the era numbers for a negative year or day count, which cannot happen between 1980 and 2043 (`y >= 0 ? y : y - 399`, `z >= 0 ? ...`, 10), the divisors of the calendar algorithm that give the same results in these 64 years (`yoe / 100` as `/ 101`, 1459, 36523 and so on, 6), clamps whose limit changes nothing (`if (d > 31) d = 31` with a five-bit day, `if (mi > 58) mi = 59`, `if (h > 22) h = 23`, `second > 58 ? 59 : second`, 10), the size of two text buffers (4) and `now.hour % 25` (1). The integration: 18 changes (the three limits of the config reading, the order of the lines, the three guards when writing, `time_row()`, `fmt_time()`, the clock row, the clock buffer and its size, the title, the place of the row, the order of the three values, one `own`, the number of values, the type of the row): all caught; three of them, the guards when writing, were not caught at first, and a test for them was added.
  - *Pictures.* `settings-date-time.png` is new (the clock in it is the test value 2026.10.27 21:05) and `settings-system.png` has the new row.

- **The title of a Settings page is its own name** (`settings.c` `settings_draw()`; on request: "remove the '>' from the settings headers, just make it show the current open setting, not the whole path to it"). It was `System > Appearance`, now `Appearance`; the same for *Date and Time*, *Console Information*, *Plugins*, *Manage Plugins*, *Install New Plugin*, *Console Storage* and the two plugin pages (*Install to*, *Uninstall from*, which no longer name the plugin: the page before them does). `PARENT[]` is still how ○ finds the page before. Tests: `settings/settings_test.c` (every title, one per page; the Japanese *Console Storage* page now has the same title as the internal storage's name, 本体ストレージ, so the test counts two). Pictures made again: `settings-appearance.png`, `settings-console-information.png`, `console-storage.png`, `manage-plugins.png` (it needs a `PLUGINS.TXT` in the demo's ARK folder: cwcheat.prx for all, prxshot.prx for game and vsh, psp5time.prx for game and pops), `install-plugin.png` and `settings-system.png`.

- **PS1 to PS4 have the colours of the real consoles' themes** (`theme.c`, `THEMES[]` rows 9 to 12 and the colours in `deco_ps2()`, `deco_ps3()` and `deco_ps4()`; on request: "these ref pics are the actual color schemes of the PS1/2/3/4 themes, please make sure that our PS1/2/3/4 themes use the same colors"). *(Since this entry the moving shapes were replaced and the strengths of the lamp, the haze and the sheen were made the same for every theme: see the first entry of this section; the numbers of `THEMES[]` and the shapes named here are the old ones.)*
  - *The pictures* (four, from the maintainer): PS1 grey with a warm beam, PS2 black with blue-violet light and a lavender mist at the bottom, PS3 black with gold and cream light and a tan wave, PS4 blue-violet with purple at the lower right. Before: PS1 light grey, PS2 deep navy, **PS3 blue** and PS4 saturated cyan-blue.
  - *How the colours were found.* A private tool draws the real `theme.c` (the surface: gradient, right-hand shade, glow, haze, sheen, beam) with the mock graphics chip for any set of 21 numbers (`surf_top`, `surf_bottom`, `glow`, `haze`, `sheen`, `beam`) and compares it with a picture: the mean colour of 16 x 9 blocks and ten
    chosen points (the lamp, the mist, the corners). A random search (several starts, steps that shrink) found the numbers. An unrestricted search gave unnatural palettes (a pink lamp for PS1, alphas of 0 or 255), so the lamp and the haze were kept to the colour family of the picture and the alphas to a range. The numbers were then checked by eye next to the pictures
    and a few were set by hand (PS3 and PS1 neutral instead of green or blue-grey, PS2's right side darker, PS4's bottom less red, the haze of PS2 and PS4 softer because with a high alpha its end at x = 250 shows as an edge).
  - *The rows now:* `ps1` surface (53, 51, 49) to (41, 45, 48), lamp (255, 241, 203); `ps2` (3, 3, 62) to (0, 0, 1), lamp (60, 50, 255); `ps3` (12, 11, 9) to (8, 7, 6), lamp (255, 237, 209); `ps4` (20, 56, 141) to (22, 0, 6), lamp (132, 230, 255), purple `sheen` (43, 35, 114, 230).
    The accents (PS1 light grey, PS2 blue-violet, PS3 gold, PS4 blue), the cards without a picture and the `intro` colours follow. The moving shapes took the same hues (PS2 towers and orbs blue-violet, PS3 waves warm cream instead of white, PS4 waves and particles blue-violet).
    PS1's symbols keep their classic colours. The Library's picture, the switches and the progress bars take the accent, so they changed with it (`lib_palette()` is unchanged).
  - *What is not the same as the pictures, and why:* the surface is one vertical gradient with a mild shade on the right and one lamp, so the pictures' soft falloff is only approximated. The lamp is a little weaker in blue than PS2's and PS4's pictures (a strip at the top: PS2 blue 189 against 249, PS4 216 against 248), the beam is sharper than the pictures' light, and the right side of PS2 and PS4
    is a little brighter than theirs (PS4: the top right is (20, 31, 81), the picture's (16, 16, 40)). Changing that would need a per-theme shape of the lamp or of the shade, which was not done.
  - *Tests* (private, `gfx/theme_test.c`, 506 checks, 46 of them new): the mean colour of the four corners of each theme against the corners of its picture (a mean difference of at most 12 over the twelve numbers, none further than 50 off), the character of each (PS1 neutral and warm at the lamp, PS2 blue on the left and black on the right, PS3 warm and never blue, black on the right, PS4 blue on the left and purple at the lower right),
    the colour of the lamp strip, the accents and the dark cards, and what the moving shapes add to the picture (PS2 blue-violet, PS3 warm cream, PS4 blue). The fingerprints of the Library's picture of the four themes (and the full-pixel one of PS2) were made again after looking at the frames. `boot_test.c` (the intro ends on the surface, all 13 themes) passes unchanged.
    *Mutation checks* (17 hand-made changes of `theme.c` in a copy, each run through the theme test): PS3's surface blue again, PS1's lamp blue, PS4's sheen removed, PS2's surface too bright, PS3's waves white, PS4's waves orange, PS2's towers green, PS4's surface red, PS1's surface red, PS3's haze strong, PS3's lamp pink, PS2's lamp red,
    the four accents changed, PS1's card bright. Ten were caught by the corner checks at once; two got through them (the removed sheen of PS4 and the pink lamp of PS3), which is why the checks of the lamp strip and of PS4's lower right were added, and they catch both now; the accents and the card are caught by the accent checks (added after the first twelve).
    The pictures of the README: `theme.png` was shot again (PS3).

- **Sound: new recordings, all as loud as one another; the gain is gone** (`res/sounds/*.wav`, `sound.c`, `tools/convert_sounds.py`; on request: "new enhanced sounds that now have the same volume as each other (no longer leaving move and boot noticeably quieter): replace the existing sounds with those", then "remove the gain then and keep converting them to the proper format").
  - *What came* (`enhanced_ui_sounds_v2.zip`, made by the maintainer): `move`, `select`, `back`, `error`, `boot` as **24-bit** mono PCM at 44.1 kHz. `load_wav()` only takes 16-bit PCM (`rd16(b + body + 14) == 16`), so they would have been refused and the built-in sounds played instead, without a word. They were converted with `python3 tools/convert_sounds.py <folder>`
    (ffmpeg to 16-bit; silence below -60 dBFS cut at the end, 5 to 14 ms, and at the start down to 1 ms, 0 to 11 samples; the level is not touched). Lengths: move 0.361 s, select 0.942, back 1.069, error 0.982, boot 4.872 (the old ones 0.367, 0.956, 1.080, 0.993, 4.878).
  - *The levels* (dBFS, peak / average where it is not quiet): move -5.1 / -17.6, select -7.0 / -21.4, back -8.3 / -21.4, error -3.9 / -19.1, boot -5.6 / -21.6. The old set was -27.3 / -40.0, -15.4 / -29.8, -18.6 / -31.7, -18.1 / -33.3 and -17.9 / -33.9: *move* 10 dB below *select*. The old set was played with `FILE_GAIN` 2.5 (+8 dB), which put *select* at about
    -21.8 on average: the new *select* is at -21.4, so it is the same as before and the others are raised to it (*move* by 14 dB, *error* 6, *boot* 4, *back* 2).
  - **The gain is removed**, not set to 1: with the new files a gain of 2.5 clips every one of them (peaks 1.0 to 1.6 times full scale). `FILE_GAIN` and the clamp that went with it are gone from `load_wav()`; a sample is the file's sample (stereo is still mixed down to mono: `(L + R) / 2`). **A consequence:** a `.wav` that somebody made for the older versions plays 8 dB quieter now. `tools/convert_sounds.py`'s notes say to aim for peaks of -4 to -8 dBFS.
    Two sounds at the same time are added and limited at full scale as before; with these peaks a *select* over a *back* could touch the limit, which is rare.
  - *Tests* (private, group `sound`, now 6151 checks): the gain checks are "the file as it is" (first sample -995, a full-scale file unchanged, stereo `(1000 + 3000) / 2` = 2000), a `#error` if `FILE_GAIN` comes back, and `shipped()` checks of the shipped files: all load, none at full scale, **each peak between -9 and -3 dBFS** and **the five within 6 dB of each other in average level** (the old set was 10 dB apart; the new one is 4 dB).
  - *Mutation checks* (`mut47_all.py`, 21 changes of the conversion loop of `load_wav()`: the gain put back, the left or the right side only, the sum not halved, a mono file read as stereo, and the numbers and operators of those lines): all 21 caught.
