  - **Not tried on a PSP**, and the host tests say nothing about how it sounds: the loudness is the maintainer's to judge. Making them quieter or louder as a whole needs the gain back (one line in `load_wav()`), or other files.

- **Sound: no crackle when a sound is started again before it has ended** (`sound.c`; on request: "when you scroll fast, for example holding the nav button, you can hear crackling between the sounds; it is not in the original files").
  - *The cause.* `sound_play()` started a sound that was still playing over from its beginning *in the same voice* (`pos = 0`): the wave was cut in the middle. The recording of *move* was 0.37 s (16175 samples; the old `res/sounds/move.wav`, peak 3527 after the gain of 2.5 it was played with then; the new one is 0.36 s, see the entry above), and holding a direction starts it every 85 ms (every 45 ms after 1.2 s: `step_input()` in `ui.c`; popups every 80 ms), so almost every start was
    a cut: a step from the sample that was playing to the first sample of the new start, measured on the shipped file at 20 to 100 ms after the start: median about 1000, up to 3465 (10 % of full scale), where the largest step between neighbours inside the file is **215** (the cut is up to 16 times that: the same ratio with the new file, 17851 against 1106, which is the old one made 5.1 times louder). 12 to 22 of those a second are the crackle. The files are not at fault, as said.
  - *The fix.* `Voice` has a `release` counter. A start of a sound that is already playing in a voice (and not fading yet) puts that voice on `release = RELEASE` (256 samples, 5.8 ms) and the new start takes **another** voice. In the mixer a voice with `release > 0` is multiplied by `release / RELEASE` (a straight line down; integer maths) and ends when the counter reaches 0 (`release` runs RELEASE, RELEASE-1 ... 1,
    so the first sample after the new start is at full level, with no step, and the last at 1/256). Different sounds are added as before, and a sound that is started only once is untouched. With the earlier start gone in 256 samples, a held button uses two voices at a time (four exist). When all four are busy (more than four starts between two blocks) the new start takes a voice that is fading out already, else the one that is furthest on (a hard cut,
    as before, but only then). The mixer's block loop moved out of `audio_thread()` into `mix_block()` unchanged apart from the fade-out (so a test can run it without the thread). The recordings are not changed, nothing is added to the start of a sound (they begin near 0), and no setting was added.
  - *Not changed:* the buffers and the thread (two blocks of 512 samples, `sceAudioOutputPannedBlocking`), `sound_fade_out()`, the idle tail of 8 blocks, `sound_release()`, `sound_wait()`.
  - *Tests* (private, group `sound`, now 6145 checks): `mix_block()` is run on its own, so the output is compared **sample for sample** with what the fade-out must give (every start fades from the next start of the same sound; `expected()` in `sound_test.c`), for the shipped `move.wav`, a made-up waveform that starts at once and the built-in *move*: a second start after 1 to 30 blocks, a held button (every 3 blocks and every block), at most two voices at a time, no voice left, the last start playing on unchanged
    once the others are gone, another sound (*select* over *move*) added and not faded, seven starts between two blocks (all in range, the newest plays whole, nothing left). Not from the model: the largest step of the output while the button is held is the file's own (215 with the old file, **1106** with the new one; 3 times the sound's own step is the limit; a cut gave 16 times).
  - *Mutation checks* (`mut46_all.py`, 79 changes of `mix_block()` and `sound_play()`: the old cut put back, no fade, the fade turned round or longer, a voice that never ends or ends early, the counter not running, the new start taking the fading voice, a stolen voice, the fade applied to other sounds, no clipping ...): all 79 caught.
  - **Not tried on a PSP**: the host test shows that the output no longer steps, not how it sounds. The maintainer's ears on a real PSP are the test: hold ↓ in a long list and in Settings.

- **Console Information: *Last IP address* and *Free RAM*, and no storage rows** (`netlink.c/.h`, `system.c/.h`, `settings.c`, `lang.c/.h`; on request, in another session that worked from `8608fc3`: a *Last IP address* row that survives the Wi-Fi being closed, *Free RAM* as the only RAM row and no *Memory Stick* row; then, here: "remove the Internal Storage row as well, since this
  information is easily accessible from Console Storage"). The other session's patch applied to `8608fc3` without changes, was read through (the code, the three texts in 11 languages, its tests) and its documents were rewritten to the wording of the other entries. Nothing was tried on a PSP.
  - *The rows* (`fill_about()`): Nickname, Model, System software, CFW (ARK-x), psp5 authentic, Made by, Forked by, MAC address, **Last IP address**, **Free RAM**, then the battery rows. No *Memory Stick* and no internal storage row: both storages, with their space, are in *Settings → Storage → Console Storage*.
    Removed with the rows: `about_space()`, `device_space()` (the `sceIoDevctl` call), the four storage fields of `SysAbout` (`sys_about()` asked two file systems on every opening of the page) and the text `S_ABOUT_FREE_OF` ("%s free of %s"), which only they used.
  - **Free RAM.** `sys_about()` sets `SysAbout.ram_free` to `sceKernelTotalFreeMemSize()`: the user memory that is free now, outside the C heap (unused space inside the heap is not counted). It is read when the page is built, i.e. when it is opened, and formatted with `fmt_bytes()`. The modules and the thread stacks take from the heap reserve, so the value is not a fixed number.
  - **The heap reserve stays 8 MB.** The other session had lowered `PSP_HEAP_THRESHOLD_SIZE_KB` to 6144 (on request: "minus 6 MB instead of minus 8 MB"). It was put back to **8192** here, on the maintainer's word "if you feel like 6 MB is too tight": 6 MB is only 2 MB more than the 4 MB of ARK's `RECOVERY.PRX`, and the estimate for the modules, the network and the stacks is about 2.5 MB
    (see the memory entry below), so there would be no room at all. The `heap` source check pins the threshold to exactly 8192. *Free RAM* shows what is really left, so the PSP-1000's table or this row can say whether less would do.
  - **Last IP address.** `netlink_last_ip()` gives the last valid IPv4 address. It is read once from `<g_data_dir>/lastip.txt` (a file that is not 1 to 15 characters of an address, has a newline or any other character after it, or cannot be read is ignored) and does not start any networking. `remember_ip()` keeps it in memory and writes the file only when the address changes
    (a failed write keeps the value in memory). It is called from `netlink_state()` when the connection is up (the FTP Server) and from `netlink_stop()`, which asks the access point control (only when the network libraries were started and the Wi-Fi switch is on) for the address, because the PSP's own Wi-Fi window (`wifi.c`) can connect without `netlink_attach()` / `netlink_state()` ever being asked.
    `sceNetApctlGetInfo`'s address is only used when it has a terminating zero in its 16 bytes. A connection made entirely outside psp5 authentic cannot be seen. Before an address is known the row says *Not recorded yet*. It is history, not a status: the FTP guide and `INSTALL.md` say to use the address on the live FTP screen.
  - *Texts.* Three new strings (*Free RAM*, *Last IP address*, *Not recorded yet*), each in all 11 languages, and one removed (`S_ABOUT_FREE_OF`): 241 in all.
  - *Tests* (private). `settings/settings_test.c` (907 checks): the rows and their order, no *Memory Stick* and no *System Storage* row (even when a card is in), *Free RAM* at zero and between two openings, *Not recorded yet*, the layouts in all languages; the golden frames and hashes of the Console Information pages were updated (the other session) after looking at the host frames.
    New group `about` (`units/about_test.c`, 13 checks, and a read of the source that none of the removed names comes back): the real `sys_about()` cut out of `system.c`, against stand-ins: Free RAM read fresh on every call (6 MB, 0, 56 MB), the address copied, the rest of the structure and the values next to it untouched, no storage asked, and the free-block and heap queries of the first version never called.
    `ftp/netlink_test.c` (in a throwaway folder, a second process reads the file back): an address got through the FTP Server is remembered and saved, reading it changes nothing in the network, it stays after the Wi-Fi is stopped, and a connection made by the Wi-Fi window alone is recorded when the network is stopped; an unassigned `0.0.0.0`, a cancelled window, an address without a terminating zero, failing state
    and address calls (also ones that wrote a value before failing), no network libraries started or only some of them, and the Wi-Fi switch off record nothing; seven damaged files, one with a zero in it and one 15 characters long (`255.255.255.255`, the longest); the value stays in memory when the file cannot be written. `ftp/wifi_test.c`: the Wi-Fi window of Settings records the address.
    The `t` group (241 strings, the new ones not empty in any language), the `heap` source check (exactly 8192) and the `efrule` check (it no longer reads `system.c`: the internal storage is not shown there any more).
  - *Mutation checks* (`mut45_all.py`, 13 changes of the rows and of `sys_about()`: the *Memory Stick* and the internal storage row put back, the *Last IP address* row removed, always *Not recorded yet* or the condition turned round, the *Free RAM* row removed, halved, a fixed 6 MB or not read, the two rows in the other order, the address not copied or cut short): all 13 caught.
    The other session's own checks (13 for the RAM rows and the reserve, 33 for the first version of the last-IP code) were all caught too; they are in the private bundle.
    - **Not tried on a PSP.** The README picture of Console Information was taken again in PPSSPP with the test build of this code (`make EXTRA=-DPSP5_TEST`), the demo stick and ↓ nine times from the first row, so that it shows *CFW*, *MAC address*, *Last IP address* (*Not recorded yet*: the demo was never on Wi-Fi) and *Free RAM* (PPSSPP's number, 8.1 MB). The MAC address, the version of ARK and the RAM in it are the demo's.

- **FTP: a guide for when the computer cannot find the PSP** (`docs/FTP-TROUBLESHOOTING.md`, new; links added in `INSTALL.md` and `README.md`; on request: the maintainer's own guide, "check it, make sure it's tailored to psp5 authentic and ARK-4/5 and integrate it in the best way"). Nothing about this is in the code.
  - *Where it lives.* Its own file in `docs/` (about 200 lines of commands for three systems would swamp INSTALL.md), with one bullet in the existing "No route to host" list of `INSTALL.md` (the symptom is the same) and a clause in the README's FTP bullet. The CHANGELOG has a line under *Network*.
  - *What was changed from the original text.* The name is *psp5 authentic*. The quick checks come first and include *WLAN Power Save* (the one cause that psp5 authentic itself detects and shows a hint for); the original only had client isolation. A table to see that the address lookup really fails (`ip neigh show`: `INCOMPLETE` / `FAILED`;
    `Get-NetNeighbor`: `Unreachable` / `Incomplete`; `arp -an`: `(incomplete)`). Where to find the MAC address in psp5 authentic (*Console Information*) and in the XMB; the PSP's Wi-Fi is 2.4 GHz only. A port test for each system (`nc -vz`, `Test-NetConnection`). `arp -s` as the Command Prompt alternative on Windows.
    A note for phones and tablets (no static ARP without special access: use a computer or *USB Mode*). **The sentence that said a test on a real device had worked was left out** (the docs never claim a test on a PSP); the guide says it is a workaround for one cause and that, if the entry makes port 21 answer, the address lookup was the obstacle.
    The cause ("some routers do not pass ARP on") is worded as a possibility, not a fact: nobody has found out whether the router or the PSP's Wi-Fi is at fault. Nothing in it says "ARK" matters to the cause: the Wi-Fi is the PSP's own, so it is written as "the same on ARK-4 and ARK-5".
  - *Checked against the code (section 8 of the original).* `ftp.c`: a session with no transfer and no command for `IDLE_MS` (5 min) gets `421 Timeout` and is closed; the listener stays. `ftpscreen.c`: in `FTPS_RUNNING` a `LINK_FAILED` from `netlink_state()` (the apctl state left "got IP") calls `fail(S_FTP_LOST)`, which stops the server and shows *The connection was lost* with ✕ *Start* and Back.
    The guide uses these texts. (A transfer is never cut by the idle timeout: it only applies while `kind == X_NONE`.)
  - *Commands checked.* Linux: `ip route get`, `ip neigh replace ... nud permanent`, `ip neigh show` (`PERMANENT`), a second `replace` and `ip neigh del` were run on this container's `eth0` with a made-up address and then removed (a dummy interface could not be made here). Windows: parameter names, the hyphen MAC format and the default `PolicyStore`
    (an entry is made in the active and the persistent store, so it stays after a restart) from Microsoft's page for `New-NetNeighbor`. macOS: `arp -S`, `-d` and `ifscope` from the `arp(8)` page (it does not say whether entries survive a restart; the guide says "normally gone"). `arp -s` / `-d` on Windows, `Test-NetConnection` and the macOS `nc` flags from general knowledge: **not run**.
    Nothing was tried on a PSP or on a real home network.
  - Not done (could be next): a line about it on the FTP screen itself (it needs 11 languages and a change in `ftpscreen.c`; the *WLAN Power Save* hint is the model for it).

- **Console Information: "CFW (ARK-4|5)" with the whole version of ARK on the right** (`system.c`, `system.h`, `settings.c`; on request: "why don't we make it *CFW (ARK-X)*, X being the 4 or 5, and on the right side the full version, just like with System software and psp5 authentic").
  - *Where the version comes from* (read in the source of ARK-4, `PSP-Archive/ARK-4`, and of ARK-5, `PSP-Arkfive/ARK-5`, not tried on a PSP): `sctrlSEGetVersion()` returns `ARK_MAJOR_VERSION`, `sctrlHENGetVersion()` returns `ARK_MINOR_VERSION` and `sctrlHENGetMinorVersion()` returns `ARK_MICRO_VERSION`
    (names ARK took over from PRO / SE-C: the SDK header documents them as the "SE/OE version", the "HEN version" and the "HEN minor version"; ARK uses them as major, minor and micro). ARK-4's `ark.h` also has `ARK_REVISION` (206 in its main branch); no SystemControl function returns it, so it is not shown. `launch.c` already said that `sctrlHENGetVersion()` is 0 on ARK 5.0.x.
  - `sys_init()` calls `ark_version_text(major, sctrlHENGetVersion(), sctrlHENGetMinorVersion(), s_ark_full, ...)` inside the ARK block (the three numbers are read once). It makes "4.20.69" when the major is 1..99 and the other two 0..999, else "" (no ARK: the functions are not there and give 0 or an error value;
    an unresolved import of the user stubs just returns). `sys_ark_full_version()` gives the text. The test build (`fake.txt` with `ark=4|5`) makes up "4.20.0" or "5.0.0" so the demo has something to show.
  - `fill_about()`: the label is `CFW (ARK-%d)` with `sys_ark_version()` (4 or 5), the value is the text, or a dash when it is empty; no ARK: no row, as before. A row keeps the *pointer* to its label (`add()` does not copy it), so the label is a `static char cfw[24]`, not a local. "CFW" was not translated before and still is not: nothing new in `lang.c`.
  - **README picture:** `docs/screenshots/settings-console-information.png` was taken again with the PPSSPP kit (PPSSPP 1.20.4 headless with `ppsspp-1.20.4-headless-shots.patch`, the test build `make EXTRA=-DPSP5_TEST` of the code at `c606001`, the demo stick of `make_demo.py`, `fake.txt` `model=3 ark=5`, script: the button presses of Settings, ↓ ↓ ✕ (System), ✕ (Console Information)). It differed from the old picture only in the *CFW (ARK-5)* row (y 283 to 314); it was taken again later, scrolled down to the new rows (see the Console Information entry above). The version in it, *5.0.0*, is made up by the test build (`fake_device()`): the demo PSP has no ARK to ask.
  - *Tests* (private). New group `arkver` (`units/arkver_test.c`, 20 checks, `ark_version_text` cut out of `system.c`: real versions, the edges 1/99 and 0/999 and one beyond, error codes, a missing function (0, 0, 0), a buffer of 4; and three reads of `system.c`: the call with the three functions in the right order, the getter, the major from `sctrlSEGetVersion()`).
    `settings/settings_test.c` (884 checks, was 879): the row for ARK-5 and ARK-4 between *System software* and *psp5 authentic*, a dash when there is no version, the label still valid after the page is built again, and the golden pictures: *Console Information* and *rows with texts that are too long* (both show the row; looked at, with real glyphs) and the seven
    hashes of "the make-up of the menu for each PSP" (checked first: with the old row put back in `settings.c` those seven pass with their old values, so the row text is the only change).
  - *Mutation checks* (`mut44_all.py`, 41 changes: 23 + 3 from the generator over `ark_version_text` and the row, 15 by hand: the order of minor and micro, the major from `s_ark`, the format, the empty text, the getter, the size passed, the label (no number, no "CFW", a local array), the value (not the text, always a dash, the condition turned round, the old text), the row condition). 39 caught. The 2 that live are `cfw[24]` as `[25]` and `[23]`: the longest label is 12 characters.

- **Ideas from the original project's issues 37 and 19** (`scan.c`, `scan.h`, `art.c`, `system.c`; on request: "could we face the issues of the original psp5 repo ... then do the #37 fix and the #19 fix, and mention in the changelog that we took inspiration from them"). The original is
  [08Weber/psp5](https://github.com/08Weber/psp5); its v2.0.1 / v2.1.1 notes and issue pages were read, the code here is our own. The other issues that were looked at, and what was found (by reading the code, nothing was tried on a PSP):
  - *Issue 21* (plugins on the other storage) is not a problem here: `plugins_load()` / `plugins_locations()` read the ARK folder and `SEPLUGINS` on `g_root` and, when there is a second storage (`other_root()`), `ARK_01234` and `SEPLUGINS` on it as well. Nothing to do.
  - *Issue 29* (USB Mode waits for the cable on a PSP Go): the cause there was the 2.0.1 start (charging off, `usb.prx` reloaded, a storage size set). `sys_usb_start()` already starts like arkMenu does, which is what 2.1.1 went back to. What we do not have is their retry (cable seen,
    computer not connected for 5 s: deactivate and activate once more). Not added; it can be when a PSP Go owner reports it.
  - *Issue 17* (PSP Go, psp5 started from `ef0:` does not see the Memory Stick): the original relaunches itself with apitype 0x141. psp5 authentic asks for the same through `EFAWARE=1` in the EBOOT's PARAM.SFO (see Version 1.1.0 below), which a PSP Go owner confirmed on ARK-4. **Not known:** whether ARK-5 honours it.
    To try on a PSP Go with ARK-5: psp5 authentic on the internal storage and games on the Memory Stick. If they are not listed, the relaunch is what is missing (`sys_ms_is_ef()` is then true and `scan_all()` leaves `ms0:` out).
  - **Issue 37 (TempGBA single-game folders), part 1: `PBOOT.PBP`.** The folder of such a game holds an `EBOOT.PBP` that is the same in all (a TITLE with nothing in it, no pictures) and a `PBOOT.PBP` with the game's name and pictures; the XMB shows the latter.
    - `Entry` got two fields at its end: `pboot_flags` (`ENTRY_ICON_IN_PBOOT`, `ENTRY_PIC_IN_PBOOT`: the offsets of that picture are those of the `PBOOT.PBP`, not of `path`) and `pboot_stamp` (its size and time in one number, 0 = there is none). No path was added to the entry (it would be 256 bytes for each of up to
      several hundred games): `entry_pboot_path()` makes the path from the `EBOOT.PBP`'s. `sizeof(Entry)` is 8 bigger, so `load_cache()` (`h.entry_size == sizeof(Entry)`) drops the list cache of an older version by itself: every game is read once after the update.
    - `add_eboot()` stats `<folder>/PBOOT.PBP` first (one more `sceIoGetstat` for each game folder at every start, also when the list comes from the cache) and asks `from_cache()` with the stamp, so a `PBOOT.PBP` that is added, changed (size or time) or removed makes that one game be read again. Then `read_pboot()`:
      title (if it has one), icon (if `icon1 > icon0`) and picture (if `snd0 > pic1`) each win on their own, and whatever it lacks stays the `EBOOT.PBP`'s. A file whose first four bytes are not `\0PBP` is ignored, its stamp is still kept. The `EBOOT.PBP` is still what is started (`path`, `launch.c` untouched) and
      the kind (`CATEGORY`) and the disc ID are still its own. A folder with only a `PBOOT.PBP` (an installed update) is still no game.
    - `art.c`: `read_embedded()` / `decode_embedded()` take `in_pboot` (the flag of that picture; the icon fallback of `art_load_bg()` uses the icon's flag). `cache_path()` mixes in `pboot_stamp * 2246822519` (0 for an ordinary game: its file names do not change), so a picture cached from an `EBOOT.PBP`
      is not served for the same game with a `PBOOT.PBP`, and a changed `PBOOT.PBP` gives a new picture.
    - Not changed: *Information* still lists the `EBOOT.PBP`'s own PARAM.SFO, and the helper plugin / playtime still name a game by the `EBOOT.PBP`.
  - **Issue 37, part 2: a title with nothing in it.** `sfo_get_string()` copies an empty `TITLE` over `e->title`, so the folder name (or the file name of an image) that `add_eboot()` / `add_iso()` put there first was wiped and the card had no name. `entry_read_sfo()` now goes through
    `sfo_title()` (without ™ ®, without blanks around it) and only takes the title when something is left. It is for every kind: homebrew, PSN, PS1 and images (`.iso`, `.cso`, `.zso`, `.dax`, `.jso`); the UMD keeps "UMD". Titles lose blanks at both ends (they were kept before; nothing sorts or looks up by them).
  - **Issue 19 (unstable overclock / CFW settings), part 1: ARK-5 applies the clock at the first module load.** The original's v2.0.1 loads and unloads `PSP_MODULE_AV_AVCODEC` in `sys_init()`; the comment in its source says ARK-5 puts the clock and the cache in force when a module is loaded after the system reached its running state. Not verified here.
    In psp5 authentic that first load was `music_init()`, called by `app_start()` after the intro, the scan and `loader_start()`: the PSP changed its clock under a running scan. `apply_ark_settings()` (`system.c`) does it inside the ARK block of `sys_init()`, which `main()` calls before `app_start()` (before `sound_init()`, `umd_start()`,
    the intro, `scan_all()` and every thread); it unloads only when its own load worked (an "already loaded" answer is somebody else's). `music.c` is unchanged and loads the codecs as before.
  - **Part 2: a wait before the reset.** `settle()` (50 ms, `sceKernelDelayThread`) before `scePowerRequestColdReset()` (`sys_restart()`), `scePowerRequestStandby()` (`sys_shutdown()`) and `sctrlKernelExitVSH()` (`sys_exit_xmb()`). Settings → Restart saves `SETTINGS.TXT` and resets in the same moment (`settings_commit()`, then `run_action()`).
    The original also only waits (its notes say "flush"; no `sceIoSync` is in its source), and so it is here: `sceIoSync` was not added because nothing here could try what it does on a PSP Go. The wait is not a guarantee.
  - *Tests* (private). New group `pboot` (`scan/pboot_test.c`, fixtures `scan/mkpboot.py`, 68 checks): TempGBA in a category folder and in `PSP/GAME`, title / icon / picture each taken from the `PBOOT.PBP` or left with the `EBOOT.PBP`, PSN kind kept, a `PBOOT.PBP` that is wrong in its first four bytes / has no title / has offsets that run backwards, an update folder with only a
    `PBOOT.PBP`, empty / blank / missing / ™® / padded / one-character titles of homebrew and of six images (`.iso`, `.cso`, `.jso`, `.dax`, `.zso`), unused bytes in front of the PARAM.SFO, a PARAM.SFO of exactly 64 KB (read) and one of 64 KB + 1 (not), a path of 255 characters, a size and time whose stamp would be 0, no file or folder left open, the list cache (nothing read again; `PBOOT.PBP` changed in size only, in time only, added, removed; a cache whose entries have another size). `art/art_test.c` (39 checks): icons and backgrounds from the `PBOOT.PBP`,
    the flag needed, `EBOOT.PBP` and `PBOOT.PBP` with different icons not mixed up in the cache, a replaced `PBOOT.PBP` shows its new icon. New group `settle` (`units/settle_test.c`, 6 checks, and three reads of the order of lines: the wait before `sctrlKernelExitVSH`, the early load before `snprintf(settings...)` in `sys_init()`, `sys_init()` before `app_start()` in `main()`).
  - *Mutation checks* (159 changes: 98 from the generator over the new code of `scan.c`, `art.c` and `system.c`, 61 by hand: the title rules, every line of `read_pboot()`, the stamp, the cache comparison, the flags, the paths, the order and the length of the wait, the module that is loaded and unloaded). 102 were caught at once, 57 lived.
    Of these 15 showed real holes and are closed by tests (a one-character title read as no title; a path with no slash; a stamp of 0, three changes; the 64 KB limit of a PARAM.SFO, two changes; a path of 255 characters, in `scan.c` and `art.c`; the folder name cut to 127; the argument of the reset; the seek to the PARAM.SFO (every fixture had it
    right after the header); `PIC0` taken for `PIC1` (every fixture had no `PIC0`); the `EBOOT.PBP` left open). 42 still live and change nothing: 10 in the new code (a title loop that stops one character early after the leading blanks went; `*size == 1` for `== 0` and removing that guard: a PARAM.SFO of 0 bytes yields no title anyway;
    `if (1)` for `if (stamp)`: opening a file that is not there fails; the mode argument of `sceIoOpen`, which the PC ignores; `fd < 1` and `fd <= 0`, a handle is never 0; the buffers `pboot[257]` of `scan.c` and `art.c`, which are only bigger) and 32 in lines of `add_eboot()` and `read_embedded()` that this change did not touch but that lie inside the ranges that were mutated:
    return values (`return 1` for 2 or 0: only a 0 matters, and a game folder with an `EBOOT.PBP` and a sub-folder with another is not in the fixtures), `sceIoOpen` modes and `fd` checks, `cat[8]`, `: 1` for `: 0` for an offset whose size is 0, `||` for `&&` in the header check, `size >= MAX_PNG_SIZE`, the umd branch of `read_embedded()`, `blur = 1` for 0 on a picture of one colour.

- **Manage Plugins: the Destination is the PLUGINS.TXT that is edited, and the plugin file stays where it is** (`plugins.c`, `plugins.h`, `settings.c`, `lang.c`; on request: "when you ask for destination, you should ask for the place you want to edit the lines in for the PLUGINS.TXT file, not where to paste the file; absolutely don't delete the file upon uninstalling, keep it where it is; edit the lines so they guide to the path where the file is resting;
  if the PLUGINS.TXT file is missing, create one").
  - *Before.* `plugins_install()` moved the plugin file into the folder of the place that was chosen (a rename on one storage, else a copy and the old file deleted) and wrote lines with the new path; the row was called "Location". Uninstalling already took only the lines out. *Now.* Nothing is moved, copied or deleted by any code in `plugins.c` (`copy_file()` and `move_file()` are gone; the new source check `plugkeep` greps for `sceIoRemove`, `sceIoRename`, `sceIoRmdir`, `copy_file`, `move_file`).
  - `plugins_install(src, loc, levels)`: `loc` is the PLUGINS.TXT to edit (`PluginLoc.file`; `.dir` is only the folder to make); the lines `<runlevel>, <src>, on` carry the path the file was chosen with, case kept, ms0: or ef0: as it is. Refused (nothing written, nothing kept in memory): no level, no file name, a file that is not there, a line over 191 characters, a PLUGINS.TXT that was only partly read, no room for the lines (96 a file), a file that could not be written.
    A PLUGINS.TXT that is not there is made with the first line, and its folder (`SEPLUGINS`) with it: `file_index()` makes an empty known file, `plugins_save()` makes the folder when the file cannot be opened. `PLUGIN_EXISTS` and `S_PM_EXISTS` ("A file with that name is already there") are gone; `lang.c` has 239 strings.
    `plugins_has(loc, path, level)` now takes the path of the plugin file, not its name: a file of the same name in another folder is another plugin file with its own lines.
  - `plugins_free_places()` (Install to): the places in the files that name the plugin as before, and now also every other PLUGINS.TXT of `plugins_locations()` that has no line for it, there or not (all three runlevels, with the path of the plugin's first line, which is where the file rests); a file that did not fit is not offered. A plugin with no line at all has no places (nowhere to say where it rests).
    So a plugin with an `always` line still has Install to for the other files.
  - *The page* (`pm_fill_new()`): File (the name), **Location** (where the file rests: `plugins_place_label()` of its path, e.g. "download (ms0:)"; it stays there), **PLUGINS.TXT** (the choice: "ARK_01234 (ms0:)", "SEPLUGINS (ms0:)" and the same on ef0:; the label is the file's name, not a string), then the switches XMB, Games, PS1 and Install. The question names the plugin and the places as before.
  - *Tests* (private). `cfg/cfg_test.c` (213 checks): the lines only; the file as it was and no copy; the PLUGINS.TXT and the folder made; the same file in two PLUGINS.TXT files; a file of the same name elsewhere; the refusals; a file that did not fit or has no room, with the lines also not kept in memory; a plugin on ms0: with its lines on ef0: (40000 bytes untouched); Uninstall leaves the file; the places (counts for every case, a missing file, a partial one, the lists of an earlier longer load not read).
    `settings/settings_test.c` (879 checks): the page with the new rows, the choice, the question, the lines written, Install to with the new places (the button found by position), a same-named file in SEPLUGINS, failures. The `t` group counts 239 strings. `plugkeep` is the new source check.
  - *Mutation checks* (`mut38_plugins_lines.py`, 109 changes of the new code of `plugins.c`, `settings.c` and `image.c`, 69 caught on the first run). 40 lived. Seven were holes and are closed by the tests above (a stale entry past the end of the list read as a line of the plugin, twice; lines kept in memory after an install that failed, three ways; a partial file offered as a place, two ways). Those seven and the two harmless ones next to them were run again with 16 changes of the cover limit in `image.c`: 23 of the 25 were caught.
    33 change nothing: the permission bits of `sceIoMkdir` / `sceIoOpen` (the PC ignores them), `fd < 0` as `fd < 1` or `<= 0` (a handle is never 0), the sizes of `dir[160]` and `rests[64]`, the guard `slash > dir + 4` and its neighbours (a failed `sceIoMkdir` of a bare device is harmless), `sizeof(s_files[1].path)`, `file_index(..., 1)` as `2` or as the first question, `-1` as `-2`, the limits that `add_line()` repeats (`strlen(src) + 15`, `> LINE_LEN`, `room > MAX_LINES` without `partial`, `k <= PLUGIN_LEVELS`),
    `loc->file[1]` for `[0]` (a path is never one character), `ok = 1` as the start (there is always a place), `Item.immediate` 2 for 1, and the three boundaries of `MAX_FILES` that need more than five known files (4 places and an ARK folder somewhere else are all there can be).

- **Memory: the heap leaves 8 MB free, and a big progressive JPEG cover is judged by the memory it needs** (`main.c`, `image.c`; on request: "this is the free memory while the launcher runs, row 2 is RAM, we only have about 600 KB free: significantly improve the RAM management without losing any functionality").
  - *What a PSP-1000 showed.* Partition 2 (the user memory, 24 MB): TOTALFREE 618496, MAXFREE 507904 while the launcher ran (partition 5, the 4 MB at 0x08400000, was free and is not used).
  - *The cause is the heap setting.* `main.c` had `PSP_HEAP_SIZE_KB(-7168)` with the comment "leave 7 MB free". In the SDK this is built with (pspdev v20261001, `_sbrk` in `libcglue.a`, read in the disassembly) a negative or missing `sce_newlib_heap_kb_size` means "the heap is `sceKernelMaxFreeMemSize()` minus `sce_newlib_heap_threshold_kb_size` KB", and the
    threshold is 512 KB (the value 0xFFF80000 is added) when the program does not declare `PSP_HEAP_THRESHOLD_SIZE_KB`. The magnitude of the negative number is not used at all. So the heap took all the free memory but 512 KB (the 507904 and the 618496 above), and the 7 MB that were meant for ARK's `RECOVERY.PRX` (it needs 4 MB; psp5 loads it with
    `kuKernelLoadModule`), the AV decoder modules, the network modules (`sceUtilityLoadNetModule`, `sceNetInit` with a pool of 256 KB), the USB modules and the stacks of the threads (16 to 64 KB each, `sceKernelCreateThread`) did not exist. Nothing was measured of what these took, so it is not known which of them failed; the Recovery menu is the one that surely could.
  - *The fix.* `PSP_HEAP_SIZE_KB(-1)` (all of it) and `PSP_HEAP_THRESHOLD_SIZE_KB(8192)`: the heap is the largest free block minus 8 MB, about 13 MB on a PSP-1000 where it was about 21. 8 MB is the 4 MB of the Recovery menu, about 2.5 MB of modules, network and stacks, and room. Checked in the built ELF: `sce_newlib_heap_kb_size` = 0xFFFFFFFF and `sce_newlib_heap_threshold_kb_size` = 0x2000
    (the build before: 0xFFFFE400, and no threshold symbol). **Not tried on a PSP**; the maintainer's table is the test: the free memory should be about 8 MB less what the modules and stacks took. Nothing in the program is smaller or turned off.
  - *Is 13 MB enough?* Estimated from the code, not measured (a PSP-1000 would have to say): the heap holds the cards' icons (up to 37 of 46 KB: `KEEP_RANGE` 18 each way), the backgrounds (3 of 234 KB), the sounds (0.7 MB, 0.3 MB once the start sound is let go), the game list (428 bytes a game) and the music list (492 bytes a track), the fonts (intraFont with `CACHE_ALL`; the Japanese font only when needed;
    their size is not known) and the pictures being decoded. About 6 to 8 MB with a library of some hundred games and songs, 8 to 10 MB with a thousand of each. The program image (1.9 MB: 1.2 MB code, 0.67 MB fixed buffers, `psp-size`) is not in the heap. A cover that is a PNG of 1024 x 1024 takes 4 MB while it is decoded, plus its file (up to 2 MB, `MAX_COVER_TAG`).
  - *Progressive JPEG covers.* libjpeg keeps every coefficient of a progressive picture before it makes a pixel: 128 bytes a block in every component, nothing to do with the size the cover is scaled to. Measured with libjpeg-turbo 3.1.4 (Pillow; the toolchain has 3.1.3), peak extra memory while decoding, files of 0.3 to 1.3 MB: baseline 1000 x 1000: 0.8 MB; progressive 1000 x 1000: 3.8 MB (colours at half size) or 6.6 MB (full);
    1600 x 1600: 8.4 or 15.6 MB. The old limit was the number of pixels (`MAX_PROGRESSIVE_PIXELS`, 1600 x 1600), which with a 21 MB heap was allowed to take most of it, and with 13 MB can take all of it: a malloc that fails in libjpeg ends in `jpeg_fail` (no cover), but the icon loader that asks at the same moment gets NULL and marks that icon `SLOT_MISSING` for good.
    Now `progressive_bytes()` adds the blocks of the components (`comp_info[i].width_in_blocks * height_in_blocks`, valid after `jpeg_read_header`) times 128, in 64 bits, and `decode_jpeg()` refuses a progressive picture over `MAX_PROGRESSIVE_BYTES` (5 MB) before it allocates: about 1300 x 1300 with the colours at half size, 930 x 930 with all of them. **This is a loss for a rare cover:** a progressive 1400 x 1400 was shown before and is not now.
    Everything that is not progressive is read a row at a time and is as it was (`MAX_COVER_PIXELS` still holds). Not done, and why: reading a PNG a row at a time, scaling as it goes, would take the 4 MB of a big PNG cover down to a few hundred KB, but an interlaced PNG cannot be read that way and it is more code than this change; a memory readout in Information (the maintainer has his own tool).
  - *Tests* (private). New source check `heap` in `run_all.sh` (the heap is -1 and the threshold between 6 and 12 MB; 8 changes, 6 caught and the two limits themselves are allowed), new group `jpeg` (`image/jpeg_budget_test.c`, 15 checks, with a stand-in for libjpeg that answers with the header the test sets: not progressive has no limit; exactly 5 MB (40960 blocks) is decoded and one block more is refused before anything is allocated;
    the blocks of all components are added and only the components there are; real sizes (1300 / 1400 at half colours, 900 / 950 at full, a grey 1600); sums that would wrap in 32 bits). Mutation checks of the new lines in `image.c`: 16 changes, all caught.

- **The Library's background waits for the game's art** (`ui.c`; on request, after a test on a PSP-1000 with Show Game art on: moving from the Library card to the card next to it showed the theme background faintly before the picture, which was dark. "The library background waits until the game's art appears, just like it works normally for the theme background; with no art keep the blurred icon as before, and with no icon either fall back to the theme background instead of black").
  - *What was wrong.* A trace of the real code with a stand-in loader (brightness of the screen, 0..1): Library card 0.05 → dip 0.04 → the surface 0.20 for as long as the picture took (it is asked for after `BG_DELAY` 0.18 s, then loaded) → the picture fades in over it (a dark one: down to 0.035). Only a move from the Library card showed it: between two game cards the old picture cross-fades into the new one and the surface is never seen.
    A card with no picture at all stays on the surface (0.20); it never went black. The chain for a game's background is unchanged and in `art_load_bg()`: its own `PIC1`, else its `ICON0` blurred (`blur` 10), else NULL.
  - *Now.* In `update_background()`, `want` (which background lies under the pictures) is 1 also when the Library's is under now (`bg_lib`), the Games tab is on a game (`sel >= 0`: Show Game art is on) and the loader has not answered for it (`bg_index != sel`; `loader_take_bg()` sets `bg_index` when it hands back the picture, or NULL for a game with nothing). So the Library's background stays under the card while the picture is awaited, however long that takes
    and whichever cards you move over. When the picture arrives it fades in over the Library's background (as over the surface); the swap to the surface is then made unseen when it covers the screen (new branch `else if (ui->bg_cur)`: while a picture is fading in there is no dip and no swap). When the loader answers with no picture (or Show Game art is off), `want` is 0 and the swap is the dip, as before.
  - *Tests* (private, `ui/menu_test.c`, 1120 checks; `ui/stubs.c` has `g_stub_bg_none` for the loader's "no picture" answer; `NO_ART()`): the Library's background stays for 1.5 s with no answer, no dip, and the picture is asked for; on to another game, back to the Library card, game number 0: the same; the art fades in over it with the background showing through (checked against `tint * alpha + background * (1 - alpha)`) and the swap at fade 1.0; black art never lighter than the Library's background on the way (no flash of the surface); no picture at all: the dip and the surface, and from there another game stays on the surface; Show Game art off after that: the dip, no waiting.
    The old Library-card tests that expected a dip with no answer from the loader now give the answer first.
  - *Mutation checks* (`mut40_library_wait_for_art.py`): 23 changes of the new lines: 18 caught on the first run, the game-number-0 test was added for `sel >= 1` and `sel > 0`, and 3 change nothing (`tab == TAB_GAMES` before the test, as the Music tab has no cards, and the bounds of a value that only falls).

- **The Library card shows the Library's own background** (`ui.c`, `ui.h`; on request: make sure the Library card shows the same background as the Library itself, so that it is fluid). Before, the Library card (`HOME_LIBRARY`, `ui_selected()` = -1) had no picture to show, so the Games tab drew the theme's surface behind it,
  and opening the Library jumped to the Library's drawn background and faded it up from black (`tab_anim` 0.4 → 1). Now what lies **under the pictures** of the Games tab is chosen by the card: `ui->bg_lib` = 1 on the Library card, 0 on any other card and in Music (the Library tab itself always draws its own and keeps `bg_lib` = 1).
  - *Only one picture fits in video memory.* `theme_backdrop()` and `theme_backdrop_library()` share the one snapshot in VRAM (`scene_ready()` draws it again when the other one is asked for), so the two cannot be mixed in a frame without drawing a scene twice every frame (the Library's alone is 42112 bytes of the 128 KB display list and some 500 draw calls) or holding a second picture in video memory (which by my count of the 2 MB has no room next to the frame buffers; not tried).
    So a change of `bg_lib` is made where it cannot be seen. `update_background()` (the block before the fade of the pictures is advanced, so that a picture that covers the screen at that moment still counts as covering) makes it **at once when `bg_cover()` is 1** (a picture is up, or one is changing to another, or one has just started to fade out: it hides the base), else it **dips**: `bg_dip` rises at `BG_DIP` 8 a second to 1, the swap is made in the dark
    (`bg_lib = want`), and it falls again, 8 updates down and 8 up at 60 frames a second; `draw_background()` lays a black rectangle of `255 * bg_dip` over the whole background (and only the background) while `bg_dip` > 0. `bg_cover()` is also what the shades of a picture use (it was the same expression in `draw_background()` before).
  - *Opening and closing.* `set_tab()` sets `lib_calm` = 1 when the Library is opened with `bg_lib` = 1 and no dip left: the Library's black fade-in (`tab_anim`, the part of `draw_background()` for the Library) is then skipped, because its background is already on the screen; it is cleared when `tab_anim` reaches 1, so a later change of the Library's cards (a new sort) comes up out of the dark as before.
    Opened before the swap was made (✕ in the very update that reached the card, or while the black is on its way out) it comes up out of the dark as before. ○ goes back to the Library card with `bg_lib` still 1 and no dip. `ui_init()` sets `bg_lib` from the first card, so a first start with nothing to play (the Library card alone) starts on its background without a dip.
  - *What it looks like.* Game with a picture → Library card: the picture fades out over the Library's background (no dip). A game with no picture at all (the loader answers with none), a game card → Library card with nothing to hide the swap, Show Game art off, and to Music: a quarter of a second of dip. (Library card → a game whose picture is still to come is not one of them: it waits, see the entry *The Library's background waits for the game's art* above.) The first frame of the Games tab with the Library card chosen costs 45888 bytes of the display list (the Library's first frame costs 47056).
    (The first version swapped to the surface at once when the Library card was left, so the surface showed for the time the picture took to load: a PSP-1000 showed it as a faint theme background before a dark picture. That is what the entry above changes.)
  - *Tests* (private, `ui/menu_test.c`; 1096 checks in all, in every theme tried and with Show Game art on and off): on the Library card the strip at the right is the Library's picture, not the surface, and no picture is asked for; to a game card the dip (swap in frame 7, black over the whole screen including the corners, back at frame 15, never rising after the swap) and then the surface; a dip of 0.5 is a black of 127/255; ✕ on the card opens the Library calmly (no black at any step, `lib_calm` cleared when it has settled);
    ○ back with no dip; opened in the very update that reached the card, or with the dip not over: with the black as before; a picture up: the swap at once, no dip, the Library's background left when it is gone; a picture only partly covering does not hide the swap; a picture half faded in lies over the background, which shows through it (the surface on a game card, the Library's picture on the Library card);
    a first start with only the Library card; the display list. The old check of a fading-out picture's shade is stronger (half the strength at half). The old checks of the Games tab and the Library background needed no change (they test a game card).
  - *Mutation checks* (`mut37_library_card_bg.py`): 76 changes of the new lines (55 from the generator, 21 by hand); the first run left 16, the tests above were added for the ones that were real gaps (a half-faded picture hiding the base, the black not covering the edges, ✕ in the very update, the dip left over when the Library opens, a partly covering picture hiding the swap, the shade of a fading-out picture) and 67 are caught in all.
    The 9 that live change nothing that can be seen: the upper clamp of a value that falls (`0, 2`), the black fade of the Library at `tab_anim` = 1 (`< 2`, `<= 1`: it is black of 0), the base drawn under a picture that hides it completely (`bg_fade < 2`, `<= 1`, `||`), the black rectangle drawn with the strength 0 (`bg_dip >= 0`), and the two conditions that were already so (`tab == TAB_GAMES` before the Library card test: Music has no cards; `tab == TAB_LIBRARY ||` in `want`: the Library has its own branch).
    *Seen once, not explained:* in one of some fifteen runs of the `menu` program the old check "a picture shows as it is where nothing shades it" (and the one after it) failed with every sampled pixel dark; it did not come back in the other runs, nothing in `src/` had changed between them and the new code cannot dip there. Watch for it.

- **The File Manager lists the internal storage once when `ms0:` is the internal storage** (`files.c`; on request: fix the File Manager the same way as the game scan, the music scan, the plugins and the rest). `open_list()` listed `ms0:/` and `ef0:/` whenever each could be opened,
  so with ARK pointing `ms0:` at the internal storage (`sys_ms_is_ef()`, a PSP Go started from the internal storage) both names opened the same storage and the list would have shown it twice, the first as "Memory Stick" (found by reading the code, not seen on a PSP).
  Now `ms0:/` is left out when `ef0:/` can be opened and `sys_ms_is_ef()` says that `ms0:` is the internal storage: one storage, listed as `ef0:/` (System Storage; the game scan keeps `ef0:` too, because that is the path a game has to get), and `enter()` goes into it at once, with no list of storages.
  With no `ef0:/` that can be opened, `ms0:/` stays whatever `sys_ms_is_ef()` says. The plugin picker (`files_open_pick()`) uses the same list.
  - *Tests* (private). `files/popup_test.c` and `files/files_test.c` stub `sys_ms_is_ef()` now. New checks in `popup_test.c` (506 in all): `ms0:` redirected with `ef0:/` open: one drive, `ef0:/`, no list, Back leaves; no Memory Stick (`ms0` moved away): the internal storage alone and entered at once, also with the redirect; redirected but no `ef0:/`: `ms0:/` stays; the plugin picker with the redirect.
    The source check `efrule` of `run_all.sh` asks for the two lines of `open_list()` now.
  - *Mutation checks.* 15 changes (4 from the generator, 11 by hand: the redirect ignored, tested without `ef`, `||` for `&&`, `ms0:` never or always listed, `ef0:` hidden when redirected, `ef` wrong, always or never set, the model check put back). 14 were caught. The one that lived is `s_repeat_dir = 0` as `1`, the key-repeat reset on the next line of `open_list()`: not part of this change.

- **The PS Vita, two zips, the sounds next to the EBOOT, the internal storage by `ef0:` alone** (`system.c` / `system.h`, `sound.c`, `files.c`, `plugins.c`, `tools/make_release.py`, `tools/release-readme.txt`; on request: merge the PSP Go version into the others and let the launcher just look for `ef0:`;
  only two releases (ARK-4 and ARK-5); the PS Vita works, so it is a supported device; the sounds lie with the EBOOT in the launcher folder, with no extra folder; a longer `READ ME.txt` for the zips with the steps of every device).
  - *The internal storage (`ef0:`).* Three places asked `sys_model() == 4` before they looked at `ef0:`: `open_list()` in `files.c` (the File Manager and the plugin picker), `other_root()` in `plugins.c` (the second pair of `PLUGINS.TXT` files and the places a plugin can be installed to) and `sys_about()` in
    `system.c` (the *System Storage* rows of Information). Each of them already had the question that matters (`dev_present("ef0:/")`, `dir_exists(probe)`, `device_space("ef0:", ...)`), so the model check is simply gone and the comments say that nothing else is asked. Everything else worked that way before:
    `scan.c` (`ef0:/ISO`, `ef0:/PSP/GAME`), `music.c`, `saves.c`, `ftp.c` (`dir_exists("ef0:/")`), `storage.c` and `gameops.c` try `ef0:` on every device and get nothing where it is not. What stays tied to the model is hardware, not storage: `umd.c` (the Go has no UMD drive) and the Go-only block of ARK settings in `settings.c`.
    The rule is "there when it can be opened". Two names for one storage is still handled by `sys_ms_is_ef()` (ARK points `ms0:` at the internal storage), as before. One difference on the other models: `sys_about()` now calls `sceIoDevctl("ef0:", ...)` there too (it used to stop at the model check); `storage.c` already did that on every model. *(Later: Console Information no longer has a row for either storage, and `sys_about()` asks neither: see the Console Information entry at the top.)*
    **Not tried on a PSP.** A PSP-1000/2000/3000 and a Vita should answer "no" for `ef0:`, as they do for `scan.c`; if some device answered for `ef0:` it would now be shown as a second storage, which is what was asked for.
  - *The sounds* (`sound.c`, `system.c`). `sys_set_root()` also sets `g_self_dir`, the folder psp5 was started from (`argv[0]` up to the last slash, `ms0:/PSP/GAME/PSP5`; empty when there is no slash or the folder does not fit the 128 bytes). `open_wav(name)` tries three places, in this order, and the first file that opens is the one
    (a bad file there is the built-in sound, as before; the next places are not tried): `<g_self_dir>/<name>.wav` (next to `EBOOT.PBP`, also when the folder has been renamed), `<g_root>/PSP/GAME/PSP5/<name>.wav` (psp5 started as ARK's launcher is `VBOOT.PBP` in `ARK_01234`, which has no sounds; `g_root` is `ef0:` on the internal storage)
    and `<g_data_dir>/sounds/<name>.wav` (where 1.2.0 and older had them: an update that only replaces `EBOOT.PBP` keeps its sounds). `g_data_dir` is still `<root>/PSP5` for `psp5.cfg`, the cache, the covers and the played times; only the sounds moved. The zips put the five files in `PSP/GAME/PSP5` (`tools/make_release.py`), and `tools/convert_sounds.py` says so.
  - *The zips* (`tools/make_release.py`, `tools/release-readme.txt`). Two zips, `psp5-authentic-v<version>-ARK5.zip` and `-ARK4.zip`; the PSP Go zips are gone (their `EBOOT.PBP` was the same file, only the `READ ME.txt` and the name differed). Each holds `PSP/GAME/PSP5/EBOOT.PBP`, the five sounds beside it, `ARK_LAUNCHER/VBOOT.PBP` (the same file) and `READ ME.txt`.
    The READ ME is the file `tools/release-readme.txt` with `@VERSION@`, `@ARK@`, `@OTHER_ARK@`, `@OTHER_ZIP@` and `@VITA_NOTE@` filled in (a place that is left over stops the script), written in Windows line ends and plain ASCII with lines of 72 characters at most. It has seven parts: is it the right zip, unzip, copy (a part each for the PSP-1000/2000/3000, the PSP Go and the PS Vita),
    ARK's launcher (needed on the Vita), the first start and where games go, updating, something went wrong. `INSTALL.md` has the same steps for the three kinds of device; `README.md` says "Supported devices". CI (`.github/workflows/build.yml`) runs the same script.
  - *What the maintainer said about the Vita* (the steps in the READ ME and in `INSTALL.md` come from this): the PS Vita 1000 and 2000, ARK-5 as the standalone install (`FasterARK_psvita.vpk` from `github.com/PSP-Arkfive/FasterARK`); the `PSP` folder (and the `PSP5` folder, as the sounds were in it then) went to `ux0:pspemu/` and `VBOOT.PBP` to `ux0:pspemu/PSP/SAVEDATA/ARK_01234`.
    ARK-4 on the Vita is not confirmed. The READ ME of the ARK-4 zip says so and sends a Vita owner to the ARK-5 zip.
  - *Tests* (private). `files/popup_test.c` (two drives with a model that says "not a Go", no list of drives when `ef0:/` can not be opened with the model 1 and 4, the plugin picker's two storages, and the ordinary File Manager again once `ef0` is taken away), `cfg/cfg_test.c` (the four `PLUGINS.TXT` files read on a model 2 when `ef0:/` can be opened; only `ms0:` when it can not, on a model 4 and 2, with `ef0` moved away and back; the places a plugin can be installed to the same way),
    `sound/sound_test.c` (the files now go to `PSP/GAME/PSP5`; the new `places()`: only the old place, the game folder before the old place, a renamed folder of its own before the game folder, ARK's launcher, an unknown folder, a bad file in the first place refusing instead of trying the next, the internal storage with its own `PSP/GAME/PSP5` and not the Memory Stick's, all gone),
    `units/selfdir_test.c` (new; `sys_set_root()` cut out of `system.c` by `run_all.sh`: an app, ARK's launcher, `ef0:` with capitals, a renamed folder with a space, the top of the storage, no file name, no slash, an empty path, no path, an earlier folder never staying, 127 bytes kept and 128 not), the source check `efrule` (new, in `run_all.sh`: `system.c` cannot run on a PC, so its `has_ef` line is read, and `files.c` / `plugins.c` must not contain `sys_model` at all),
    and `release/release_test.py` (new, the group `release`: the script run on fake builds in a throwaway folder, 88 checks: the two names, every file in each zip and nothing else, the right build in each, the sounds equal to `res/sounds` and the five names `sound.c` loads, the READ ME's line ends, ASCII, width, places, parts and wording (no "tested"), a missing build stopping the script, and the docs naming no PSP Go zip and saying that the Vita works).
  - *Mutation checks.* Changes of the new code one at a time: 31 in `sound.c` (`open_wav`), `system.c` (`sys_set_root`, `has_ef`), `files.c` and `plugins.c` (`other_root`), 8 + 5 + 4 from the generator and 14 by hand (the model check put back in each of the three places, the second storage always or never there, the wrong device name, the order of the three sound places, the root fixed to `ms0:`, the own folder tried when empty, the old place gone).
    27 were caught. The 4 that lived change nothing: `strncasecmp(g_root, "ef0:", 5 or 3)` (the root is `ms0:` or `ef0:`, which differ at the first letter) and `char probe[8]` as 9 or 7 (`"ms0:/"` is 6 bytes with its end). For the release script, READ ME and docs 24 changes (each made in a copy): every one that changes where a file goes, a name, the build used, the line ends, the Vita text, the ARK named, a left-over place, a missing build and the docs was caught;
    three were not and are not holes: `VBOOT_ARKMENU.PBP` and `PSP5/sounds` are each said twice in the READ ME (the test asks that they are said at all), and the first try of the Vita wording in `INSTALL.md` / `README.md` was too loose (the word is in other sentences too) and was made to look at the paragraph that has to say it. `system.c` itself is not run on a PC: `has_ef` and `sys_set_root()` are checked as above; the PSP's own answer for `ef0:` is not.

- **The Library has a background of its own, drawn by the program** (`theme.c` / `theme.h`, `gfx.c` / `gfx.h`, `ui.c`; on request, with a render of a twisted ribbon of fine dotted lines as the example: "render, not use, like the themes and icons").
  - *What is drawn* (`theme.c`, the section *The Library's own background*). `lib_scene()` draws the picture, `theme_backdrop_library(t, time)` shows it and adds the little star (`lib_star()`, the only thing that moves). The coordinates are fractions of the screen (`lib_x()`, `lib_y()`).
    The picture is a black gradient (the theme's `surf_top` / `surf_bottom` at 16% / 10%), two glows added to it (the copper light above the crown), the **petal** (19 curves, each a cubic Bézier from the crown to the foot, `lib_petal_point()`, 24 points, fine lines
    with `lib_hair()`, a seed that is fixed), the rims (`lib_rims()`: the left rim, the foot and the crown ring, with `gfx_stroke()`), the **fan** (`lib_fan()`: 15 lines to the tip and two strokes), nine big **rings** at the bottom right (`lib_rings()`, turned by -0.45 rad), faint **sweeps**
    (`lib_sweeps()`) and **dust** (`lib_dust()`: 80 specks on the right, 240 on the petal, 46 along its copper edge). The same picture in every theme.
  - *Colours* (`lib_palette()`): the fine lines are white mixed 28% with the theme's `beam`, the warm light is `dust` (the gold of the PS5 colours) or the `accent` for PS1 to PS4 (their `dust` is 0), the bright edges are white mixed 14% with the warm light, and the black has the trace of `surf_top` / `surf_bottom`.
  - *One picture in video memory.* `scene_ready(t, a, library)` is what `theme_backdrop()` did first, now with `s_scene.library` in the key: the surface and the Library's picture share the one snapshot (`gfx_snapshot_*`), and the one that is wanted is drawn when the tab changes (a tab change is the only time; in a frame
    after that it is one image). The first frame in the Library asks 42112 bytes of the 128 KB display list for the picture, 47056 for the whole frame (cards and text), 48480 with the Sort popup open.
  - *`gfx_polyline()`* (new, `gfx.c`): a `GU_LINE_STRIP` through the points, 1 px, a colour at each point, 16 bytes of the display list a point (`gfx_stroke()` is the smooth one: six vertices a point). The thin lines of the picture use it. The mock GE draws a line strip one pixel at a time.
  - *`ui.c`.* `draw_background()` has a Library branch: `theme_backdrop_library()`, and black over it of `255 * (1 - smooth(tab_anim))` while `tab_anim` (0.4 to 1 when a tab is opened) is below 1, so that it comes up out of the dark with the cards; it returns before the surface and the game's picture. `update_background()`
    makes `sel` -1 in the Library: no picture is asked for, one that is up fades out and is freed, one that arrives is freed (the same path as *Show Game art* off); the Games tab asks again when it is opened. `draw_dim()` darkens only Music now (the Library is dark already). *Show Game art* is for the Games tab.
  - *A bug found by the tests, fixed.* `sinf(PI)` is a little below 0, and `powf()` of that is NaN. The last point of a hair, of a sweep and the last speck of the copper edge had an alpha of NaN; `(int)NaN` is 0 on a PC (the point was invisible by chance, and the last speck never showed) but the MIPS gives
    the biggest `int`, which would have made these points opaque on a PSP. `lib_col()` treats NaN as 0 and `lib_bell()` cuts the sine at 0. The theme test is built with `-fsanitize=float-cast-overflow` (`run_all.sh`), which stops at such a cast: it stopped at the old code. The last speck of the edge is drawn now.
  - *Tests* (private, not in git): `gfx/gfxtest.c` `test_polyline()` (the vertices, the colours from point to point, fewer than two points draw nothing, 16 bytes a point, one draw call), `gfx/theme_test.c` (460 checks; the Library has a section of its own: the picture is not the surface and is the same in every theme except for its colours, the black / lines / glow follow the theme
    (PS5 copper, Cobalt Blue blue, PS2 the accent), the star is the brightest place, the tip of the fan and the foot of the petal are lit, only the star moves from one frame to the next, the surface and the Library alternate with the same pictures, a **fingerprint of the picture** in all 13 themes (mean colours of 4 x 4 pixel blocks, 6 bits a colour) and of three of them
    to the last bit of every pixel (when the picture is changed on purpose, run the test once, read the new values from the failing lines and put them in), the draw calls that are added (8 in the first frame, 6 after: two glows and the star; the mock now counts them, `g_blend_add` / `g_draws_add`, because it mixes everything by alpha), the mode is back
    to the usual one), `ui/menu_test.c` (39 new checks in three themes: the Library's background is its own picture and not darkened, not the surface, Music is still the Games background darkened; a picture that is up when the Library is opened is not drawn and is let go; no request for a picture in the Library, however long it stands still; one that arrives is thrown away;
    the Games tab asks again; the black over the background at `tab_anim` 0.4, 0.6, 0.8, 0.95 and none at 1; the first frame fits the list with room to spare), `ui/stubs.c` (`loader_request_bg()` and `loader_take_bg()` record and hand out), the whole-pixel checks of `ui/pixel_test.c` and the frame of the Library's Sort button in `ui/menu_test.c` are unchanged (the crown ring, the fan and the glow of the picture lie clear of that button: its frame is measured against what is behind it).
  - *Mutation checks* (private, `mutation-batches/mut34*.py`; every number and comparison of the new code): `gfx_polyline()` 10, 8 caught; **not caught, nothing changes (2):** the `z` of a vertex (1 instead of 0: the GE draws in 2D) and the index pointer of `sceGuDrawArray()` (1 instead of 0: there is no index in the vertex type).
    `theme.c` 481, 431 caught. **Not caught, nothing changes (50):** the alpha byte of the palette colours and of the star's white (`lib_col()` replaces it; 5), the clamp of `lib_col()` at 1 (no alpha reaches 1; 5), arrays that are bigger than they have to be, `xy[1 * LIB_PTS]` (48 floats are what 24 points need; 18), loops that write one point more than is drawn (5), `<` against `<=` on a random float (2),
    the soft flag and the adding flag as 2 (they are tested for true; 7), the clamp of `scaled()` at 255 (4; its value is at most 41), 0.1 px of the length of a ray of the star (the mock draws in whole pixels; 1), and `s_scene.valid` and the first `||` of the condition of `scene_ready()` (3; the surface's own, from before: the mock keeps the picture, so forgetting it
    changes nothing it can see).
    `ui.c` 24, in the three places that changed (`update_background()`, the Library branch of `draw_background()`, `draw_dim()`): 15 caught. **Not caught (9):** `-2` instead of `-1` for `sel` (it is only asked if below 0), the black over the background drawn at `tab_anim` 1 (`<= 1` or `< 2`: its alpha is 0; 2), and six in lines that were not changed (the Games branch of `draw_background()`: 4, and where the darkening of Music starts: 2).
    Two holes found and closed: the fade check was too wide for the steps of the black (one step is 0.004 of what is under it; it is 0.0001 now), and the corners of the fade were not looked at.
- **FTP: the network is started like ARK's launcher does it, and a hint for WLAN Power Save** (`netlink.c` / `netlink.h`, `wifi.c`, `ftpscreen.c`, `lang.c` / `lang.h`; on request: a computer said "No route to host" although the PSP showed its address).
  - *What ARK does* (read in `PSP-Archive/ARK-4`: `extras/menus/arkMenu/src/net_mgr.cpp`, `network.cpp`, `extras/modules/pspftp`, `contrib/PSP/GAME/pspftpd`; the repository of ARK-5's launcher could not be found, ARK-5 says it moved to `PSP-Arkfive/ARK-5`, which is the CFW only): `initializeNetwork()` loads the net modules, `sceNetInit(256 * 1024, 42, 0, 42, 0)`, `sceNetInetInit`, `sceNetResolverInit`,
    `sceNetApctlInit(10 * 1024, 48)` and `sceNetApctlAddHandler(handler)` with a handler that does nothing; the Wi-Fi is joined with the netconf dialog (`type 3`, the last connection; psp5 authentic keeps the list, `type 0`, so the player chooses); the address is `sceNetApctlGetInfo(8)`; the server is
    a thread (priority 0x18, kernel) that binds 0.0.0.0:21 and waits in `accept`, a thread for each client. psp5 authentic had `sceNetInit(0x20000, 0x20, 0x1000, 0x20, 0x1000)` and `sceNetApctlInit(0x1600, 42)` (the numbers of the SDK's samples) and no handler.
  - *What changed*: `netlink_start()` uses ARK's numbers and registers an empty `apctl_event()` handler (a failing `sceNetApctlAddHandler` is not a failure; `sceNetApctlDelHandler` is called in `netlink_stop()` only for a handler that was added, before `sceNetApctlTerm`). There is no `sceNetResolverInit`: nothing here looks a name up, and linking `libpspnet_resolver` would
    risk the "stubs out of order" of `libpspnet_inet` (see `netstub.S`). `wifi_dialog()` (the Wi-Fi connection row) is now `netlink_start()`, the dialog, `netlink_stop()`: one place starts the network libraries (it had its own copy with the old numbers). **Not done**: the netconf type 3, a thread for the server (a "No route to host" is the computer's `ARP` question
    that nobody answers, which no program does, so the server is not where it is decided).
  - *What is not known*: the cause. "No route to host" comes from the computer's side before any TCP: the PSP did not answer the ARP question. The PSP-FTPD documentation says to switch off **WLAN Power Save** of the PSP's settings, and Sony's manuals say the same for a connection that does not work: the system then
    talks "at full power". `netlink_power_save()` reads `PSP_SYSTEMPARAM_ID_INT_WLAN_POWERSAVE` (3) with `sceUtilityGetSystemParamInt()` (1 = on; a failed call, a missing value or another number is "not on"). `ftpscreen.c` asks it once, when the server has started (`s_power_save`, forgotten by `ftpscreen_open()`), and while the server waits with
    no computer connected it draws the hint `S_FTP_POWER_SAVE` in two lines (380 px wide at most, at y 224 and 237, size 0.5, orange; above the capsule) under "Waiting for a computer". The texts are Claude's, not checked by native speakers. psp5 authentic cannot switch the setting off itself (the SDK only reads it; the registry key is the system's). INSTALL.md has the list of
    what else to check (the same network, no client isolation, `ping`). **Nothing of this has been run on a PSP.**
  - *Tests* (private, not in git): `ftp/netlink_test.c` (62 checks: the numbers and the order, the handler (added after the access point control, deleted first, number 0 is a handler, a failing add is not a failure and nothing is deleted then, a second start adds it again), the power save (on, off, 2, -1, a failing call, a call that gives no value, the number 3 is asked)), `ftp/wifi_test.c` (35: both
    functions with the real `netlink.c`: the new numbers and the handler in the order of start and stop), `ftp/ftpscreen_test.c` (140: asked once when the server starts, the hint in two lines at the places, not with a computer, not while sending, not when the setting was off at the start, not after `ftpscreen_open()` again, not when no server runs; the golden file has two new states in 11 languages and 3 test fonts: the old
    lines are all still there), `ftp/psp_inc` (stand-ins: `psputility_sysparam.h`, the handler calls). The old `wifi/wifi_test.c` (the first test of `wifi_dialog()`, with stand-ins for everything) is retired (`wifi_test.c.retired`): `wifi_dialog()` goes through `netlink.c` now and `ftp/wifi_test.c` tests it with the real one.
  - *Mutation checks* (private, `mutation-batches/mut33*.py`; 61 changes of `netlink.c` and `ftpscreen.c`; `wifi.c` has none left that is a number or a comparison): one hole found and closed (a call that says yes and gives no value). Not caught, nothing changes (10): the "not started" values `-1` against `-2` in `netlink.c` (the tests are `>= 0`; 7), the size of the two lines in `ftpscreen.c` (`[3][96]` for `[2][96]`, the second argument of `text_wrap()` is 2) and the width of 380 against 379 and 381
    (the plain mock measures 6 px a letter: 63 letters fit either way).
- **Library cards without corners, names that scroll, straight bars, held keys in popups, one text size** (`gfx.c` / `gfx.h`, `ui.c` / `ui.h`, `saves.c`, `files.c`, `settings.c`, `art.c` / `art.h`, `loader.c` / `loader.h`, `power.c`; on request, with a picture of the console's Storage page as the example).
  - *Square cards* (`ui.c`). `draw_card()` has `r = grid ? 0 : w / 12`, and `card_plate()` draws a `gfx_rect()` when `r` is 0. The ring in the grid is `gfx_frame(l - 1, t - 1, rt - l + 2, b - t + 2, 2, ...)` (one pixel outside the card, one inside, a straight edge); the Games tab keeps `gfx_edge_ring()`. `SELECT_GROW_W` and `SELECT_GROW_H` are gone: the selected card of the grid is `sw x shh` like the others (only the start animation of a game still grows it).
  - *Square pictures* (`art.c`, `loader.c`). The cache holds the picture before the corners are cut (`ntx_save()` comes first), so one `.ico` serves both tabs. `art_load_icon()` cuts the corners afterwards (`tex_round_corners()`), the new `art_load_icon_square()` does not. `IconSlot.round` says which one a slot has. `loader_set_round()` (called every frame by `ui_update()` with `ui->tab != TAB_LIBRARY`) sets what the loader makes from now on, and `loader_focus()` frees every READY slot of the other kind, so a change of tab makes the cards near the focus again (the others when they come near). `loader_start()` sets the rounded kind again. The Library card and the folder cards are drawn by `ui.c` and have no picture to load.
  - *One text size* (`ui.c`). `TITLE_SIZE` 0.66 is the size of a game's name (Games and Library), of *Play* / *Open* and of the time played (`PILL_SIZE`); the tag (PSP, APP ...) is 0.5 bold in a 15 px box in both tabs. The old numbers were the same for *Play* and the time played already; the name next to the big card had a size of its own that shrank (`Ui.label_size` is always `TITLE_SIZE` now, `fit_label()` only sets the room).
  - *Names that scroll* (`gfx.c`). `text_marquee_offset(overflow, t)` is 0 for `MARQUEE_WAIT` (1.2 s), then goes `MARQUEE_SPEED` (36) pixels a second to the end, stays `MARQUEE_END` (1.0 s), and starts again. `text_draw_marquee(x, y, size, bold, color, room, s, t)` is `text_draw()` when the text fits; else it cuts to `room` with `sceGuScissor()` **inside the clip that is set** (`gfx_clip()` / `gfx_clip_reset()` now remember it in `s_clip_*`), draws the text at `x - floorf(offset + 0.5)` (whole pixels) and puts the old scissor back. The time belongs to the caller: `Ui.label_t` (restarts in `fit_label()` when the selected card changes), `Ui.lib_label_t` (restarts when the card changes, not when the room changes because of the clock), `S.name_t` / `S.name_for` in `saves.c` (`Save.main_room` and `Save.name_over` come from `lay_row()`; the title after a scrolling name starts at `main_room + 8`) and `s_name_t` / `s_name_cur` in `files.c`. Only the highlighted row scrolls; the others keep the cut name with "...". Settings rows and popup labels still use `text_fit()` (they are not names of games).
  - *Straight end of a bar* (`gfx.c`). `gfx_bar_fill(x, y, w, h, r, color)` draws the used part of a bar: a `gfx_round_rect()` one radius too long, cut at `x + w` by the scissor (whole pixels, inside the clip that is set), so the left end is round, the right end is a straight edge, and nothing is drawn twice (a fade stays even). It is used by `settings.c` (the first piece of the Console Storage bar; the other pieces are `gfx_rect()` as before), `saves.c` (the header of *Manage Saved Data* and *Games and Apps*) and `files.c` (the File Manager).
  - *Held keys in popups* (`ui.c`, `power.c`). `menu_step()` moves at once on a new direction, repeats after `POPUP_REPEAT_FIRST` (0.32 s) and then every `POPUP_REPEAT` (0.08 s), the same numbers as the lists. The four places that open a popup set `menu_timer = POPUP_REPEAT_HELD` (1e9), so a direction that is held when the popup opens neither moves nor repeats until it is let go. `power.c` has the same with `s_timer`. The popups of `saves.c`, `files.c` and `settings.c` used the repeating `vertical()` already.
  - *Sorting* (`saves.c`). `saves_open_games()` starts on `GSORT_LEAST` (it started on `GSORT_USED_NEW`, the second choice of the popup). `set_sort()` (both screens) puts `S.sel`, `S.top` and `S.scroll` at 0 after `resort()`, so a new sort starts at the first row (before, the highlight stayed on its row number and the list scrolled to it).
  - *Tests* (private, not in git): `gfx/gfxtest.c` (`test_bar_fill()`: every width from 2 px, the straight right end, nothing outside, an even fade, the clip kept and put back, parts that do not start on a whole pixel, the corners of the screen after `gfx_clip_reset()`; `test_marquee()`: every rule of `text_marquee_offset()`, the picture only inside the room at every time, an offset of 10.45 is drawn 10 px to the left, inside a clip, a name that fits), `ui/loader_test.c` (new: the real `loader.c` with its thread over a stand-in for `art.c`: rounded to begin with and after every start, square after `loader_set_round(0)`, freed at once and made again when the corners change, nothing made again when they do not, what is far from the focus is freed, a missing picture too), `art/art_test.c` (the square picture: same size, opaque corners, pixel for pixel the rounded one outside the corners, no second cache file), `ui/library_test.c` (the popups: the Sort popup, the view popup and the power popup move at once, repeat after 0.32 s and then every 0.08 s, stop at the ends with no sound, and a direction held at the opening does not move them; Games and Library: the tag, the name, *Play* and the time played have the same sizes and the places the code says, 80 x 44 rectangles for the plates, the straight frame and the ring of the big card with their numbers, the colours of a card without a picture, the timers of the titles), `ui/label_test.c` (the Games title keeps its size and its whole text, its room is what is left after the tag, it stands still and then moves, it never draws right of the margin, its time starts when the card is selected), `ui/pixel_test.c` (the cards of the Library are 80 x 44 and 88 px apart **and the selected one too**; the tag next to the big card is pixel for pixel the one at the bottom of the Library, as light too), `saves/saves_test.c` (the name scrolls only in the highlighted row, where it is drawn, its time starts again with the next row, the folder name of a row that is not read yet, the title after the name, the bar call with its numbers, 2 % when nothing is used, a new sort starts at the top, Games and Apps starts on *Least used*), `files/popup_test.c` (the same for the File Manager: the bar call, the scrolling name in its room, a short highlighted name is plain), `power/power_test.c` (the repeat of the power popup and its rate), `settings/settings_test.c` (the first piece of the Console Storage bar; the golden pictures of the two storage pages are new). `ui/popup_sig.h` records three more calls (`gfx_bar_fill`, `gfx_edge_ring` and `text_draw_marquee`, with its room and time), and `run_all.sh` wraps them.
  - *Mutation checks* (private, `mutation-batches/mut32*.py`; 251 changes one at a time in the new code, then 162 more runs of the ones that were not caught after a test was added): `gfx.c` 24, `loader.c` 15, `art.c` 4, `power.c` 12, `settings.c` 6, `files.c` 31, `saves.c` 40, `ui.c` 119. A test that loops for ever counts as caught (a watch killed such a mutant after 5 minutes of CPU).
    *Holes found and closed.* The reset of the tracked clip (`gfx_clip_reset()` put 1 where 0 is), the rounding of the offset and of the edges of a bar (0.5 against 0.6), the first-time value of `s_round`, the freeing of far-away pictures (never tested before: `loader_focus()`), the repeat rate of the power popup, the move sound at the ends of a popup, everywhere the highlighted name of *Manage Saved Data* / *Games and Apps* / the File Manager is drawn (place, weight, room, the name that is used when the row is not read yet), the length and the minimum length of the bars, a short highlighted name that must not scroll, the geometry of the tag, the title, *Play* and the ring of the big card in Games (they had no test of their own), the colours of a card that has no picture yet.
    *Not caught, nothing changes (49).* `gfx.c`: the edges of the comparisons that give the same result on both sides (`t < 1` against `t < 0` in `text_marquee_offset()` for a time that is below the waiting time anyway, `<` against `<=` at exactly the end of a step, `w < room` for a name of exactly the room), `max` / `min` of the clip against itself when equal, an empty scissor (`<` against `<=`: nothing is drawn either way), and `right - left + 2 * r` against `+ 1 * r` or `+ 3 * r` in `gfx_bar_fill()` (the part after the cut is never drawn). `loader.c` and `power.c`: the first value of `s_round` (`loader_start()` sets it), the reset value of `s_timer` (`power_open()` sets it), a float that is exactly 0. `art.c`: `load_icon(e, 2)` against `1` (any non-zero). `files.c`, `saves.c`, `ui.c`: buffer sizes that are never filled; the clamp of `used` at 1 (it never is above 1); the minimum of 36 px for the title of a save (the room is always more); the sizes and the weight in `text_width()` (the plain mock gives 6 px a letter, whatever the size, and neither mock font has a bold: the run with the real font's letter widths (`MOCK_ATLAS`) catches a wrong size in the tag of Games, 106 pixels differ, but not a wrong weight of the tag or of *Play*); a name that is as wide as its room to the pixel (the mock widths are multiples of 6, the rooms are not); the slide of the Library title while it appears (`* 6`, an old line); `ring > 0.01` (a ring that fades is between 1 % and 11 % for one frame); `w / 12.1` (a radius a hundredth less); `254 * pa` for the plate of the Library card (one 255th of the opacity); `LABEL_Y - 12.5 + 0.6` (the same floor).
- **Manage Plugins** (`plugins.c` / `plugins.h`, `settings.c` / `settings.h`, `files.c` / `files.h`, `main.c`, `lang.c` / `lang.h`; later, on request).
  - *Files* (`plugins.c`). `plugins_load()` reads up to `MAX_FILES` 6 files: `<ARK folder>PLUGINS.TXT`, `<root>/SEPLUGINS/PLUGINS.TXT`, and, when there is another storage, `<other>/PSP/SAVEDATA/ARK_01234/PLUGINS.TXT` and `<other>/SEPLUGINS/PLUGINS.TXT`. `<root>` is `g_root` (the storage psp5 runs from). The other storage exists when it can be opened (`ef0:/` when psp5 is on ms0:, `ms0:/` when psp5 runs from ef0:; the model is not asked, see *The internal storage (`ef0:`)* at the top of this section); it does not exist when ARK maps ms0: to the internal storage (`sys_ms_is_ef()`). A file is read once however its path is written (`load_once()` compares with `strcasecmp()`: ARK-4 without an ARK folder has SEPLUGINS as its ARK folder). Every `Plugin` has its `path` now (the text of the second field). Which of these files ARK itself reads is ARK's business; psp5 only offers the places the maintainer named.
