  - *Lists.* `plugins_names()` (distinct names, `strcasecmp()`, first seen first), `plugins_lines_of(name)` (indexes for `plugins_get()`), `plugins_free_places(name)` (for each distinct `(file, path)` of the plugin's lines: the runlevels of `PLUGIN_LEVEL[]` = `vsh`, `game`, `pops` that have no line there; `xmb` counts as `vsh`, `psx` and `ps1` as `pops`; an `always` / `all` line in that file means nothing is free there), `plugins_file_path()` and `plugins_place_label()` ("SEPLUGINS (ms0:)": the folder of the file and its storage).
  - *Changes.* `plugins_remove(idx, n)` takes the lines out of their files (a comment stays, a file with no line left is written empty, a file that was only partly read is never written: nothing at all is changed then, nor for a wrong index), makes the lists again (`reparse()`) and writes. `plugins_add(file, level, path)` adds `"<level>, <path>, on"` unless that line is there (the same runlevel by its aliases, the same path whatever its capitals). `plugins_locations()` is where a new plugin can go: the ARK folder (only when it exists; `sys_ark_dir()` on the root storage, `PSP/SAVEDATA/ARK_01234` on the other) and `SEPLUGINS` (always; it is made when needed), root storage first, no folder twice. `plugins_install(src, loc, levels)`: nothing is changed when the file the lines go into was only partly read, or has no room for the lines (96 lines at most); a file of the same name in the folder gives `PLUGIN_EXISTS` (the same path as `src` is no move); the file is renamed when `src` and the folder are on the same storage, else copied in 16 KiB pieces and the old one deleted (when only that delete fails the plugin is installed all the same; a copy that fails leaves no half file); then the lines (in the order XMB, Games, PS1; lines that are there are not added again), and `plugins_save()`. When `plugins_save()` fails after the move, the plugin stays moved and the answer is `PLUGIN_FAILED`.
  - *Settings* (`settings.c`). Five pages: `PG_PLUGMGR` (child of `PG_PLUGINS`), `PG_PLUGEDIT` (child of it; its title is the plugin's name, `PM.name`), `PG_PLUGADD` and `PG_PLUGDEL` (children of `PG_PLUGEDIT`), `PG_PLUGNEW` (child of `PG_PLUGMGR`). They are filled when the page comes into view (`page_enter()` calls `pm_fill_*()`), not by `build()`, so a page of Manage Plugins is empty until it is opened. `PG_PLUGINS` is made by `fill_plugins()` (the row Manage Plugins first, then the switches; `Item.plugin` is still the number of the line, from 1) and again after every change. The rows of the lists are switches with `Item.immediate` set (nothing to save) and a button, an `IT_ACTION` with an action of `PA_ADD`, `PA_DEL` or `PA_NEW` (100 and up: Settings does these itself, `settings_update()` does not give them to main.c). A page row (`IT_PAGE`) draws `Item.info` at the right now (the places of the plugin in Manage Plugins). The button calls `pm_ask()`: nothing switched on gives `S_PM_NOTHING` and the error sound; else the question (`S.ask`, `popup_ask()` as in the other screens, wrapped to four lines, Cancel chosen first; `settings_modal()` is true while it is open). ✕ on the first button calls `pm_apply()`: it saves the switches of Plugins first (`flush_plugins()`, the same as `settings_commit()` does, so the numbers of the lines do not shift under them), changes the files, makes the page Plugins from the files again, says Done / Failed / `S_PM_EXISTS`, and goes to the page where the change shows (the plugin's page; the list when the plugin has no line left; the list on the new plugin's row after an install; the same page when it failed).
  - *A new plugin.* `SA_PLUGIN_PICK` (the first row of Manage Plugins) is a visit (`settings_is_visit()`); `main.c` stops the app, runs `files_mode(1)` (the File Manager loop with `files_open_pick()`, the power popup works there too), starts the app again and, when `files_picked()` has a path, calls `settings_plugin_new(path)`, which opens `PG_PLUGNEW`: the file's name, Location (`IT_CHOICE` over `plugins_locations()`, `Item.set` fills the page again because the free runlevels depend on the place), a switch for each runlevel that has no line there yet (`plugins_has()`), and the button. `files.c` pick mode (`s_pick`): only folders and files that end in `.prx` (any capitals) are listed, ✕ on a file closes the screen with its whole path in `s_picked`, ○ goes up and out of the top with nothing chosen, □ and START do nothing, the hints are ✕ Open / Select and ○ Back, the title is `S_PM_CHOOSE`. `files_open()` is the whole File Manager again (and forgets the choice).
  - *Decided here.* The plugin file is never deleted by Uninstall (only the lines): the maintainer did not ask for it; a plugin with no line left is out of the list, and *Install New Plugin* takes the file again. The default place is the first of `plugins_locations()` (the ARK folder, as psp5's own helper plugin does). `PM_MAX` 24 rows in a list of places (more lines of one plugin are not listed).
  - *Texts.* New in all 11 languages: `S_PM_MANAGE`, `S_PM_NEW`, `S_PM_INSTALL_TO`, `S_PM_UNINSTALL_FROM`, `S_PM_INSTALL`, `S_PM_UNINSTALL`, `S_PM_ASK_INSTALL`, `S_PM_ASK_UNINSTALL`, `S_PM_NOTHING`, `S_PM_EXISTS`, `S_PM_CHOOSE`, `S_PM_SELECT` (239 strings). The File and Location labels are `S_I_FILE` and `S_I_LOCATION`. Names in title case (`caps_test.c`): Manage Plugins, Install New Plugin, Install to, Uninstall from, Choose a Plugin.
  - *Tests* (private, not in git): `cfg/cfg_test.c` (new `manage_tests()`: the four files on a PSP Go, with one storage, with ms0: as the internal storage, from the internal storage, with no Memory Stick; names, lines, free places and the aliases; labels; remove from two files, a wrong index, a partly read file; add, duplicates, aliases, no room, a path that is too long; the places; `plugins_has()`; install on one storage and across storages (40000 bytes), the file exists, nothing asked right, no room, a copy, read or write that fails, a delete that fails, `PLUGINS.TXT` that can not be written), `settings/settings_test.c` (the pages and rows, the titles, the buttons and the question with every answer, saving first, a new plugin, the place popup, the runlevels that are there, `PLUGIN_EXISTS`, Failed, no plugins, 28 lines of one plugin, every language drawn on whole pixels), `files/popup_test.c` (the picker: the list, the hints, the title, what does not work, ✕ and ○, a PSP Go, back to the whole File Manager), `run_all.sh` (the source check of `main.c`: the picker is a visit and gives its file to Settings). All 60-odd test programs now wipe their own folder in `/tmp` before they start: a folder that an earlier run (one that was stopped) had left under the same process number made `settings_test` fail now and then (Console Storage counted the files of the old run); that was the failure that was seen once in a full run and could not be made again. A second cause of such failures was in the PC stand-in for the PSP's calls (`posix/posixio.c`): its table of open folders was not safe for two threads (the counting thread of `gamesize.c` and the screen open folders at the same moment), and under load a handle could be taken twice, so the menu test now and then crashed in `sceIoDread()` (and then the six `popup:` tests that compare its pictures failed too); the table has a lock now, and the counts of open files and folders are atomic.
  - *Mutation checks* (private, `mutation-batches/mut31*.py`, 518 changes one at a time: `plugins.c` 252, `settings.c` 197, `files.c` 61, `main.c` 8 through the source checks of `run_all.sh`; the generator's changes for the new code only, plus eight by hand for `main.c`). The first time 81 of the `plugins.c` changes and 86 of the others lived (one change made the cfg test run for ever and was stopped by hand: a hang is a catch). *Holes found and closed:* a runlevel that is none of the three (umd, a game's ID, homebrew) taken for the XMB (`-0` for `-1`); the lists of an earlier, longer load showing through after a shorter one (`reparse()`, `plugins_free_places()` and the compacting of `plugins_remove()` read one place too far); the first name listed twice; the short paths of `plugins_place_label()`; line 0 refused in `plugins_remove()` and `plugins_add()`, the count taken for a line; the 175 / 176 characters of a path that fit in a line; a bare `ms0:/` as the ARK folder; the 159 / 160 characters of a path in `plugins_install()` (160 loses its last letter); the checks of a first file that did not fit or had no room (only slot 1 was tried); the question's choices and colours and that its panel is drawn at all (`popup_ask` is recorded in the settings test now, and the dark panel is looked for on the screen), its fade (7 a second, in and out), the sound of a move in it; the switches that are not settings (`Item.immediate`, on all three pages); no icon on the rows of the plugin pages; the first place that is chosen when the number of a place is too big; no Install button when every runlevel is there; a stale switch past the end of a list that came into the question or into the change; Plugins made from the files again after a change (a.prx gone, b.prx still off); the row of a new plugin that is not the last on the list; only Games switched on giving one line; four places on a Go (`loc_text[4]` was read one past the end); a name of 47 letters whole; a long question cut at four lines. *Found by the tests, not by a change:* a line that was added was put at the end of the list in memory, not where the file has it, so the order was another one until the next start (`add_line()` makes the lists again now, the cfg test compares them with a fresh load); the chosen row of a page that is made again with fewer rows could be past its end (`settings_update()` clamps it).
    *Not caught, nothing changes:* the sizes of buffers (`name[48]`, the texts of the places, `char list[512]`, `dest[160]` and the others: they are never filled to the end by the tests, and one more or one less changes nothing that can be seen); a truthy value (`2` for `1` in `S.ask`, `Item.immediate`, `pf->dirty`, the answer of `plugins_has()` and of `move_file()`); the handle of an open (`< 1` for `< 0`: a handle is never 0); the permissions of `sceIoOpen` / `sceIoMkdir` (the PC ignores them); `strncasecmp(.., 5 or 3)` over a storage name (`ms0:/` and `ef0:/` differ at either length); `-2` for `-1` (nothing is compared with it); `roots[3]`, `!dir[1]` and `ark[1]` (the same answer for every path that exists); loops that go one too far where the place it reads is zero (`kill[]` of a slot that is not there, `PM.lvl_on[3]` that the mask of `plugins_install()` drops, the row after the last of Manage Plugins) and the ones that need more rows than a list has (`MAX_ITEMS` is 52, a list has 49 at most); `MAX_FILES` 6, which at most 4 files and the places that are not files yet can not reach (a guard); `ok = 1` before a branch that has no storage to install to (there is always SEPLUGINS); the size the question's text is measured at (the stand-in for the font has the same width at every size) and its width by one pixel. `files.c`: only changes of lines that were there before (the sort of two names, the first line of the title, the hints' array) or that change nothing (`s_path[1]`, `open_list(2)`).
- **Keep in Home, six ways to sort the Library, Console Storage and Games and Apps** (`home.c` / `home.h`, `gamesize.c` / `gamesize.h` and `storage.c` / `storage.h` (new), `saves.c` / `saves.h`, `settings.c` / `settings.h`, `ui.c` / `ui.h`, `folders.c` / `folders.h`, `config.c` / `config.h`, `main.c`, `lang.c` / `lang.h`, `Makefile`; later, on request). This replaces the pins, *Add to Home* and *Remove from Home* of the entries below.
  - *Home* (`home.c`). There is no pinning and nothing is taken off Home. `home.txt` has `keep <key>` (the games kept, in the order they were kept, at most `HOME_KEEP_MAX` 9) and `recent <key>` (the cards opened last, at most 40). An older file is read: `pin` is `keep`, `hide` is ignored, `?library` is ignored (the key of a game is never empty and never starts with `?`); the next write has only `keep` and `recent`. `home_build()` gives the disc first (outside the ten), then the games, then `HOME_LIBRARY` (always the last card). The games are put in the order of `home_order_key()` (3 = on the list of opened cards, newest first; 2 = played before, by `lastplayed_time()`; 1 = never played, by `Entry.file_time`; then the title, then the place in the list). The first nine (`room`) are the cards. A kept game that is not among them comes in at the end of the nine, in the order of the list, and each one takes the place of the last game that is not kept (`missing[]`, `chosen[]`): so a kept game that is among the nine stays where it is, and a game that is not kept never pushes out a kept one. `home_keep(e, on)` returns 0 for the disc and for the tenth kept game (nothing is changed), `home_forget(e)` drops the game from both lists (a deleted game), `home_state()` has `HOME_SHOWN | HOME_KEPT`. Out of memory: the keys (first `malloc`) make the disc and the Library card; without `rank` or `okey` the games come in the order of the list.
  - *The options popup* (`ui.c`). `menu_build()` gives *Delete* (when `gameops_target()` allows it), *Information* and *Keep in Home* (`MI_KEEP`; not for the disc). The Library card has no options: no popup (`open_card_menu()` returns when `menu_n` is 0), and no Options button under it (`draw_details()` does not draw the ⋯ circle, and → from Open does not go to it: `sel_card(ui) != HOME_LIBRARY` in the Games branch of `ui_update()`). *Manage Saved Data* (`MI_SAVES`, `UI_SAVES`), `MI_PIN`, `MI_UNPIN`, `MI_ADD`, `MI_REMOVE`, `MI_REMOVE_YES`, `Ui.ask_remove`, `menu_ask_remove()` and the toasts of pinning are gone. ✕ on *Keep in Home* calls `home_do()`: it asks `home_keep()`, turns `Ui.menu_kept` (what the switch draws: a 26 x 14 switch like Settings', `theme_toggle_on()`) and sets `Ui.menu_home_dirty`; **the popup stays open** and Home is made again (`home_changed()`) only in `menu_close()`, so the card does not leave under the player's thumb. A refused keep (nine are kept) shows `S_HOME_KEEP_LIMIT` and plays the error sound. `ui_forget_entry()` calls `home_forget()`. `ui_relist_gone()` is for several deleted games at once (Games and Apps): everything is built again, the selection goes to the first card, and the sizes are counted again.
  - *Sorting the Library* (`ui.c`, `ui.h`, `folders.c`). `LIB_SORT_NAME_AZ`, `_NAME_ZA`, `_ADDED_NEW`, `_ADDED_OLD`, `_SIZE_BIG`, `_SIZE_SMALL` (`LIB_SORT_COUNT` 6, in the order of the popup; `MI_SORT_*` follow it). One comparison per way (`s_game_cmp[]`, `s_folder_cmp[]`); the disc is first in all of them; a tie is the name, then the place. A folder: the folder numbers are in the order of the names (Z-A turns them round), `Folder.newest` and the new `Folder.oldest`, or the size of its games together (`fsize`). `played_key()` and `MI_SORT_PLAYED` are gone. The popup is wider (it takes the longest label and room for a check mark, drawn like the tick of a language in Settings) and the Sort button's text is `S_SORT_*`. A size is `gamesize_of()`; sizes that are counted later sort the Library again (`sort_by_size_again()` in `ui_update()`: only when the way is a size, at most once a second, the same card stays selected, no slide). `psp5.cfg` has `librarysort=0..5` (`CONFIG_LIB_SORTS`, a `_Static_assert` in `ui.c` keeps it equal to `LIB_SORT_COUNT`); an older file's `libsort=` is read only when there is no `librarysort=`: 0 is name A-Z, 1 (recently added) is `LIB_SORT_ADDED_NEW`, 2 (recently played) is name A-Z; a `librarysort=` that is not 0..5 is name A-Z (the old line is not asked then).
  - *Sizes* (`gamesize.c`). An ISO (and a file the scan saw) is its `Entry.file_size`; an app, a PS1 game and an update are the folder `gameops_target()` gives, counted by `gameops_tree_stats()` in a thread of its own (`psp5_gamesize`, priority 0x30 like the loader), one folder after the other. `gamesize_start(list)` forgets what was counted and makes a table (open addressing, 64-bit FNV-1a of the entry's path, `Slot { key, bytes, fallback, dev, done }`, twice the number of entries) and the jobs (a copy of the folders: the list may change under the thread); the thread writes `bytes` then `done`. `gamesize_of()` is `bytes` once `done`, else `fallback` (the main file); `gamesize_known()`, `gamesize_total(dev)` (0 ms0:, 1 ef0:, -1 both), `gamesize_version()` (grows with every folder) and `gamesize_busy()`. Without memory or a thread nothing is counted and every size is the main file's. `ui_init()` starts it, `ui_free()` stops it, `ui_relist_removed()` and `ui_relist_gone()` start it again.
  - *Console Storage* (`storage.c`, `settings.c`). `storage_start()` asks `gameops_dev_space()` for ms0: and ef0: (ef0: is not asked when `sys_ms_is_ef()`), puts the internal storage first on a PSP Go with a Memory Stick, and counts `MUSIC`, `VIDEO`, `PICTURE`, `PSP/PHOTO` (Media) and `PSP/SAVEDATA` in a thread (`psp5_storage`; on the spot when there is no thread); `storage_get(block, &info)` gives the total, the free space, `games` (`gamesize_total()`), `media`, `saved`, and `other` (used minus the three; 0 until `ready`, which needs the thread done and `gamesize_busy()` false). `PG_CSTORE` is the page (child of `PG_STORAGE`, the first row of it): `IT_STORE` rows (`Item.icon` is the colour, `Item.block` the storage), made again every frame by `store_fill()` from `storage_get()`; the status block is drawn by `draw_store_page()` and is not a row, so it cannot be chosen. A block is `STORE_HEAD_H` (only when there are two) + `STORE_STATUS_H` + four rows + `STORE_GAP`; `store_scroll` goes to the block of the chosen row. The bar is the colours of the four parts one after the other (the first piece is a rounded rectangle, 6 px wide at the least, the others plain; a part that is not empty is `ST_MIN_PIECE` 2 px at the least, so that Saved Data is seen; the last piece is cut at the end of the bar), the rest is the dim track. The colours (`ST_COLOR`) are the console's own, read from a picture of its Storage page: blue (20, 150, 236), purple (81, 3, 174), orange (215, 90, 6), grey (189, 187, 174). ✕ on *Games and Apps* gives `SA_GAMES`, on *Saved Data* `SA_SAVES`; `settings_action_dev()` says which storage (-1 when there is one). *Media* and *Other* do nothing. `settings_commit()` calls `storage_stop()` (nothing counts in the background once Settings is left).
  - *Games and Apps* (`saves.c`). It is the screen of Manage Saved Data with `S.mode == MODE_GAMES`: `saves_open_games(list, dev)` makes the list at once from the game list (`Save.entry` is the place in the list, `dir` is that number as text so a sort can find the row again, `locked` is `!gameops_target()`; the disc is not in it), `saves_open_on(list, dev)` is the saved data of one storage (`saves_open(list)` is both). Seven ways (`GSORT_*`): *Least used* (seconds played, `playtime_seconds()`), *Date used* both ways (`lastplayed_time()`; a game never played is last whichever way), *Name* both ways, *Size* both ways (`gamesize_of()`; `refresh_sizes()` takes new sizes and sorts again). The popup, Sort button, marks, question (`S_GA_DEL_ASK`, `S_GA_PROTECTED`), progress, hints and the free-space bar are the same code. A delete (`start_delete()` / `worker()`): the path is `item_path()` (`gameops_target()` for a game, `deletable()` for a save), an ISO is removed with `sceIoRemove`, a folder is counted for the progress (`W.total` grows) and then removed with `gameops_tree_delete()`; the games that are gone are kept in `s_gone[]` (their places in the game list, the last place first) for `main.c` (`games_deleted()`: `ui_forget_entry()`, `art_forget()`, `scan_remove_at()`, `loader_start()`, `ui_relist_gone()`). The panel shows the game's picture (`art_load_icon()`), its ID (an app: the name of its folder; an ISO without an ID: the file name), when it was played last, its date and its size. Manage Saved Data sorts *Last updated*, *Name*, *Size* now (`SORT_DATE`, `SORT_NAME`, `SORT_SIZE`).
  - *main.c.* `UI_SAVES` is gone. `SA_GAMES` and `SA_SAVES` are handled before `run_action()` (the screen runs over Settings, which stays open: the `in_saves` branch comes before the `in_settings` one; ○ goes back to the same row and `settings_back()` counts the storage again). They are not "visits" (`settings_is_visit()` is false): no `app_stop()`.
  - *The cog* (`ui.c`). Selected, it is `button_bg()` (the dark pill that turns white: a circle) with a black `gfx_gear()`; the circle is as high as the frame of a selected tab (`COG_BTN_D` is `TOP_FRAME_H`, 24) and has no ring round it. The names of the first list of Settings are drawn at the size of the other rows (`text_fit(0.62, 0.48, ...)` for all).
  - *Texts.* New: `S_SORT_NAME` (now "Name"), `S_SORT_NAME_AZ`, `_NAME_ZA`, `_ADDED_NEW`, `_ADDED_OLD`, `_SIZE_BIG`, `_SIZE_SMALL`, `S_SORT_LEAST`, `S_SORT_USED_NEW`, `S_SORT_USED_OLD`, `S_HOME_KEEP`, `S_HOME_KEEP_LIMIT`, `S_CS_TITLE`, `S_CS_SIZE`, `S_CS_FREE`, `S_CS_GAMES`, `S_CS_MEDIA`, `S_CS_SAVED`, `S_CS_OTHER`, `S_GA_DEL_ASK`, `S_GA_PROTECTED`, in all 11 languages (title case where it is a name, checked by `caps_test.c`: *Keep in Home*, *Console Storage*, *Games and Apps*, *Media*, *Saved Data*, *Other*). Gone: `S_SORT_RECENT`, `S_SORT_PLAYED`, `S_HOME_ADD`, `S_HOME_REMOVE`, `S_HOME_PIN`, `S_HOME_UNPIN`, `S_HOME_ADDED`, `S_HOME_REMOVED`, `S_HOME_PINNED`, `S_HOME_UNPINNED`, `S_HOME_PIN_LIMIT`, `S_HOME_REMOVE_ASK` (227 strings now).
  - *Tests* (private, not in git): `home/home_test.c` is rewritten for this model (the first start, the file and its damaged lines, `pin` / `hide` / `?library` of an older file, the order, a kept game that is among the nine and one that is not, nine kept and the tenth refused, a restart, forget, the forty, games played from the XMB, `home_recent_rank()`, the room of the answer and no memory, ties, 30000 random operations against a model written apart from `home.c`); new `gameops/gamesize_test.c` (`gamesize.c` and `storage.c`: ISO and folders, the version, a list made again, no thread, no memory, a thousand games, one storage and two, the folders of Media and Saved Data, `other`); `saves/saves_test.c` has the Games and Apps section (seven ways to sort, the buttons, locked games, the panel, one storage, sizes that arrive later, a delete of an ISO and of a folder with the places that are gone, 11 languages); `settings/settings_test.c` has Console Storage (one and two storages, the numbers, ✕ on every row, moving and scrolling, the picture, 11 languages; two more golden pictures and the new hashes of the first list and Storage); `ui/menu_test.c` and `ui/library_test.c` (the popup of three choices, the switch that keeps the popup open, Home made again when it closes, nine kept and the toast, deleting a kept game, the Library card with no popup, the six ways to sort with sizes counted in the background and the Sort popup with its check mark, the cog as a white circle with a ring in 11 languages; the Library again with the letter widths of the real font), `units/config_test.c` (`librarysort`, the older `libsort`), `units/caps_test.c`, the string count (227), `power/main_check.py`.
  - *Mutation checks* (private, `mutation-batches/mut29a.py` to `mut29u2.py`, and `mut29sv2` to `mut29sv4`, `mut29n2` and `mut29e2` for the second rounds): about 1,270 different changes of the new code, one at a time, each run against the groups that test the file: `gamesize.c` and `storage.c` 159; `home.c` 143 (and 156 more for what survived); `settings.c` 326 (logic 54, drawing 153 and 119) and `config.c` 45; `saves.c` 347 (the Games and Apps code 146, run with `SAVES_FAST=1`, which skips the Manage Saved Data part of the test; what the fast run cannot reach, the saved data of one storage and the old sorting, was run again in full); `ui.c` 233; `folders.c` 19. What survived was closed by a test, or is listed here.
    *Holes found and closed.* A kept game stayed kept after it was deleted (`games_deleted()` gave `ui_forget_entry()` a copy of the entry, which it compared with itself: `main_games_check.py` reads the source for the pointer into the list). Comparisons that only worked because the qsort of the PC asks in one order (a comparison that gave 0 for "smaller" still sorted right on the PC): every comparison of Games and Apps is checked to be a strict order for every pair of games, and every sorting is checked on the list turned round, shuffled, with the disc last (the first place is a game then), with two games, and with three of one name (the place in the list decides). Sizes that arrive later: the fixture makes a folder bigger than its EBOOT, so the order changes when the number comes (Size both ways, and the size in the row is made again). The progress of a delete counting only the folder and not what is in it; a file that can not be removed (the delete stops, says Failed, the game stays in the list); the text of an empty list (both screens); a place outside the game list given to `item_path()`; Saved Data of one storage (`saves_open_on()` with 0 and 1, and with one storage only); `Folder.oldest` (also for a folder with one game); the date under the game in the panel; the width of the Sort popup of Games and Apps; the bar of Console Storage (the fixtures are too small to show on 416 px, so its pieces are checked from numbers of their own: the widths and the rounding, 1 px shown and 0.45 px not, the first piece a pill of 6 at the least, the last piece cut at the end of the bar). The test that the Sort popup ends above the bottom of the screen found that the clamp in `draw_sort_menu()` never moved it (64 + 206 = 270, the limit): it is a `_Static_assert` now.
    *Not caught, nothing changes.* The size or the sign of a comparison result (`-2` for `-1`; only the sign counts), and `<=` / `>=` after a `!=` of the same two numbers (also for equal times in `folders.c`); the size of arrays (`devs[3]`, `root[41]`) and `malloc(0)` or two items for an empty list; truthy values (`2` for `1` in `matched`, `ready`, `done`); a handle of 0 or less (`fd <= 0`: a handle is never 0); the number of a thread (`>= 1` for `>= 0`: an id is never 0), and the unused arguments of a thread; `T.count = list ? n : 1` (the list is NULL only when `calloc` fails, and then no game was read); `S.pic_sel` and `S.pan_for` starting at 0 instead of -1 (`pan_ready` is 0 and a game is `ready`, so the panel is made at once, and the time the picture waits starts at 0 either way); `x + w >= bx + bw` in the bar (the same width); the first piece `w < 7 ? 6 : w` and `w <= 6 ? 6 : w` (the same for whole numbers); the sizes of the texts of the Console Storage page (the smallest size of `text_fit()`, bold, and the widths 0.4 and 0.5 of the list): no text of the page is made smaller to fit in any of the 11 languages (checked), so those limits are never reached.

  - *Later, on request:* the selected cog is a white circle as high as the frame of a tab (24, `COG_BTN_D` is `TOP_FRAME_H`) with no ring; the Library card has no Options button (not drawn, and → from Open stays on Open); *Media Gallery* is *Media* (all 11 languages); the colours of Console Storage are the console's own (read from a picture of its Storage page) and a part that is not empty is 2 px wide at the least (`ST_MIN_PIECE`). Tests (private): `menu_test.c` (the cog 24 across, as high as 24 measured on the pixels, nothing round it; the three dots of the Options button on a game's card and not on the Library card, in 11 languages; → from Open; ✕ on Open opens the Library), `settings_test.c` (the pieces of the bar: 2 px for 1 px and for 0.45 px, nothing for an empty part, the colours, the two new golden pictures), `caps_test.c`. Mutation checks (`mut30a.py`, 62 changes: the cog lines of `ui.c`, the Library card's Options button, `ST_COLOR`, the smallest piece, the 11 names): 60 caught (one of them, a menu test that hung, was stopped by hand: it did not hang when the same change was run again), 2 change nothing (`>=` for `>` where the cog's fade is exactly 0.01, and `<=` for `<` where the width is exactly 2).
- **Settings: one list at a time, six categories, System with six pages** (`settings.c`, `lang.c` / `lang.h`; later, on request, with a picture of the console's Settings as the example).
  - *The model.* `Category` is a page now (`name`, `items[]`, `count`; the `desc` is gone). `PG_ROOT` is the first list; `PG_PSP5`, `PG_NETWORK`, `PG_SYSTEM`, `PG_STORAGE`, `PG_SCREEN` and `PG_CONTROLS` are its six rows, in that order; `PG_INFO`, `PG_APPEAR`, `PG_LANG`, `PG_ARK`, `PG_PERF` and `PG_PLUGINS` are the six rows of `PG_SYSTEM`.
    `PARENT[]` says where ○ goes. A row that opens a page is `IT_PAGE` (`Item.action` is the page, `Item.icon` the picture of the first list: `ICO_APP` ... `ICO_CONTROLS`, none elsewhere); a language is `IT_LANG` (`action` is the language, a tick on the one in use). The state (`S`): `page`, `item[PG_COUNT]` (the chosen row of each page, kept when
    it is left), `top[PG_COUNT]` (the first row shown) and `scroll` (where the list is on its way to `top`). `focus`, `cat`, `cat_top` and `cat_scroll` are gone. ✕ on an `IT_PAGE` calls `page_enter()` (which asks the PSP again when it is `PG_INFO`, because the battery moves); ○ goes to `PARENT[]`, and ○ on `PG_ROOT` returns `SA_CLOSE`. **← and → are not used** (they opened a page and went back while Settings had two columns): the D-pad's and the analog stick's sideways pushes do nothing, make no sound, and `S.stick_x` and the code that turned a push of the stick into ← / → are gone; `stick_dir()` still looks at both axes, so that a push that is mostly sideways does not move the list up or down.
    The visits (Wi-Fi, FTP, File Manager, USB Mode) come back to the page and the row they left, because `S` is not reset (only `settings_open()` does that).
  - *The list.* `LIST_X` 24, `LIST_W` 432 (the file manager's sides), `LIST_TOP` 58, `ROW_H` 26, `LIST_ROWS` 7 (not `ROWS`: the test has an array of that name), a frame and a faint fill round the chosen row, a line between the others (from the label on the first list, where the icon is), `gfx_clip` round the rows, and a bar at the right (3 px, 6 px outside the list, as the file manager's)
    only when there are more than 7 rows. **Scrolling is the file manager's:** `top` moves only when the chosen row goes above it or below its seventh row, and `scroll += (top - scroll) * clamp(dt * 16)`. (Before, the list kept the chosen row on the last row shown and moved with every step, up and down.)
    The title is `Settings` on the first list, the page's name on the six, and `System > Appearance` and so on on the pages of System (`text_fit` to the list's width).
  - *The pages.* `build()` makes the names first (the rows that open a page are named after it), then the rows. The old *System* category is `PG_ARK`, named by `s_ark_name` = `S_CAT_ARK` ("%s Settings") with "ARK-4", "ARK-5" or "ARK" (when `sys_ark_version()` is neither); *Performance* is `PG_PERF`; *Theme* is `PG_APPEAR` (*Appearance*); *About* is `PG_INFO`
    (*Console Information*, see below); *Memory* is `PG_STORAGE` (*Storage*); *Display and Sound* is `PG_SCREEN` (*Screen and Sound*). The language was a `IT_CHOICE` with a popup in *psp5*; it is `PG_LANG` now, one `IT_LANG` row per language (`own`, saved in `psp5.cfg`), and `set_language()` does what `choice_set()` did for it
    (`lang_apply()`, `font_ensure_cjk()` and `build(1)`: every name is made again in the new language, the page and the row stay). `PG_PSP5` has six rows (the language is gone). Nothing else about a setting changed (the popups of the choices, `immediate` for the PSP's own, `own` for `psp5.cfg`).
  - *Console Information* has two new rows right under the version: `S_ABOUT_ORIGINAL` ("Made by") with `08Weber` and `S_ABOUT_FORK` ("Forked by") with `Xioonae`; the row of the version is `APP_NAME` ("psp5 authentic") with `PSP5_VERSION`.
  - *Icons* (`draw_icon()`, no image files): the PlayStation logo of the start screen (`gfx_logo`, 12 high, in the middle), `gfx_wifi`, the cube of System (`gfx_cube()`, new: `ICON_CUBE` in `icons.c` / `icons.h`, `cube_render()`: a hexagon of three faces, every pixel sampled 8 x 8 times, the faces as three opacities of the one colour, 255, 199 and 128, so the outline and the edges between the faces are as soft as the other icons'; when the image cache is full `gfx_cube()` draws three flat `gfx_quad`s: a `GU_TRIANGLE_STRIP` takes the corners 1, 2, 4, 3), a drive, a screen on its foot and a controller; every edge on a whole pixel (the audit looks at rects and circles).
  - *Strings.* The eight descriptions (`S_DESC_*`) and the code that wrapped them (`wrap_desc()` and the rest, about 90 lines) are gone. Renamed: `S_CAT_MEMORY` -> `S_CAT_STORAGE`, `S_CAT_DISPLAY_SOUND` -> `S_CAT_SCREEN_SOUND`, `S_CAT_THEME` -> `S_CAT_APPEARANCE`, `S_CAT_ABOUT` -> `S_CAT_INFO`; new: `S_CAT_ARK`, `S_ABOUT_ORIGINAL`, `S_ABOUT_FORK`. 219 strings
    (it was 224), all new and changed texts in the 11 languages. The older notes below speak of the old names (*Memory*, *About*, *Theme*, *Display and Sound*) and of two columns: they were true when they were written.
  - *Tests* (private, not in git): `settings/settings_test.c` is rewritten for the pages (623 checks: the six rows and their icons, every page opened by one row of its parent, System's six rows and the ARK page's name for ARK 4, 5 and neither, ↑ ↓ ✕ ○ and the stick on every kind of row, ← and → doing nothing anywhere, back to the row you came from, the Language page, the file manager's scrolling and the bar,
    the title of every page, the icons' boxes, opacity and place, the label's place with and without an icon, the same checks of the pages as before: Controls, Screen and Sound, Network, Storage, Console Information with its two new rows, Appearance, the popups; whole pixels on every page and row and in all 11 languages); `units/caps_test.c` (the new names in title case, exact texts in 11 languages, "sul" and "sulla" are small words in Italian) and `run_all.sh` (219 strings); `icons/icontest.py` (the cube: the exact pixels, bit for bit, the tones, soft edges, the box, a guard after the image, the margin written) and `gfx/gfxtest.c` (`test_cube()`: the picture against the exact sum, the tones at full, half and 254 opacity, a position a fifth of a pixel off, the cube too big for an image; `GFXTEST_CUBE_ONLY=1` runs only that, for the mutation runs, which would take minutes with the 3000-frame stress).
  - *Mutation checks* (private, `mutation-batches/mut20*.py` to `mut28a.py`): about 1,000 changes of the new code, in rounds (`settings.c`: the pages and the build 180, choices, language, `page_enter()`, `settings_update()` 74, the stick 16, icons and rows 230, the frame 115; `icons.c` and `gfx.c`: the cube 60 and 26). Each round that left survivors was followed by tests until what was left changed nothing,
    and the survivors were run again. **Holes found and closed:** the stick counting along the wrong axis near a tie (the four corners and the steps next to a tie are tested), a new language losing the switches that are not saved (`build(1)`), a chosen row one past the end of a list, the fade-in and the popup's fade and the list's scroll speeds (`dt` of 0.1 s), the move sound, the 0.4 s wait for an action's sound,
    the exact cube (the picture is compared bit for bit with the same sum worked out in Python; the cube too big for an image is drawn flat with the exact tones, also at an opacity of 254), the ARK-4 clamps, the thresholds of Console Information's rows and of Screen and Sound's (0 is a value; the equalizer 0 to 4; AVLS 0 and 1), the first plugin's number, and every drawing detail:
    two kinds of hash that the test keeps (see the next paragraph) and the clip, the fill and frame of the chosen row, the lines (which rows, from where), the eight rows that are drawn, the bar, the title's place, size and fit, the fade-in cover and the notice's fade in and out.
  - *The test keeps two pictures* (`settings_test.c`; `SETTINGS_GOLDEN=1 ./settings` prints the new numbers when a change is meant): a hash of **every drawing call of a frame** (what, where, size, colour and text) for 18 pages and rows (the first list, every page, a scrolled list, plugins, rows with texts that are too long, rows with a label that has just room), made as the pages in the README pictures were looked at, and a hash of **the make-up of the menu**
    (every page and row: name, kind, own / the PSP's / a plugin's, what it sets, how many values and what they are, what it says) for ARK-5, ARK-4, a PSP-1000, a PSP Go and with plugins. They kill the changes of a number, a colour, a position or a flag that no check of its own would look at.
    *Not caught, nothing changes (about 60):* the size of a buffer that a text never fills, an array a size too big, a flag set to 2 instead of 1, `<=` instead of `<` where the two are the same (a row that sets a value to itself; `c->count <= MAX_ITEMS`, which is never reached), the speed of the popup's own scrolling (14.1 for 14), a threshold of the stick near the middle (128 +- 1: only a push of more than 88 counts),
    the bold flag of a text (the PC's font is one font), the width limit of a label or a title by one pixel (the PC's text widths are 6 px a letter, whatever the size), and `title[96]` +- 1. `build(1)` against `build(2)` is the same, and `if (*dx || *dy)` in `stick_dir()` against `&&` too (a push that is far on one axis only is never mostly the other). Full private run after it: 68 groups, ALL PASSED; both ARK builds (`make`, `make ARK=4`) without a warning.
- **The app is called psp5 authentic** (`Makefile` `PSP_EBOOT_TITLE`, `res/ICON0.PNG` and `tools/make_icon.py`, `lang.c`, `ftp.c`, `tools/make_release.py`, the documents; later, on request). Only what a person sees changed: the title in the XMB, its picture (the script draws "psp5" large and "authentic" under it, in Inter Light, the nearest of the fonts of a Linux box to the Segoe UI Semilight it was made in), `S_BOOT_PSP5` and `S_IS_LAUNCHER` in 11 languages, the greeting of the FTP server (`220 psp5 authentic FTP server`),
  the README, INSTALL, CHANGELOG and the text in the release zips. **What did not change on purpose:** the folders and files `PSP/GAME/PSP5`, `PSP5`, `psp5.cfg`, `psp5time.prx`, `PSP_MODULE_INFO("psp5")`, the names of the code's functions and the headers of the source files (the original's copyright notice stays).
- *Pictures of the README* (`docs/screenshots/`): `settings.png`, `settings-system.png`, `settings-console-information.png`, `settings-appearance.png`, `settings-screen-sound.png` and `settings-controls.png` are new (the last was made again); `settings-about.png`, `settings-display.png` and `settings-theme.png` are gone. `games.png`, `options.png`, `power-menu.png` and `theme.png` were shot again because the Games tab fills itself now (Neon Harbor, which was played, is the first card); the others are the same pixel for pixel.
  How: the kit's `shoot.sh`; `fs.txt` is the new script for Settings (up, right, right and ✕ for the cog wheel, then the lists), `fa.txt` loses the five →, `fc.txt` goes Settings → Storage → File Manager. The rest is as in the notes of the earlier pictures.
- **FTP Server** (*Settings → Network → FTP Server*; new `ftp.c` / `ftp.h`, `netsock.c` / `netsock.h`, `netlink.c` / `netlink.h`, `ftpscreen.c` / `ftpscreen.h`; `wifi.c` / `wifi.h`, `netstub.S`, `main.c`, `settings.c` / `settings.h`, `lang.c` / `lang.h`, `Makefile`, `files.h` (a comment)).
  - *Layers*: `ftp.c` is the whole server and knows nothing of the PSP's network: it uses `netsock.h` (listen, accept, recv, send, close, all non-blocking) and `sceIo*`. `netsock.c` is that interface on the PSP's Inet library. `netlink.c` starts the network
    libraries and watches the connection that the PSP's own Wi-Fi window (`wifi.c`) makes. `ftpscreen.c` is the screen. `main.c` runs it in `ftp_mode()`, like `usb_mode()`.
  - *No thread*: `ftp_step(now_ms)` is called every frame and never waits (`ftpscreen_update()`, and `ftpscreen_poll()` up to four more times between the picture and `gfx_end()`, 2 ms apart, at most 10 ms into the frame, so that the next command of a
    computer does not wait for the next picture). A step does at most `STEPS` (4) chunks of 16 KB per transfer. Up to `MAX_SESSIONS` (3) computers; the fourth gets `421` and is closed. A command is read when the answer to the one before has gone (so the
    answers never pile up). While a transfer runs only `ABOR` and `QUIT` are taken; the other lines wait for its end.
  - *What it speaks*: USER, PASS (anything is accepted), QUIT, NOOP, SYST, FEAT (SIZE, MDTM, REST STREAM, UTF8, EPSV; **no** MLSD, so clients use LIST), OPTS UTF8, AUTH/PBSZ/PROT (502), TYPE, MODE, STRU, ALLO, PWD, CWD, CDUP, PASV, EPSV, LIST (also `-a`, `-la`), NLST, RETR, STOR,
    APPE, REST, DELE, MKD, RMD (also the X-forms), RNFR, RNTO, SIZE, MDTM, ABOR. **Not done**: active mode (PORT, EPRT answer 502), MLSD, TLS, a password, another port. A listing is the Unix style (`drwxr-xr-x 1 psp psp size Mon dd hh:mm name`), the year instead of the time for
    another year or a time that is not a time (month 0, day 32, a year before 1980 is "Jan  1  1980").
  - *Paths*: the computer sees `/ms0` and (when `ef0:/` can be opened) `/ef0`. `vresolve()` makes the path (`.` and `..` resolved, `..` at the top stays, the first name of `ms0` / `ef0` written in small letters, no control characters or `\ : * ? " < > |`, at most 255
    characters and 48 names); `real_path()` accepts only those two devices (so flash0 and flash1 cannot be reached, also not through `..`). `/ms0` itself cannot be written to, removed or renamed; the top `/` has nothing to write. REST is a position that `sceIoLseek32` can
    reach (up to 2 GB − 1); a REST past the end of a file is `554`. RMD looks itself that the folder is empty (`dir_empty()`): PPSSPP's `sceIoRmdir` removes a folder with everything in it.
  - *The data connection*: passive only. The port is taken from 49152 on, one after the other (1000 of them, up to 32 tried), so that no port 0 is needed; the address in the `227` answer is the one the control connection has on the PSP (`netsock_local_ip()`), or the one that
    `netlink` got (`ftp_start(port, fallback_ip)`). Every transfer needs its own PASV (also after a failed command). The computer has 20 s to connect (`425` after that); a transfer that moves no byte for 60 s is ended (`426`); a computer that says nothing for 5 minutes gets
    `421`. The times run on a millisecond clock that may wrap (all the sums are `now - then` on `unsigned`).
  - *The Wi-Fi window*: the first version drew its own list of the saved connections (`sceUtilityCheckNetParam` / `GetNetParam`) and joined one with `sceNetApctlConnect`. On a real PSP it showed nothing the player could use (no window to choose a Wi-Fi), and none of that code had ever run on one. It is gone. `wifi_connect()` (`wifi.c`) opens the
    PSP's own window (the netconf dialog, "connect to an access point", the one the *Wi-Fi connection* row shows: the player chooses and the system connects), and returns 1 (a connection was made), 0 (closed) or -1 (the network or the window would not start). `wifi.c` was split for it: `run_dialog()` is the dialog loop that `wifi_dialog()` had (the same code, moved), `wifi_dialog()`
    still starts the libraries itself and stops them again; `wifi_connect()` lets `netlink_start()` start them and, when a connection was made, leaves them running and calls `netlink_attach()`; in every other case it calls `netlink_stop()`.
  - *Netlink* (`netlink.c`): `sceUtilityLoadNetModule` (common, Inet), `sceNetInit`, `sceNetInetInit`, `sceNetApctlInit` (as `wifi.c` does), and, after `netlink_attach()`, the state from `sceNetApctlGetState` (4 = the address is asked with `sceNetApctlGetInfo` and must be a proper one; 1 to 3, 5, 6 are progress; "none" is no connection;
    a minute without an address is a failure; "none" or anything but 4 after it was up is *lost*, told once). The minute counts from the first question after `netlink_attach()`, because the window may have been open for a long time. `netlink_stop()` disconnects (when attached) and undoes exactly what was started, in the reverse order.
  - *Netsock* (`netsock.c`) and what PPSSPP taught: **(1)** the sockets that `sceNetInetAccept` gives are *blocking* in PPSSPP (it ignores `SO_NONBLOCK` for them), so a `recv` with nothing to read stopped the whole app. Now every `recv`, `send` and `accept` is asked with a `sceNetInetSelect` without
    waiting first, and so no call can wait whatever the mode is (and no error number has to be told from "not now"; the numbers 11, 35 and 0x8001000B still mean "not now"). **(2)** The sets of `sceNetInetSelect` are **256 bits** on the PSP; the SDK's own `fd_set` is only 64 bits, so
    the library wrote past it and the program jumped to address 0. `netsock.c` has its own set (`NetFdSet`, 8 words). **(3)** The Inet functions are imported by hand in `netstub.S` (13 functions, with the numbers read from the SDK's `libpspnet_inet.a`: linking that library gives "stubs out of
    order"); `ftp/main_check.py` compares every number with a table and every call in the sources with the list. **(4)** PPSSPP lists the names of its memory stick in capitals (the checks of the Python client compare in small letters).
  - *The screen* (`ftpscreen.c`), the style of USB Mode (the title at 28, 34; the sign in the middle; the ○ capsule at the bottom right; the theme's backdrop): the first two pictures come before the PSP's window opens; the Wi-Fi switch off (when the window gave no connection and the switch reads off: looked at twice a second, the window opens again when it is on; when the window is closed with the switch on, `FTPS_STOPPED`: "The FTP server is not running" and the hints ✕ Start (opens the window again, with the select sound) and ○ Back; the failure messages have ✕ Start too, because `fail()` has stopped everything and the window can start it again);
    connecting (the window has made the connection, the address is awaited); running (the address big, `Port 21`, "No password needed", then "Waiting for a computer" or "Computers connected: n", or "Sending" / "Receiving", the file name, a bar and the bytes: 260 px wide, full for a file that goes
    out, the faint full one for a file that comes in, because its size is not known); and the messages: the network could not be started, could not connect, the connection was lost, the server could not start. HOME while the window is up ends the window and the screen. *The buttons of the window*: the ✕ and ○ that the player presses in the PSP's window are still held when it closes (a ○ that cancels it would close the screen at once, a ✕ that chooses would count as ✕ Start): after every window `s_release` is set, and ✕ and ○ do not count until a frame with both let go has come. The hints are drawn by `hints()` (one capsule, one or two items, 14 px apart). ○ always closes (`ftpscreen_close()` stops the server and the network).
    The power popup (SELECT) works here as on the USB screen: a choice ends the screen first (a connection does not survive Sleep). `scePowerTick(PSP_POWER_TICK_SUSPEND)` every frame keeps the PSP from sleeping by itself (the screen's own backlight timer goes on).
    `S_DESC_NETWORK` says what both rows do; 15 new strings (224 in all) in 11 languages; `settings_is_visit()` has `SA_FTP`, so Settings come back on the same row.
  - *Tests* (private; `host-tests/ftp/`): `ftp_test.c` (376 checks, on the real sockets of the PC, the test is the client and steps the server; then **curl** (list, download and upload of 5 MB, DELE, MKD, RNFR + RNTO, resume with `-C -`) and **Python's ftplib** (`ftp_client.py`: login, list, 3 MB up and down with spaces and UTF-8 in the name, resume
    both ways, rename, delete, folders, errors, ASCII mode, a second connection) run against it), `netsock_psp_test.c` (53 checks: the real `netsock.c` against a mock of the Inet library that has the PSP's address layout and 256-bit sets, so a too small set is found by AddressSanitizer; the mock also checks every call it gets: the set and the number of select and its zero timeout, the protocol, the backlog, the clean address of bind, no flags in recv and send, the option values; sockets with the numbers 0, 3, 31, 32, 33, 63, 64, 65, 127 to 129, 191, 192, 255 work and 256 is refused), `netlink_test.c` (51 checks), `wifi_test.c` (30 checks: the real `wifi.c` and `netlink.c` against a mock of the window; group `wificonnect`; next to the older `host-tests/wifi/wifi_test.c` of `wifi_dialog()`),
    `ftpscreen_test.c` (129 checks: every phase and what each result of the window does, ✕ Start and ○, the buttons that are held when the window closes, the two hints to the pixel, the stops, the drawing in 11 languages, and a golden file), `main_check.py` (`ftp_mode()`, `run_action`, the Settings row, the Makefile, `netstub.S`), and the Network rows in `settings_test.c`. **The whole thing was also run in PPSSPP** (a test build over a demo memory stick with Wi-Fi switched on in its ini: the
    app connects, listens on port 21, and curl and ftplib from the PC do the same sessions): `ftplib` all ok, a 2 MB upload and download byte for byte. That is how (1) and (2) were found. Nothing has been run on a PSP.
  - *The golden file* (`ftpscreen_golden.txt`, private, about 6100 lines): every call that draws (kind, place, size, colour, font, text; also the clear colour and the backdrop's call) of 14 states in 11 languages, each with three test fonts (a letter is 15, 28 and 45 pixels a size unit, the bold font a quarter wider: the plain mock measures 6 pixels a letter whatever the size, so no
    limit of `text_fit`, `text_wrap` or `text_ellipsis` would ever have mattered), more English states (the bar at 0, 3.5, 4.5 pixels, half, full, a file of 1 byte, a file that is bigger than its size, a file that comes in, another address and port), and frames where a text is half a pixel under and
    over the limit it is fitted or cut to. The test compares its pictures with the file line by line (`FTPSCREEN_GOLDEN`); when the look of the screen is changed on purpose, run it once with `FTPSCREEN_GOLDEN_WRITE=1` and look at the difference. The test sets its font with `font_init()` each time, because `gfx.c` remembers the widths of texts.
  - *The test mocks are strict*: `posix/posixio.c` counts every call that would write to, make, remove or rename a whole device (`g_io_dev_writes`: there must be none) and, when the FTP test asks for it (`g_io_dread_strict`), wants a cleared entry before every `sceIoDread` (`ftp.c` clears it each time; older code clears it once, which is fine on the PSP, so it is not the default), and can fail a read or an open and take a byte less than it is given (`g_io_read_fail`, `g_io_open_fail`, `g_io_write_short`); `ftp/netsock_posix.c` can refuse every send or only the short ones (the answers), fail a send or a read, count the bytes sent, and the test checks that every answer of the server ends with CR LF.
    The runs of the sockets, the link and the screen have time limits (120, 120 and 300 s), because a changed source can make them wait for ever.
  - *The mutation runs of `ftp.c` need a network of their own each* (`mutrun_ns.py`: `unshare -n` and `lo_up.py` to bring the loopback up): the passive ports always start at 49152 and the test checks them, so two runs side by side meet each other's sockets. The unchanged test failed in 7 of 8 runs side by side, and a first sweep
    counted about 30 changes as caught that were only caught by that. In the namespaces 16 of 16 runs of the unchanged test pass. The test's own client sockets also have `SO_REUSEADDR` now: a closed connection of the test (in TIME_WAIT) kept its port from the server for a minute, and one run in eight found fewer than 990 of the 1000 passive ports.
  - *Mutation checks* (private, `mutation-batches/mut16*.py`; about 1,070 changes of the new sources, each run against its group): `ftp.c` 379, `netsock.c` 100, the wiring 66 (`ftp_mode()` line by line, the `SA_FTP` case, the Settings row and its order and visit, the four objects in the Makefile, the count and every number in `netstub.S`), and, for the PSP's Wi-Fi window (it replaced a first version of `netlink.c` and `ftpscreen.c`, whose 125 and 369 changes were checked too and are no longer of interest):
    `netlink.c` 114, `ftpscreen.c` 295 and, after the "not running" screen, 312 more, `wifi.c` 76, and 42 + 35 written by hand (what `window()` does with each answer, ✕ Start, the guard for the buttons of the window, the two hints, `wifi_connect()` step by step, `netlink_attach()` and `netlink_state()`, the call in `main.c`).
    Every one that was not caught first was either caught by a new test (the list above is that work: for `ftp.c` that answers end with CR LF, a device is never made, removed, renamed or written, FEAT and QUIT work before the login, commands in small letters that start with a, a Telnet sign 0xF0 is skipped and 0xEF is not, the words of an empty line and of ABOR, a listing is not shown as a transfer, a computer that does not read its answers is kept and loses nothing, a read or a send that fails ends the session,
    the 150 goes out before the first byte of the file, a step moves up to four chunks (both ways), the data port is closed once the computer is connected, the minute of a stalled transfer counts from the data connection, an upload that moves a little is not cut, a reset data connection ends an upload with 426, a file that cannot be read (451), written in full (552) or opened (550), a RNFR that fails forgets the one before, three computers waiting are all accepted in one step;
    for `netlink.c` that stopping before a start does nothing, the numbers given to `sceNetInit` and `sceNetApctlInit`, all four parts of an address up to 255, the limits to the millisecond, the minute counted from the first question after attaching, a loss is told once, attaching again forgets the old address; for `wifi.c` that the data of the window has nothing but what is set (the reserved words, the result, the ad hoc data are 0),
    the background gets the time frame by frame, the window is asked exactly 120 times to end after HOME, the numbers of `sceNetApctlInit`; for `ftpscreen.c` that a connection from the window does not close the screen, that a failure of the window stays on the screen to be read, that ✕ Start opens the window again in "not running" and after a failure and in no other phase, that the ✕ and ○ held when the window closes do not count (also not a ✕ while the ○ is still held) and that a new screen has no guard, and the two hints to the pixel; for the wiring that the checks of `main_check.py` look only at `ftp_mode()`, not at `usb_mode()` which has much the same lines) or is
    one that changes nothing, listed here:
    - `ftp.c` (45): *answers*: the guard `room < 8` and the cut `n > room - 3` in `reply()` (an answer is under 300 bytes and only one waits). *Commands*: `sizeof cmd - 3` (the longest command has 4 letters), skipping the rest of a long command word (it is never found), `'z'` as the end of the small letters (no command has a z), `strlen(arg) > PATH_LEN`, `clen[0] >= 3`, `w >= n` in `real_path()` and
      `strlen(v) <= 4` in `is_device()` (the next check refuses the path anyway). *Second guards that the call backs up*: the device test and the folder test in STOR and DELE (opening a folder for writing, or removing it, fails), the `stat` before DELE, `dir_exists()` in RNFR (`sceIoGetstat` works on folders), `f != 0` in `cmd_rnto` (RNFR keeps only a path that `real_path()` took), `dir_empty()` answering 1 for a folder that cannot
      be opened (RMD fails by itself) and its `break`, `ok &&` in the year test of a listing (a time that is not known is 1980), the room test in `format_entry()` (`list_fill()` stops 400 bytes before the end, so a line fits), the first seek with `PSP_SEEK_CUR` (a new file is at 0). *Done twice*: REST is forgotten in five places (the start of LIST, RETR and STOR, `xfer_fail()`, `xfer_close()`), each is enough; so are the name, `done`
      and `total` in `xfer_close()` and `done` in `xfer_begin()`, `bpos = 0` after a read and the reset in `flush_buf()`, `last_active` in `xfer_begin()` (the connection of the computer sets it again), `s_port = 0` in `ftp_stop()` and `s_listen >= 0` in `ftp_port()`. *Limits that only move work to the next step*: `guard < 8` in `session_step()` (only after a line that gets no answer), `!s->quit` in that loop (an answer waits after QUIT,
      so the loop stops anyway), `last_active` after a command (the bytes of the command set it), `kind == X_NONE` in the idle test (a moving transfer sets `last_active`, a stopped one ends after 60 s), the early close and the second `flush_out()` in `session_step()`, the early return after a partial send, after "nothing came" and `continue` after `list_fill()` in `xfer_step()` (the loop is cut by `STEPS` either way),
      `drop_input()` copying `in_len` bytes (the extra ones are never used), `s_listen < 0` in `ftp_step()` (accept on -1 fails by itself), `flush_buf()` with an empty buffer (sending 0 bytes).
    - `netlink.c` (14): `-2` instead of `-1` as "not started" (6 places; all tests are `>= 0`); `s_ip[17]`; `s_connecting`, `s_fresh` and `s_up` as 2 (only tested for true or false); the start values of `SceNetApctlInfo` and of the state (the library writes them, or the call fails and they are not read); `s_ip[1]` instead of `s_ip[0]` in `netlink_ip_value()` (a one-letter address is no address);
      `s_up = 1` in `netlink_stop()` (the next `netlink_attach()` sets it to 0).
    - `wifi.c` (3): `-2` instead of `-1` for "not started" of the net, the Inet library and the access point control in `wifi_dialog()`.
    - `netsock.c` (3): `bits[9]` for the set (one more word that is never read), `-2` for a number that does not fit (callers only look at `< 0`), `ready(s, 2)` (any non-zero is "write").
    - `ftpscreen.c` (18): `s_switch_wait = 1` in `ftpscreen_open()` and no `s_switch_wait = 0` in `window()` (it is set to 0 where the phase is entered or left in `ftpscreen_update()`); `s_release = 2` (only true or false); `still = 2` (only true or false); bigger arrays in `hints()` and for the two items (`bw[3]`, `iw[3]`, `both[3]`: only `n` items are used);
      `hints()` taking the width of the first symbol for both (✕ and ○ are equally wide); the sizes of `line`, `num` and `num2` (127 to 129 and 31 to 33: no text is as long); `f < 0 ? 1` (a share is never below 0), `f <= 0 ? 0` and `f >= 1 ? 1` (the same value), `bw * f <= 4 ? 4` (the same at 4). The text under a message (a second, smaller line that had to be wrapped)
      was code that nothing used any more after the list was gone: 17 changes of it survived, so it was removed.
    The full private run after all this: ALL PASSED (68 groups); the ARK-5 and the ARK-4 build without a warning.

- **Recently played** (*the Library's sorting of this name is gone: see* Keep in Home, six ways to sort ... *above; `lastplayed.c` stays and is used by the Games tab, Information and Games and Apps*) (new `lastplayed.c` / `lastplayed.h`; `ui.c` / `ui.h`, `playtime.c` / `playtime.h`, `home.c`, `main.c`, `config.c` / `config.h`, `lang.c` / `lang.h`, `Makefile`).
  - *The times* (`lastplayed.c`): `<data dir>/lastplayed.txt`, one line per game: a packed time, a space, the game's key (`entry_cover_key()`, as in `playtime.txt`); at most 4096 games; read like `playtime.txt` (CRLF, damaged lines,
    numbers over 10 digits or over 4294967295 or 0 skipped, a last line without its end ignored, the same name twice keeps the later time). A time is the packed local time of `scan_pack_time()` (year since 1980 in six bits: 1980 to 2043;
    `lastplayed_pack()` gives 0 outside that, `lastplayed_now()` is the PSP's RTC through it), the same kind as `Entry.file_time`, so the time a game was played and the time it was added compare directly. `lastplayed_note(key, time)` keeps only
    a later time and writes the file at once when something changed (a time of 0 or a name of 80 bytes or more is ignored and takes no place); `lastplayed_time(e)` is 0 when nothing is known (a game that was played and then copied again keeps its time: the key is the game's name, not the file). *The disc* has a time too, by its disc ID (see *The disc* below).
    `lastplayed_sort_time()` of the first version is gone: nothing uses it since the Library follows Home (below).
  - *Who writes them*: `main()` calls `lastplayed_load(DATA_DIR)` before `playtime_load(DATA_DIR)`, and `lastplayed_note_launch(&chosen)` right after `home_note_launch(&chosen)`. `playtime.c` `merge_played()` reads the `sce_st_mtime` of each file of the helper plugin
    (the plugin writes it again every few seconds while a game runs, so it is when the game was last played; before 1980 / not readable: now) and, only once `playtime.txt` is saved and the files removed, notes it and puts the game in `s_plays`, the games
    played since psp5 was last open, oldest first (an insertion by time that keeps the order the files came in for the same time; 256 at most, as the files taken at one start). `playtime_plays()`, `playtime_play_key(i)` and `playtime_plays_taken()` give the list out once.
  - *Home* (`home.c` `apply_plays(list)`, the end of `home_load`): each of those games that is in the list (not the disc) is pushed to the front of `s_recent` with `push_front` (the oldest first, so the one played last is in front), `home.txt` is written when
    something changed, and the list is taken. Not before the list has a game: an empty list means the card could not be read (the plays wait for the next `home_load`). Pinned cards stay pinned. *Unplayed games on Home*: see *The Games tab is always full* below (Home is
    filled with the games that were never played now, so nothing is left out any more).
  - *The Library* (`ui.c`): `Ui.lib_sort` is `LIB_SORT_NAME`, `LIB_SORT_RECENT` or `LIB_SORT_PLAYED` (`ui.h`; the names have `LIB_` because `saves.c` has `SORT_NAME` of its own, and Manage Saved Data is not changed); `psp5.cfg` has `libsort=0` to `2` (anything else is by name).
    `rebuild_library()` makes the key of every game once (`played_key()`, a `u64`) and the key of every folder (the biggest of its games') when the sorting is by played, sorts with `cmp_played` / `cmp_folder_played` (a bigger key first; the same
    key by name; the disc first) and frees them. The key is explained under *Recently played follows Home* below. The Sort popup has three choices (`MI_SORT_PLAYED`, `S_SORT_PLAYED`: "Recently played" and its ten translations), the button says *Sort by: Recently played*. A folder that is open sorts its games the same way.
  - *Not changed on purpose*: the played times are not read from `playtime.txt` (a game can have a time played and no last time, and the other way round).
  - *Tests* (private, not in git): new `units/lastplayed_test.c` (68 checks: the packed time and the years that fit, the file and its damaged lines, 4096 games, only later times, the disc id, psp5 starting a game, the plugin's files with their modification times,
    unset clock, files that leave no time, a failed save, 256 at a start, the order of the plays), `home/home_test.c` (the plays put in front, once, with a pinned card, a game that is gone, the disc, an unreadable card, forty remembered), `ui/library_test.c`, `ui/menu_test.c` (three choices) and
    the loops over the 11 languages for the Sort button, `units/config_test.c` (`libsort` 0 to 2), `units/caps_test.c` (the sort names), source checks that `main.c` loads before the plugin's files are added and notes a launch. The first version of this was mutation-checked about 80 times
    (all caught but three that change nothing: the folder-name tie-break of `cmp_folder_played`, because the host's `qsort` keeps equal elements in order; a literal that is 36 bits wide instead of 32, whose numbers become 0 anyway; the disc's time in `cmp_played`, because the disc was put first before the
    times were looked at; the last one is not equivalent any more, see below). Holes found then and closed: a time of 0 that took a place in the table (in the file and noted), the disc's id found in the table, the order of the same time, a name left in the list after it was taken, and the two arrays of times
    that were not freed (an AddressSanitizer count of the bytes in use over 60 sortings).

- **Recently played follows Home** (`ui.c` `played_key()`, `home.c` / `home.h` `home_recent_rank()`; `lastplayed.c` / `lastplayed.h`).
  - *Why the Library and the Games tab differed.* The Games tab is `home_build()`: the pinned cards, then `s_recent`, the list of the cards opened last (newest first; at that time filled once, on the first start, with the newest files; a game that is started or added goes in front, a game taken off Home leaves it, forty at most).
    The Library sorted by one time per game: the time it was played, else its file time. So (1) a game on Home that was played before `lastplayed.txt` existed has no time and went by its file time, wherever Home had it; (2) a game that was only added (never played, not on Home)
    took its place between the played games by its file date, while the Games tab does not show it at all; (3) a game taken off Home, or added to it by hand, was ordered by its time in the Library but not on Home.
  - *The key now.* `played_key(e)` (it is `home_order_key(e)` of `home.c` since *The Games tab is always full*, below) has the group in its top 32 bits and the place inside the group in the low 32: group 3 = the game is in Home's list, the place is `0xFFFFFFFF - rank` (`home_recent_rank()`: 0 = newest, -1 = not in the list, always for the disc);
    group 2 = it is not in the list but `lastplayed_time()` is known, the place is that time; group 1 = never played, the place is `Entry.file_time`. A bigger key comes first, so the Library shows Home's list in Home's order, then the other played games, then the games that were never played, the newest file first.
    Pins are not looked at (a pinned card stays where Home's list has it): only the Games tab puts them in front. A folder goes by the biggest key of its games. The Library is built again when Home changes (`home_changed()` -> `relist()` -> `build_views()`), so Add / Remove / Pin
    on the Games tab change it at once.
  - *What it means.* A game that was played but then taken off Home goes after the games on Home (Home is the list the maintainer sees), and a game that fell off the end of the forty is sorted by its time among the others. A game with no time that is on Home keeps its place from Home.
- **The Games tab is always full** (*pins, hidden cards and Remove / Add / Pin are gone: see* Keep in Home *above; the rule of the order is the same*) (`home.c` / `home.h`: `home_build()`, `home_order_key()`, `home_load()`, `home_remove()`, `open_key()`; `ui.c` `played_key()`; later, on request). Home used to be the pinned cards and `s_recent` (the games opened lately); the first start copied the newest files into `s_recent` once (`seed()`), and a game that was added later was not on Home until it was started, added by hand or pinned.
  - *The rule.* `home_build()` gives the disc (first, outside the ten), the pinned cards, the cards of `s_recent`, and then fills what is left of the ten places (the Library card is one of the ten, as before) with the other games: not the disc, not pinned, not in `s_recent`, not taken off. Their order is `home_order_key()`, the key of the Library's *Recently played*:
    group 3 = in `s_recent` (the place is `0xFFFFFFFF - rank`), group 2 = played before (`lastplayed_time()`), group 1 = never played (`Entry.file_time`); a bigger key first, then the title (`strcasecmp`), then the place in the list. The last tie-break makes the answer the same whatever the sort does with equal elements (the PSP's `qsort` is not stable, the PC's is; the test replaces it by one that is not).
    `ui.c` `played_key()` is `home_order_key()` now, so the two tabs cannot differ. Nothing of the filling is saved: it is worked out each time, in memory taken with `malloc` (`rest`, `okey`); with no memory for `rest` / `okey` the pinned and opened cards are still shown, and with no memory for the keys (the first `malloc`) only the disc and the Library card. `home_recent_rank()` is not changed (-1 for a game that only fills Home) and `home_state()` says `HOME_SHOWN` for these games too (it asks `home_build()`).
    A pin or an opened card always goes before the fill, so the Library's *Recently played* and the Games tab have the same games in the same order, except that the pinned cards are first on the Games tab only (the random test checks that the games that are not pinned are a prefix of the Library's order).
  - *Taking a game off.* A game that fills the rest would come back at once after *Remove from Home*, so `home_remove()` also writes the key to `s_hidden` (new: `HOME_HIDDEN_MAX` 100, newest first, `hide <key>` lines in `home.txt`; when it is full the oldest is forgotten and that game may fill the rest again). `open_key()` is the one place that makes a game the newest opened card: it takes the key off `s_hidden` and puts it in front of `s_recent`
    (starting a game, *Add to Home*, pinning a game that was not in `s_recent`, and a game played from the XMB in `apply_plays()`). `prune()` drops the hidden keys of games that are gone (not when the list has only the disc: the card could not be read). A hand-edited file with `recent X` and `hide X`: the game is shown (opened wins). The first start writes nothing (`seed()` is gone); an old `home.txt` is read as before, and games that were taken off with an older version fill the rest again (nothing wrote that down).
  - *Tests* (private, not in git): `home/home_test.c` 203 checks (the first start, filling, the games taken off and the hundred, the file and its damaged lines, a key of one letter, the disc and the key `A`, the places that damaged lines must not use, the item after the end of the list, short answers, ties, memory and `malloc` that fails, `home_order_key()`, 30000 random operations that also check that every free place is filled, that no game taken off is shown and that the games that are not pinned are a prefix of the Library's order; the home test is linked with `--wrap=malloc,free,qsort` to count the memory, make the Nth `malloc` fail, fill new memory with `A` and sort equal elements the wrong way round), `ui/menu_test.c` 964 (the filling on the cards, *Remove from Home* on a card that only fills, the first start, Home empty by `hide` lines) and `ui/library_test.c` 1144 (the first start gives -1 places, the order of both tabs).
  - *Mutation checks* (private, `mutation-batches/mut19a.py` to `mut19e.py`): 300 changes of `home.c` (269 made by the generator, 31 by hand: the fill, the order key and the tie-breaks, the hidden list and its hundred places, `open_key()`, `prune()`, the lines of the file, the memory) and 7 of `ui.c` `played_key()` (every group, the place, the direction, the old time key); the last run
    of the 85 that were not caught the first time left 40 that change nothing, the other 45 were caught after tests were added. *Holes found and closed:* memory that was not given back (`malloc` / `free` are counted); a `malloc` that fails (the answer is still the disc, the pinned and opened cards, the Library card); equal elements put in the wrong order by the sort (the tie by place in the list);
    a key of one letter and a disc whose key was not set (new memory is filled with `A`, the folder `A` is a game); an empty key in a `pin`, `recent` or `hide` line taking one of the places; a game pinned twice in the file; the Library card after nine pinned games; a line that only starts like `pin ` / `recent ` / `hide `; the first game of the list (index 0) and a pinned card
    at place 0 that are gone; an item after the end of the list taken for a game or the disc; an answer with room for 0 or 1 cards, also when the memory fails; the memory asked for when the ten places are full (the fill is skipped); the game taken off Home that is played from the XMB, started, added or pinned. *Not caught, nothing changes (40):* the size of `s_text` (a few bytes more or less, and reading the whole buffer when the file fills it), the mode of the new file (the PC's file calls ignore it) and the
    flag of the read open, a file handle of 0 (a handle is never 0), `load_file()` returning 1 or 2 for a file that is not there (the lists are empty, `prune()` has nothing to drop; the same for the `else` branch that empties `s_recent`), the end of the text for a file of 1 byte or a read error (the line ends of the old text are NULs already, nothing is read from it), `end[-1]` for an empty first line, `s_npinned < PIN_SLOTS` against `<=`
    (nine games and the Library card are all that `PIN_KEEP` lets in), truthy values (`2` for `1`), the size of the arrays for an empty list (`malloc(0)` or two items), the size of the comparison result (only its sign counts) and `>=` where the keys are different, `playtime_play_key(n)` one past the end (it gives `""`), `room >= 0` in the pinned loop and `s_npinned > PIN_SLOTS` in `home_pin_library()` (the pins never reach the room, and ten pins include the Library card),
    a failing `malloc` of only one of `rest` / `okey` (`m` stays 0 either way), the arrays of `home_state()` one card bigger, `find_key(s_recent) < 1` in `home_pin()` for the card at place 0 (it is put in front again where it is), and a disc key that starts with the byte 1 (no file name does). Full private run after it: 68 groups, ALL PASSED; both ARK builds (`make`, `make ARK=4`) without a warning.
- **Information shows when a game was played last** (`info.c`, `lang.c` / `lang.h`; `info.c` now includes `lastplayed.h`, and `lastplayed.o` was already in the `Makefile`). `time_row()` is the old `modified_row()` made general; `last_played_row()` puts `L(S_I_LAST_PLAYED)` ("Last played") right after *Modified* in `iso_rows()` and `pbp_rows()`, and right after *Location* in `umd_rows()` (see *The disc* below). No row when `lastplayed_time(e)` is 0.
  The text is new in all 11 languages (`S_I_LAST_PLAYED`: Senast spelad, Zuletzt gespielt, Dernier lancement, Última vez jugado, Ultimo avvio, Laatst gespeeld, Jogado pela última vez, Последний запуск, 最終プレイ日時, Naposledy hráno); the strings are 209 now.
- **The power popup on every screen** (new `power.c` / `power.h`; `main.c`, `ui.c`, `settings.c` / `settings.h`, `info.c`, `saves.c` / `saves.h`, `files.c` / `files.h`, `Makefile`; later, on request). SELECT used to open the popup only in Games, Library and Music (`MENU_POWER` in `ui.c`).
  - *`power.c`*: `power_open(in)` / `power_update(in, dt)` (↑ ↓ and the stick choose, ✕ gives `SA_XMB`, `SA_SUSPEND`, `SA_SHUTDOWN`, `SA_RESTART` or `SA_RECOVERY` and closes at once, ○ START SELECT close; a direction that is held when it opens does not move the choice; the sounds are the ones of `ui.c`),
    `power_draw()` (dims the screen and draws the panel, nothing when it is closed and faded), `power_reset()`, and `power_frame(in, dt, can_open)`: what a screen's loop does every frame, before its own update: SELECT opens the popup unless `can_open` is 0; while it is open, and for the press that opens it and the press that closes it, `*in` is cleared, so the screen never sees the button
    that closed the popup (○ would go back, ✕ would do the row). `power_popup_draw(sel, a)` is the panel itself (200 x 150 with the five choices, in the middle, rising into place like the questions): **`ui.c` draws its own `MENU_POWER` with it too**, so there is one drawing code; its state (`menu_open`, `menu_sel`) stays in `ui.c` because it shares the menu machinery with the options popups.
  - *`main.c`*: `power_step(&in, dt, can_open)` = `power_frame()` and `sound_wait(400)` when something was chosen. Settings (`can_open` = `!settings_modal()`: a choice's popup), Information (always), Manage Saved Data (`!saves_modal()`: a delete running, the question, the Sort popup) and the File Manager (`!files_modal()`: a job running, the options popup or the question) ask it before their update; the USB screen too (always).
    A choice of the three that leave psp5 (`run_action()` of Settings, Information and Manage Saved Data: those screens are closed first with `info_close()` / `saves_close()`; Settings has its own rows for the same actions) works like the same rows in Settings; Sleep (`sys_suspend()`) comes back to the screen. The File Manager and the USB screen run with everything else stopped, so they return the choice (`files_mode()` / `usb_mode()` return an `SA_*`, 0 = none):
    the File Manager does Sleep on the spot, as with the PSP's own switch; USB is stopped first (`sys_usb_stop()`) and then `after_visit(action)` does Sleep or `leave_for(action)` (new, the four ways out that were the end of `run_action()`). `power_reset()` is called before each screen opens (a popup that was still fading out is not carried to the next screen).
    Each screen calls `power_draw()` right before `osd_draw()`. Not changed: the PSP's own Wi-Fi screen (`wifi_dialog`, the system's), the start-up screen and the intro, the delete / copy progress panels (a job is running: `*_modal()`).
  - *Tests* (private): new group `power` (`power/power_test.c`: closed draws nothing; it opens on Sleep (the second choice), rises into the middle (200 x 150 at 140, 61), five choices 28 px apart, the frame, the dim; ↑ ↓ and the stick stop at the ends, a held direction runs no further; ✕ gives each of the five actions and closes; ○ START SELECT close; the other buttons do nothing; the sounds; `power_frame()` with can_open 0 and 1, the buttons that are kept from the screen, the press that closes it kept too,
    fading out, `power_reset()`; the five names in all 11 languages; at rest on whole pixels; and `power/main_check.py`, which reads `main.c` and checks that every screen asks `power_step()` the right way, that a choice is done the right way and that every screen is reset before it opens); `ui/menu_test.c` prints the signature of its own popup and `run_all.sh` requires it to be the same line as `power_test.c`'s (`SIG-POWER`: panel, dim, shadow, colours, frame, text);
    `settings/settings_test.c`, `files/popup_test.c` and `saves/saves_test.c` check `settings_modal()`, `files_modal()` and `saves_modal()` (a popup, a question, a running job).
    The Settings, Information, Manage Saved Data and File Manager tests also open the popup (`power_open()`) and draw the screen, and check that the dark panel is on the screen when it is open and not when it is closed or reset (a screen that forgot `power_draw()` would show nothing); `menu_test.c` checks that the card screens' popup rises and fades in.
  - *Mutation checks* (`mutation-batches/mutbatch14a.sh` to `14e.sh`, 70 changes): all caught. In `power.c`: the choice it opens on, a held direction when it opens, the clamps at both ends, the order of the actions and of the names, ✕ not closing it, no sound, each of ○ START SELECT left out and one button too many, the sounds, a held direction running on, no fading in or out, a closed popup choosing, no dim, the width, the rise, the text and frame positions, the opacity of the others, the font size,
